rename: the ring length is not a public fact, but `frames` has shipped
under that name since the HUD and a rename is a break for nothing) and
`pumps`, the number of `pump()` calls the window has taken. `pumps` is
a `u64` on the runner incremented where `PumpRunner::pump` runs its
turn, and `frames_total` a `u64` beside the ring's `push`. The doc on
`frames` says it saturates at 120. Pin in `test.mjs` beside the F57
pacer test: a stopped app with a once-a-second tick takes fewer pumps
in its second second than in its first 100 ms after a click. Then the
pomodoro's bench can be an assertion rather than a `top` reading.

### `.` F63 — What `env.audio.live` reads for a play that waited on an open the device then refused — **done (2026-09-15)**

**Done as written:** the sentence on the `live` row in `index.d.ts`,
`kui.h`, `schema::ENV_FIELDS` (so `props.md`) and `AudioEnv::live`;
`a_device_that_failed_to_open_refuses_a_play` asserts `env().live == 1`
while the play waits on `Device::Opening` and `0` with `device ==
Failed` after the apply that refuses it. Nothing changed in behaviour.

**Symptom** (pomodoro, wish 2): the smoke test's assertion on the
click's frame — `live=1` while `device=opening` — guards on `device
=== 'failed'` because "this machine cannot show the answer". A
sentence on the row — counted until the refusal, or never — would let
the guard be an assertion of its own.

**Checked, and the answer is *counted until the refusal*.** `Audio::env`
(`crates/kui-native/src/audio.rs`) reads `live` as `playing.len() +
pending.len()`, and a play that arrives while `Device::Opening` goes
to `pending`, so it counts from the frame it was asked. When the open
answers with an error, `opening()` sets `Device::Failed`, the next
`apply` flushes `pending` through `manager()`, which is `None` for a
failed device, and every flushed play lands in `refused` — the core
hears `{kind:"sound", phase:"refused"}` (AR20) and `live` reads 0 on
the apply that flushed it (`flush_pending`). So the sequence a machine with no output device shows is
`device=opening live=1` → `device=failed live=0` with a refusal event
between, and the pomodoro's guard can assert exactly that. The
`a_device_that_failed_to_open_refuses_a_play` test already pins the
refusal half; nothing pins `live` across it.

**Do:** the sentence, on the `live` row of `AudioEnv` in `index.d.ts`
(and wherever the C and Lua docs repeat it): "a play waiting on the open counts here
until the open answers; if the device refuses, the play is refused on
the next apply and leaves the count with it". One assertion in the
existing test: `env().live` is 1 while the play waits and 0 after the
apply that refuses it. Nothing changes in behaviour.

### `.` F64 — `settled()` never resolves while a keyframe cycle runs, and nothing separates a transition owed from a cycle running — **done (2026-09-15)**

**Done as written, named `owed`:** `AnimStore` keeps `owes_transition`
(set by `drive`) and `owes_cycle` (set by `sample`) where it kept
`active`; `Core::owed()` is `Owed { transition, cycle, depart,
requested, autoscroll }` with `any()` (what `animating()` answers) and
`beyond_cycles()`. Doors per binding: Node `owed()` on `Ctx` and
`KuiWindow`, `quiet(maxMs)` on `WindowLoop` (a waiter with its own
predicate beside `settled`'s), C `kui_owed` as `KUI_OWED_*` bits
(pinned in `abi_consts!`, called in `surface.c` — where the check found
the card's 120 ms entrance owed beside the cycle at 50 ms, which is the
point: two bits), the doors table row. Tests: the core's
`what_is_owed_is_readable_by_kind`, Node's `quiet()` over a fake
surface (resolves once only the cycle is left where `settled(100)` hits
its cap; waits on a requested frame), and `ctx.owed()` on the looping
`runOut` fixture reading `{cycle: true}` alone.

**Symptom** (pomodoro, wish 3): the unpinned window never settles
because a `repeat="alternate"` keyframe cycle never ends, so the smoke
test waits on `frame()` and cannot use the wait that means "nothing
owed". A `settled({ ignoreKeyframes: true })`, or a reading that
separates a transition owed from a cycle running, would let the first
window assert what the second one does.

**Checked.** `animating()` (`crates/kui-core/src/runtime.rs`) is one
bool over five sources — the anim store's `active`, `depart`, a
requested frame, `tree.any_animate`, autoscroll — and the anim store
sets `active` for a keyframed slot on every frame ("a keyframed slot
always owes a frame", `keyframes_cycle_in_every_direction` in
`anim.rs`). A looping keyframe is, to the driver, indistinguishable
from a transition mid-flight, which is right for the driver — it must
schedule the frame either way — and useless for a test that wants to
know whether the *transitions* have run out. `settled` in `index.js`
resolves on `!animating()` and nothing else, so under a cycle it
resolves only at `maxMs`, with `animating()` still true, which is what
`runOut` returning its cap says headless.

**Do:** not an option on `settled` — a wait that ignores something is
a wait that lies — but a second reading beside `animating()`: what is
owed, by kind. `AnimStore` knows at `sample` time whether the
transition it stamped is a `repeat` cycle or a finite tween; keep two
flags where it keeps `active` — `owes_transition` and `owes_cycle` —
and expose `Core::animating_why()` (name open) as `{ transition,
cycle, depart, requested, autoscroll }`, with `animating()` unchanged
as their `||`. Then `settled` gains no option and `WindowLoop` gains
`quiet(maxMs)`: resolves when nothing but a cycle is owed. The
pomodoro's first window asserts `quiet()` where its second asserts
`settled()`. Small: two bits in the anim store, one door, one wait written like
`settled`.

### `.` F65 — A headless press's default `physical` is `code` verbatim, and a window's is lower-case — **done (2026-09-15)**

**Done, one level lower than written:** the default lives in
`KeyPress::new`, which every headless door shares — Node's `key_press`,
C's `kui_input_key_down` with a NULL `physical`, Lua, `testing::key_down`,
the corpus's `KeyDown` step — and the window driver bypasses with
`with_physical`, so the fold went there: an ASCII upper-case `code`
yields its lower-case as `physical`, everything else its own code. The
`keyDown` doc says so in Node and C; the corpus fixtures did not move
(their steps are lower-case letters). Pinned in `test.mjs` beside the
layout test: `press("Z", { shift: true })` reaches the sink as `code:
"Z", physical: "z"`, a spelled `"Z"` is delivered as spelled, `$` and
`left` are their own. Listed under *What breaks* per F61.

**Symptom** (mind map, "one note from the log, not a finding"): under
headless, `press('Z', { shift: true })` reaches the sink with
`physical: "Z"`, where the window reported `physical: "z"` beside
`code: "Z"` for the same ⇧Z (the alpha.12 menu trace). `KeyMsg.physical`'s
doc (F55) says "as the lower-case one here"; `keyDown`'s says "omit it
and it equals `code`". Both are true, and they disagree.

**Checked.** The window's `physical` comes from `physical_code`
(`crates/kui-native/src/keys.rs`), a table from winit's `PhysicalKey` to the
US-QWERTY character at that position — letters are their lower-case
character regardless of shift, because the table never sees the
modifier. The headless `keyDown` / `press` doors take `physical` as an
optional fourth argument and default it to `code`, so a shifted letter
spelled as the OS spells it (`Z`, per F60) gets a `physical` no window
ever reports. Nothing in this tree binds `physical` from a headless
test, which is why it took a log to notice; the first app that binds
WASD by position and drives it headless with shift held will match in
the window and miss in the test, or the other way round.

**Do:** the default folds: a one-character ASCII letter `code` yields
its lower-case as `physical` when the argument is omitted, in the
Node door (`index.js`) and any other headless door that shares the
default. Not a re-spelling of what a test passes — F60's rule
stands, a spelled `physical` is delivered as spelled — only the
default, which was already a guess. The `keyDown` doc says "omit it
and it is the position's US letter: the lower-case one for a letter,
`code` for everything else". Pin in `test.mjs`: `press("Z", { shift:
true })` headless reaches the sink with `physical: "z"`, the same
pair the window reports.

## From the kawoosh terminal field report (2026-09-15)

The first thing kawoosh showed on alpha.13 that a user could see:
lazygit in its terminal pane, every `│` of the panel frames a dashed
line. One entry, the one defect of the day's three rounds, **built
2026-09-15** and moved here with the alpha.14 tag. The report's
introduction stays in `BACKLOG.md`.

### `!` F66 — A `cells` node draws box-drawing and block glyphs from the font, and no font's are the cell's height — **done (2026-09-15)**

**Done, as written, all five shape groups:** `crates/kui-core/src/cells/boxdraw.rs`
behind `shape_cell` — `boxdraw::draws(ch)` decides, `boxdraw::raster(ch,
w, h)` is the cell-sized mask, and the atlas gained a third map,
`get_or_insert_synth` keyed on `(char, cell_w, cell_h)`, cleared with the
glyphs on a reset. The light stroke is `max(1, round(cell_w / 8))`, the
heavy `3×` (same parity, so both centre on the same pixel), every stroke
on `(cell − stroke) / 2` from the cell size alone. Two things the entry
did not decide: the shades are flat alpha (64 / 128 / 192), since a 2 × 2
dither's phase shifts at every odd-width cell boundary; and a dash
pattern splits its gap at the cell's edges by construction (`left =
gap / 2`, the rest to the right edge), not by rounding, so the seam
between two cells is one gap wide. The double-line rules, since 41
glyphs share them: a rail ends at the *near* rail of a double on its own
side (an inner corner), at the *far* rail of a double on the other side
when nothing continues past the centre (an outer corner), and at the
centre otherwise (a line running through); a single arm runs to the
centre when its opposite continues it, to the near rail between two
doubles (a tee), to the far rail of one (a corner). Tests: the six the
entry asked for in `tests/cells.rs`, reading the atlas back; the tables
and the shape rules in the module; the `cells` example's session ends in
a box-drawn bench table with block bars, captured seamless at 2×.
CHANGELOG under both *Fixed* and *What breaks*. The `text` wish stays a
wish: a mono `text` row's line box is not a cell contract.

**Symptom** (kawoosh, 2026-09-15, macOS, lazygit 0.65.1 in a `cells`
node): kawoosh's terminal style is `size 13`, `line_height 20`, in the
bundled Iosevka Navcon. Every `│` (U+2502) renders as a dash with a gap
below it, every row; `▐` (U+2590, lazygit's scrollbar thumb) stacks as
separate black dashes rather than one bar. Any box-drawn frame in any
TUI shows it.

**Why, measured.** Iosevka's box-drawing (U+2500–U+257F) and
block-element (U+2580–U+259F) glyphs span exactly the font's own line
box: the report read the file — hhea 965/−215, typo 965/−285, and the
glyph bounds of U+2502, U+2503, U+2588, U+2590 and U+2551 all
y ∈ [−285, 965] on 1000 upm, advance 500 — so 1.25 em tall, 16.25 px at
13 px. `cells` makes the cell `line_height × scale` tall
(`StyleTable::cell_h`, `crates/kui-core/src/cells.rs`), 20 px, and
`shape_cell` places the glyph where cosmic-text's line puts it; 3.75 px
of each row is empty under the stroke. No font can know the cell is
20 px: a font fills *its* line box, and any face shows some gap unless
its glyphs happen to be 1.54 em tall. A face without these codepoints
is worse — cosmic-text falls back to another family and the strokes
change weight and width mid-frame. This is why Alacritty
(`builtin_box_drawing`), kitty, WezTerm, foot and Ghostty all bypass
the font for these ranges and draw them from the cell box. kui owns
the cell box, so kui draws them.

**Where it goes.** `shape_cell` is the one door: `lookup` caches on
`(ch, variant)` in front of it and every cell of the grid comes through
it once per table. Before shaping, a `ch` in a synthesized range is
rasterized procedurally into an alpha mask of exactly `cell_w × cell_h`
physical pixels (both on the `StyleTable`) and returned as a `CellGlyph`
at `(0, 0)` of the cell with `w = cell_w`, `h = cell_h`, `GlyphMask`
kind. The atlas needs a key that is not a font glyph's: `get_or_insert`
takes a cosmic-text `CacheKey` and `get_or_insert_image` marks its slot
`color_glyph: true` (so the image path would not tint by the cell's
foreground) — a third map keyed on `(ch, cell_w, cell_h)` beside the
two, or a synthesized `CacheKey` with a reserved font id, whichever
`atlas.rs` finds cleaner; the point is that one cell size and character
share one slot and a different cell size does not. `text` nodes are
untouched: a mono `text` row showing a box-drawn table has the same gap,
but there the line box is not a cell contract — leave it, as a wish
beside this entry.

**Shapes, in order of value.** (1) Light and heavy lines, corners, tees
and crosses, U+2500–U+254B: light stroke `max(1, round(cell_w / 8))`
px, heavy 2–3× that, both snapped to whole physical pixels and centred
so adjacent cells' strokes meet with no seam — the centreline is
computed once per cell size from `cell_w`/`cell_h`, not per glyph, so
every character in a row lands on the same pixel column and every one
in a column on the same pixel row. (2) Rounded corners, U+256D–U+2570
(lazygit's): a quarter arc of the light stroke, radius
`min(cell_w, cell_h) / 2`, joined to straight stubs at the edges. (3)
Block elements, U+2580–U+259F: exact rectangles covering fractions of
the cell, edges snapped so `▀` over `▄` fills the cell and `▐` stacked
is one bar; the shades `░▒▓` as ordered dither at 25/50/75 % (or flat
alpha; pick one and say which). (4) Dashed and double lines and
diagonals, U+254C–U+254F, U+2550–U+256C, U+2571–U+2573: the double
gap ≈ the light stroke, a diagonal one anti-aliased line corner to
corner. (5) Optional, same mechanism: Powerline PUA U+E0B0–U+E0B3,
which prompts render in every terminal. `flags::BOLD` does not thicken
a light line (the set has heavy variants), italic is ignored, and
`Raster::subpixel` is irrelevant — a plain mask, which the atlas
already blends.

**Tests**, in `crates/kui-core/tests/cells.rs`: two rows of `│` at
`size 13`, `line_height 20` — the rendered coverage column is
continuous across the row boundary, no zero-alpha scanline between
them; the same for `▐`, `█`, and `─` across two columns. `┌─┐` /
`│ │` / `└─┘` at 2× scale: the corner strokes meet the edge strokes on
the same pixel columns and rows. `line_height 16` then `20` produce
different atlas slots for the same `ch`, and the same size twice one.
A character outside the set (`a`, `é`, an emoji) still goes through
`shape_one`, so the fast path is a fast path and not a regression. The
`terminal` accessibility value and the `cell` click payload are
unchanged — the change is drawing only.

**Do:** the door, the first three shape groups, and the tests; (4) and
(5) as the same change or the next. CHANGELOG under alpha.14 as a
fix, and — since what a `│` draws changes for every `cells` node —
under *What breaks* too, per F61's rule.

## From a feature ask (2026-09-15): a drop zone

"You declare a drop zone on a box and you get events once it happens."
ADR 0031 is the design; the one entry it produced was **built
2026-09-15** and moved here with the alpha.14 tag. W19 — the position
Windows and Linux cannot report — stays open in `BACKLOG.md` with the
round's introduction.

### `~` C40 — A drop zone: `onDrop` on any node, the files as an event, `dropBg` while they hover — **done (2026-09-15)**

**Outcome.** Built whole the day the ADR was accepted, as
[`docs/adr/0031-a-drop-zone-is-a-row-and-the-files-are-an-event.md`](adr/0031-a-drop-zone-is-a-row-and-the-files-are-an-event.md)
decides: `onDrop` / `on_drop` / `KuiSpec.on_drop` on any node, four
phases of one `drop` event (`enter`, `move`, `leave`, `drop`, with
`paths` and the point), `dropBg` in the same pick as `hoverBg` and
winning over it, the zone resolved as the topmost *zone* under the
point with a node that is no zone looked past (the HTML `dragleave`
flicker cannot happen), three `InputEvent`s with doors in Node and C
and a `drop_target` reader in all four bindings, the `drop` corpus
scene in four adapters, ABI 18, the `drop` example in Rust and Node.
On macOS the runner overrides winit's window delegate for the five
`NSDraggingDestination` selectors (`crates/kui-native/src/macos_drop.rs`) and
answers the OS from a stamp; verified by dragging a file from the Finder
onto the example — the zone lit, `enter`/`move`/`leave`/`drop` in the
devtools log with the content-relative point, the green badge over the
zone and none off it, a release off every zone refused and the icon
slid home, the files leaving the window taking the zone's `leave`.


## From the alpha.14 pre-tag round (2026-09-15)

The round that tagged alpha.14. Of its two entries, C41 — a bench row
the guard did not watch, 10% slower since the drop-zone commit — was
**built 2026-09-25** and moved here; E4, the harness's, stays open in
`BACKLOG.md` with the round's introduction.

### `.` C41 — `frame_1k_curves` is 10% slower since the drop-zone commit, and no guarded row moved — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** A register spill, not a drop-zone path. Profiled at
`5e6711e` against its parent — the merge `f623663`, not `db2ff82` —
with Instruments' Time Profiler, leaf addresses mapped to the binary:
every +25 µs of the frame is inside `emit_node` (87 → 112 µs a frame),
and inside it all of it is one loop, `push_segments` inlined. At the
parent the loop keeps the carried point in a register; at the drop
commit it stores both vector values to the stack at the bottom of the
loop and reloads them at the top (`stp q4, q0, [sp, #0xf0]` / `ldp q0,
q4, [sp, #0xf0]`) and rematerializes the zero vector inside it — a
store-to-load round trip on the dependency chain of every segment, three
cycles or so × 35,000 segments, the whole regression. The commit never
touched the loop: `emit_node` grew by 540 bytes of code (the `DropOwner`
build, a second region's field, `hover_tracked` pushed out of line)
and the allocator gave the loop's registers away. Two suspects the entry
named measured innocent: `#[inline]` on `hover_tracked` and stubbing the
`DropOwner` build out whole each left +8–9%. Built: `push_segments` is
`#[inline(never)]`, with the reason beside it — on its own the loop
keeps every value in a register (disassembled: the only stack stores
are the grow path's, before the loop). `frame_1k_curves` reads 275 →
253 µs at HEAD, under the parent's 261, and the drop commit with the
one attribute 250 against 259. The row joins the guard list in
`scripts/bench-check.sh`.

**Found.** The alpha.14 bench guard read every guarded row within its
noise and three unguarded rows past ±5%; re-run alone, two were noise
and `frame_1k_curves` was not — 255 → 280 µs, +10.0% at ±1.4% run to
run. Four probes with `scripts/bench-check.sh <commit> frame_1k_curves`
(HEAD against each base, one row, ~4 min a probe) put it in one commit:
`cc070bd` (F62–F65) and `db2ff82` (the cursor) read 260 µs against
HEAD's 280–284; `5e6711e` (ADR 0031, the drop zone) reads 286 and
`3179ceb` after it 284 — HEAD is that commit's number. Nothing in ADR
0032 or the devtools chord moved it. The row is a thousand `polyline`
floats through eight knots each, flattened to 35 segments a curve every
frame; it declares no interaction, so the `HitRegion` that grew an
`Option<DropOwner>` (a `Value` inside) and the per-region `enclosing_drop`
walk in `emit` should not be on its path — and `frame_10k_segments`
(+3.6% at ±1.3%), `frame_1k_polygons` (+4.0% at ±1.5%) and
`frame_1k_closed_lines` (+3.1%) lean the same way while
`frame_10k_rects` reads −3.4%, so it is the side-list emitters, not
every node. Twenty-five nanoseconds a curve is one call that stopped
inlining or one struct that crossed a cache line, which is the shape
C15's remainder and the architecture review's two inlining
regressions had.

**Do.** Profile `frame_1k_curves` at `5e6711e` against `db2ff82`
(`cargo bench -p kui-core --bench frame -- frame_1k_curves` under
`sample`, or `perf`-style counters on Linux); the suspects in that
commit's per-frame path are `emit.rs`'s region closure (now building a
`DropOwner` under `any_drop && interactive`, which grew the closure and
may have un-inlined the segment emitter around it), `Interaction`'s new
`DropHover` field, and `Tree::any_drop`. If it is inlining, an
`#[inline]` or a split of the region build out of the emit loop; if it
is the region struct, box the `DropOwner`. Add `frame_1k_curves` to the
guard list if the fix lands, so the segment path has a row that fails.

## From the kawoosh binary-file report (2026-09-16)

kawoosh opened a `.ttf` — a binary, so a "line" is tens of KB between
the rare newlines — and its framerate HUD read 17.9 ms a frame average,
229 ms at the worst, in release, drawing each of its ~34 visible rows as
one `rich_text` of the whole line. The report's first pass had escaped
the control characters and blamed "thousands of spans, looks quadratic"
for the rest; the measurement that filed these two (the table is in
`BACKLOG.md`, with the round's introduction) showed the span count a
factor of 1.5 and the whole-line shaping the cause. Both entries were
**built 2026-09-16**, the day they were filed.

### `~` C42 — `rich_text` never takes the long-line path: a 50 KB line with one styled span is shaped whole, and a screenful of them thrashes the cache — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Outcome.** Built as the "Do" says, the day it was filed. `LongLine`
holds the spans as byte ranges with their attributes (`OwnedSpan`,
`SpanAttrs`) beside the content, `add_rich` / `measure_rich` go through
`intern_rich_any` — the rich twin of the plain door, one hash and two
lookups before the newline scan (C43's shape) — and `build_long` builds
either kind, each chunk keyed by `rich_key` over `chunk_spans` (the
spans that intersect it, sliced, the empty dropped) and shaped by
`shape_chunk` as an ordinary rich `CachedText`, so its decorations and
span metadata are chunk-local by construction and `long_highlight` /
`long_hit` / `long_caret` / `relayout_long` needed nothing. The one
thing that did: `emit_entry_rows` drew glyphs only (a plain chunk has
no decoration), so a wrapped rich chunk's rects are now cut at the row
breaks by x — row `r` covers `rows[r].x..rows[r + 1].x` of the
unwrapped run — one piece a row, shifted as the row's glyphs are.
Measured on the entry's own cases: the 50 KB one-span line 331 → 20 ms
cold, 0.13 → 0.024 ms steady; thirty-four such rows 13.5 s → 0.66 s
cold, 9.4 / 56 ms → 0.40 ms steady, the cache at 34 MB; a caret span
moving one character a frame along a 100k line 1.29 ms — the same as a
plain keystroke's, once `long_line_100k_edit` was made honest (it
alternated two letters and read a text-cache hit from the third frame,
~160 µs; inserting fresh text it reads ~1.3 ms, the chunk reshaped with
cosmic's word cache warm). Two bench rows, four tests, mutation-checked
(the rich door forced to the whole path fails three; the row
decorations dropped fails the fourth). Not built, as the entry said: a
whole-path entry's template walk. What it gives up is what C19 gave up,
listed under alpha.15's *What breaks*: the access value without runs,
the estimate for unshaped chunks, a kern pair at a cut.


**Found.** `is_long` (`text.rs`) is asked by `intern` and `measure`,
never by `intern_rich` (`text.rs:1444`), which hands the spans to
`set_rich_text` as one buffer whatever their length — `docs/props.md`
says so ("a plain text … is shaped in ~1 KB chunks"), and the C19
outcome shipped it that way on purpose, plain first. The cost is the
table above: 331 ms the frame a 50 KB single-span line scrolls in
(kawoosh's 229 ms max, at its narrower window), then every template
kept — 50k glyphs × `ENTRY_GLYPH_BYTES` (480) is 24 MB a row, so three
rows meet `DEFAULT_TEXT_CACHE_BYTES` (64 MB) and the fourth evicts the
first; at 34 rows every frame reshapes what the last one evicted (the
56 ms steady reading). And an editor's caret row changes its spans on
every caret move, which is a new key and the whole line again. Emission
is the other half: a whole entry walks every template to find the
clip's (`emit_entry`, `text.rs:1978`), 1.7 M checks a frame for that
screenful, which is the 9.4 ms before the thrash sets in.

**Do.** Chunk a rich paragraph the way C19 chunks a plain one: past
`LONG_LINE_BYTES` with no line break, cut the concatenated content by
`chunk_ranges`, and give each chunk the spans that intersect it, sliced
to it — its key is its content and *its* spans' attributes (the
`intern_rich` mix over the slice), so a caret span moving along the
line re-keys one chunk and the rest hit. `SpanDeco` and the `metadata`
span index become chunk-local; `long_highlight`, `long_hit`,
`long_caret` and the wrapped rows (`emit_long_rows`) already work per
chunk and need only the rich chunk to be an ordinary `CachedText`,
which it is. The access value is the concatenation, as a plain long
line's is. A span cut by a chunk boundary loses what C19 said a cut
loses — one kern pair per KB, or a ligature at that exact byte — and
inherits the same estimate for the unshaped width. Pin it in
`benches/long_line.rs`: a 100k line in a few hundred spans, first
frame, scroll, a caret span moved one glyph a frame — each a chunk's
cost. Not in scope: a whole-path entry's template walk (a short line
stays what it is).

### `.` C43 — A long line's cache key is a byte-serial FNV of its whole content, every frame — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Outcome.** The first of the two lines, built the day it was filed:
`key::hash_bulk` — Fx's rotate-xor-multiply round eight bytes at a
time, the length mixed first, murmur's finalizer after — and
`key::mix_content`, which sends content past 32 bytes through it and
mixes the eight bytes it returns by `fnv`, shorter content by the byte
as before; `style_key` and the rich key use it, `key::fnv` and every
digest on it are untouched. And the newline scan moved off the hit
path: `intern_any` (and `intern_rich_any`) look a could-be-long text up
under both keys first and scan only when the cache has not seen it, by
byte (`has_line_break`), where `str::contains` with a char array was
per character. Thirty-four chunked rows of 500k, steady: 26.8 → 11.2 ms
with the hash, 8.1 ms with the byte scan, **3.0 ms** with the scan off
the hit path (`long_rows_34x500k_steady`). The second line — a `key` the
app supplies on the style — is **not built**: the hash is the fix, and
the door waits for a host the hash is still too slow for.


**Found.** With every chunk shaped and nothing changing, 34 plain rows
cost 0.45 ms at 5 KB each, 2.7 ms at 50 KB and 26.8 ms at 500 KB — 1
ns a byte, linear in content the frame draws none of. It is
`style_key` (`text.rs:1037`): `crate::key::fnv` a byte at a time with
a multiply on the chain, over the whole content, so a long line's
lookup costs its length, and a Node or Lua host pays it after copying
the string across the boundary. The chunked path made shaping cost the
screenful; the key still costs the file.

**Do.** Either is a line. A wider hash — eight bytes a round (FxHash's
word step, or wyhash-style) reads at 0.1–0.2 ns a byte, five to ten
times this, no API — or a key the app supplies, since an editor knows
its buffer's revision and the line's index and would rather hand over
`(rev, line)` than have the content re-read: a `key` on the text style
(the row a text does *not* take today, per `props.md`) that replaces
the content in `style_key` when set, the app owning the promise that
one key means one content. The first is the fix; the second is the
door a host with megabyte lines will ask for. Pin with a row in
`benches/long_line.rs`: 34 × 500 KB rows steady, under 3 ms.

## From the alpha.15 pre-tag round (2026-09-16)

The round that tagged alpha.15. Its one find came from the gesture the
round owed the entries it was tagging: kawoosh, on this tree, opening
the kind of file that filed C42.

### `!` C44 — A long line with a multibyte character on a chunk edge panics in `chunk_ranges` — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** kawoosh (`kui` by path, this tree), `:e` on a 2.6 MB `.ttf`,
release: `panicked at crates/kui-core/src/text.rs:667: end byte index
2942 is not a char boundary; it is inside '�'` — the first frame, before
the window drew. `chunk_ranges` cut each window at `start +
CHUNK_BYTES` and sliced `&content[start..window_end]` there to look for
whitespace, so a multibyte character straddling byte 1024 (or 2048, …)
of a long line was a slice inside a `char`, which is a panic. A lossy
decode of a binary is U+FFFD three bytes at a time, and 1024 % 3 = 1,
so every such line hit it; but any long line with a multibyte
character at the wrong offset did — a log line with an arrow in it,
prose past 4 KB with a curly quote — plain or rich, since the chunked
path shipped in alpha.9. The whitespace search that follows the slice
already worked in `char_indices`, and the grapheme fallback in
`grapheme_indices`; it was the window itself that was cut by byte.

**Outcome.** The window's end is floored to a char boundary before the
slice (`is_char_boundary`, stepping back at most three bytes), so the
straddling character belongs to the window it starts in and every chunk
edge is a boundary — which `chunk_spans` (C42) relied on as well, since
it slices `content[a..b]` at chunk edges clamped against span edges.
One test, `a_multibyte_character_on_a_chunk_edge_is_not_a_panic`: 4000
U+FFFD, plain and then rich, drawn and queried — it panics on the line
before the fix. kawoosh's HUD on the fixed tree, the same 2.6 MB font,
sixty caret moves over the rows: **2.68 ms a frame average, 36.6 ms
at the worst**, against the 17.9 / 229 ms of the report that filed C42.

## From the kawoosh syntax-tab report (2026-09-16)

kawoosh built a Syntax tab into the panel the day after ADR 0032 shipped —
its tree-sitter tree as the host form, caret-following, folds, a node
count in the header — and the first command it wanted to write against
it could not be written.

### `~` F67 — No door selects a devtools tab from the app: a command that jumps to the app's own tab has only the strip's click and `Ctrl+Shift+N` — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** kawoosh, the session that built its Syntax tab: "kui has no
`Core` door to *select* a declared tab by name (only the strip click /
Ctrl+Shift+N), so a `:syntax_tree` command that jumps straight to the
tab would need a small kui addition." ADR 0032 gave a tab every door
*in* — `devtools_selected` / `hovered` / `picked` to read the panel,
`set_devtools_selected` and `set_devtools_pick` to drive it — and none
that *shows* the tab. The panel's `State::custom` (the declared tab on
show, by name) and `State::show(Tab)` (one of its own) were exactly the
two writes the strip's click makes, reachable only from the panel's own
`act`. An app that turns the panel on for its tab (`set_devtools(true)`,
the dock from `set_devtools_dock`) still landed on whichever tab the
user last left, three chords from its own.

**Outcome.** Two doors, in the core and the three bindings that have the
sibling doors (Lua stays a guest, as for every devtools verb):
`Core::set_devtools_tab(name) -> bool` / `setDevtoolsTab` /
`kui_set_devtools_tab` shows the tab named — one of the panel's own by
its name (`facts`, `events`, `tree`) or a declared tab's — as the strip's
click would; a declared name the panel does not list yet is kept and
shows once a frame declares it (so the command works before the first
frame, and the return says whether the panel lists it now — a declared
tab is listed from the panel's first frame on); a hidden dock comes back
`Right`, as the picker's does; `on` is not touched, that stays
`set_devtools`'s. `Core::devtools_current_tab() -> String` /
`devtoolsCurrentTab` / `kui_devtools_current_tab` reads the selection
back by the same names — unlike `devtools_shown_tab`, which is the
encoder's reading of a declared tab on show in the main window and
answers nothing for the panel's own. `DOORS` rows for both, so the verb
table pins them in each binding; a `DevtoolsTab` type in `index.d.ts`;
the ABI stays 18 (two functions, no struct). Tests: the core's
`a_tab_is_selected_from_the_app` (declared, own, undeclared with the
fallback, the chord moving on from it, the hidden dock, before any
frame, with the panel off), the C walk and the ffi readback, the Node
`devtoolsTab` test; both `devtools_tab` examples gained the page's own
"open the Inspector" button and print which tab the panel is on, and
their drives check it.


## From the kawoosh idle-frame report (2026-09-16)

kawoosh measured what its window did with nothing happening — the
devtools off, a plain buffer, normal mode — and it drew a frame twice a
second: sixteen in eight idle seconds, exactly the blink clock's half
period.

### `.` F68 — A modal editor's block caret arms the blink clock: an idle app in normal mode draws a frame twice a second — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** kawoosh (alpha.15), a `log` line in `view` and eight seconds
of nothing: 16 frames. The runner's `about_to_wait` arms the blink clock
while `Core::has_caret()` holds, and it held: kawoosh declares `caret`
on the primary selection's line in every mode — for the access tree and
the IME anchor, which is what the row is documented for — and in normal
mode draws a block over it that never reads `caret_visible`. So the
clock toggled a phase nothing read, and every toggle asked for a frame.
The contract left no way out: the howto says "keep the `caret` row on
the line either way, because that row is what arms the clock", and
`has_caret` read the row's presence and nothing else. Dropping the row
in normal mode (tried, in kawoosh) stopped the frames, but with them
went the access tree's caret — a screen reader in normal mode no longer
heard where the cursor was — and macOS's `isEditable`. A caret that
does not blink is a caret; that a declared caret blinks was an
assumption from the stock editor, where every caret is a bar.

**Outcome.** `caretSolid` (Lua `caret_solid`, C `KUI_VALUE_CARET_SOLID`
in `value_set` beside `KUI_VALUE_CARET`, Rust `NodeSpec::caret_solid`)
on a `line` declaring `caret`: the caret is solid. The row still anchors
the IME (`ime_rect`) and is still the access tree's caret; `has_caret`
leaves it out, so no clock is armed, the phase stays `true` and the view
draws its block every frame it is asked for — which, idle, is none.
Going from bar to block is not a move (`caret_stamp` holds); coming back
the clock re-arms solid from `has_caret` alone. A flag bit in a word the
struct already has: the ABI stays 18. Node gains `hasCaret()` for the
same reason C had `kui_has_caret`: a test reads it to see that an idle
view asks for no frame. Tests: the core's
`a_solid_caret_anchors_and_reads_but_does_not_blink`, the ffi caret test
extended, the Lua `a_lua_editors_solid_caret_arms_no_clock`, the Node
`caretSolid` test, the sampler scene declaring it in all four adapters.
`modal_editor` now keeps its normal-mode block solid and declares it so,
and its drive checks that normal mode arms no clock while insert mode
still blinks. kawoosh on this tree, the same eight idle seconds: 0
frames. *(Amended 2026-09-20 under RG14 (j): a solid caret's phase
stays `true` in a window without the keyboard too, where a blinking
one is hidden — by design. The runner's clock owns the phase only of a
caret it blinks; hiding the bar unfocused is the clock's business
because the bar is the clock's, and a solid caret is the view's, which
knows what its block should look like without the keyboard — hollow,
as a GUI editor's goes, dimmed, or gone — from `env.focused`, the
reading every view has. Making the runner park a solid phase off would
have chosen "gone" for every app and left the IME anchor and the access
caret declared over nothing drawn. `modal_editor` draws its block
hollow unfocused and its drive checks the phase on and the outline
drawn; the runner's comment says so.)*

## From the kawoosh second-Mac report (2026-09-16)

The first time kawoosh ran on a Mac other than the one it was built on.

### `!` F69 — A held key does not repeat on a Mac with press-and-hold on: the accent picker, or nothing, where a modal editor wanted a motion — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** kawoosh (alpha.15, kui by path), a second Mac: holding `j`
in normal mode moved one line and stopped; holding `e` opened the
accent picker over the buffer. macOS's press-and-hold
(`ApplePressAndHoldEnabled`): a held letter with accents offers them, one
without does nothing, and neither repeats — on by default, and off on
the first machine because its owner had run `defaults write -g
ApplePressAndHoldEnabled -bool false` long ago, which is why the editor
had never met it. AppKit reads the default through `NSUserDefaults` at
each key press (GLFW's `registerDefaults` has relied on that for years);
winit does nothing about it; kui had no door. A modal editor cannot
live with it — `jjjj` is how one moves — and a stock editor should not
lose it — the picker is how a Mac user writes `é`. So it is the app's
call, and there was no way to make it.

**Outcome.** `Launcher::press_and_hold(bool)` in Rust, `pressAndHold`
in Node's `WindowOptions`, `kui_press_and_hold(bool)` in C — a free
function called before `kui_run`, since it is a process setting and a
`KuiRunConfig` append would have been ABI 19 for one bool — and the free
`kui::press_and_hold` behind all three for a host running its own
window. Applied in `Launcher::shell`, before any key press: the value
goes into the **argument domain** (`NSArgumentDomain`), merged over the
process's real `-key value` arguments — searched before the app's and
the global domain, volatile, so nothing is written to the user's
preferences and a global setting the user made is left as it was; it is
what `-ApplePressAndHoldEnabled NO` on the command line would have set.
A no-op on every other platform, where a held key repeats already. Left
out, the user's own setting stands. Tests: the runner's
`press_and_hold_is_pinned_over_the_users_default_and_read_back` (both
directions, over whatever the machine says, the domain's other entries
kept); Node's `windowOptions` carries it and a non-boolean is refused;
the C counter says `kui_press_and_hold(false)` before `kui_run`, so the
walk calls it; a DOORS row, a howto entry. What is checked here is the
pin — on a machine whose global default is *off*, pinning `true` reads
back `true` through `NSUserDefaults`'s own lookup and `false` reads back
`false`, so the argument domain wins over the global one either way;
the key repeat itself is for the Mac that found it, where kawoosh now
says `false`.

**Checked in a window (RG15, 2026-09-20) — the pin does not hold.** The
premise above — "AppKit reads the default through `NSUserDefaults` at
each key press" — is wrong on macOS 27.0. The read that decides is
HIToolbox's, caught with `CFPreferencesCopyValue` interposed into a bare
AppKit probe: `TSMProcessRawKeyEvent` → `AreTSMAndEventOKForPressAndHold`
→ `TBGetAppBooleanPref_Internal` → `CFPreferencesCopyValue(
"ApplePressAndHoldEnabled", kCFPreferencesAnyApplication,
kCFPreferencesCurrentUser, kCFPreferencesAnyHost)` — the *global*
domain by name, through the one CFPreferences call that consults no
volatile domain; beside the key in HIToolbox sit five bundle ids that
get repeats regardless (`org.vim.MacVim`, `se.bzero.Vico`,
`org.gnu.Aquamacs`, `org.gnu.Emacs`, `com.apple.Terminal`). In the
same process, `NSUserDefaults` *and* `CFPreferencesCopyAppValue` answer
the pinned value — the argument domain is in both searches — which is
why the test here passed and proved nothing about AppKit. Measured, on
a machine whose global default is the string `"0"` (off): with the
global written on, an `NSTextInputClient` view driven with 53
autorepeat `keyDown`s (the WindowServer's shape, `kCGKeyboardEventAutorepeat`
set; a synthetic hold does not repeat by itself, through CGEvent at the
HID tap or `IOHIDPostEvent` alike) got every one at `keyDown:` and
`interpretKeyEvents` inserted the letter **once** — swallowed by
press-and-hold — pinned or not; with the global off, 54 inserts. Nor did
GLFW's `registerDefaults` reach it, nor the app's own domain: written at
launch (`CFPreferencesSetAppValue` + synchronize, RG15's named fallback)
or before it (`defaults write <app> …`), bare or in a bundle with a
`CFBundleIdentifier` — one insert every time. Only the global domain
moved the outcome. kui's window cannot tell the two apart under
synthetic repeats: winit forwards a repeat `keyDown` that produced no IME
input as a `KeyboardInput` with `repeat: true`, so `modal_editor`
(pinned `false` now, through a `press_and_hold` on the harness's
`Window`) moved 54 lines with the global on, and the same 54 without
the pin — the count the entry asked for needs a physical key, since on
a real hold the repeats never reach `keyDown:` at all (kawoosh's one
motion). Not done with the keyboard: the check was CGEvent-driven, and
the real-hold half rests on kawoosh's report and the read being one
function. The door stays, inert and saying so in every doc and in its
test (which now also reads the global domain the way HIToolbox does and
sees the pin not land); the two mechanisms that remain — writing the
user's global domain for the app's lifetime, or repeating in the app —
are RG16's decision. Global default put back as found (`-string 0`,
verified off again). **RG16 (2026-09-20): the user chose removal** —
`Launcher::press_and_hold`, `pressAndHold`, `kui_press_and_hold` and
`kui::press_and_hold` are gone, with the harness's `Window` row, the
DOORS row, the changelog entry and the C counter's call; the howto
entry says it is the user's setting and what to write.

## From the kawoosh mid-frame-dock report (2026-09-16)

Found while looking at something else — the idle-frame report's
follow-up — and made permanent by it.

### `!` F70 — A panel turned on mid-frame is built into a root not laid out for it: the dock in the bottom-left corner until the next frame — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** kawoosh, `:kui_debugger` (and `:syntax_tree`, F67's door):
the panel opened as a box in the window's bottom-left corner, 340 wide
and half the window's height, the app drawn full width beside and under
it, with the *right* dock's button lit. A frame later it was a right
dock. Before F68 that frame came within half a second from the blink
clock; with the idle loop quiet it did not come at all, and the box
stayed. Headless, the same frames laid out right — the difference was
*when* the panel was turned on: kawoosh calls `set_devtools(true)` from
its `view`, after `begin_frame` had run with the panel off and left the
root a plain column with no app container. `finish` read the panel as
on and built the dock into that column as the root's second child —
`Grow(1)` tall like the app above it, `side_w` wide, at `x = 0`: exactly
the box. `set_devtools_dock` mid-frame had the same shape of trouble: a
bottom dock built into a row root.

**Outcome.** `begin_frame` remembers the dock it wrapped the root for
(`dt_dock`, `None` when it did not); `finish` builds a docked panel only
when that is the dock it is building for, and otherwise — turned on, or
moved, since the frame began — requests a frame and builds nothing, so
the next frame lays the root out for it and nothing wrong is drawn in
between. Undocked (a window of its own) there is nothing to lay out
around, and the holder is built as before. One test,
`a_panel_turned_on_mid_frame_is_built_from_the_next_frame`: the frame
that turns it on builds no panel and is owed a frame, the next has the
dock at the right at full height; then the dock moved mid-frame, the
same way, to the bottom. Checked in kawoosh's window: `:syntax_tree`
opens on the right dock in the first frame after the key. kawoosh's own
half — its `devtools` flag forced into the core every frame, which also
overrode `KUI_DEVTOOLS` at launch and would have undone the panel's own
close button — went two-way on its side.

## From the kawoosh devtools-strip report (2026-09-16)

kawoosh with five tabs — its own Syntax and Perf after the panel's
three — in a right dock, and a screenshot: `Synta / x` and `Event / s`
broken across two lines, the three toggles at the strip's end, and on
the Tree tab a third of the panel empty under the inspector. The
report asked for a strip that scrolls or wraps, and for the toggles to
move into the Facts tab — then, seeing them there as a row of icons,
for each to sit on the fact it changes, "maybe as a select, which
probably should be built". F71 is what the empty third was; F72 the
strip, the selects and the widget.

### `!` F71 — A grow child's `min` or `max` keeps its share of the run: the room a clamp gives back is a hole, not its siblings' — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** The Tree tab's node list and inspector are both `Grow(1)`
in the panel's column, the inspector with `max_height(300)`. In a tall
dock each got half; the inspector was clamped to 300 and the other
half's remainder went nowhere — a hole the height of what the clamp
gave back, under the inspector. `distribute_run` computed every grow
child's share once, set it clamped, and moved on: the clamp could push
a child *past* its share (the total accounted for that) but the room a
clamp left short of it was never re-shared. Flexbox resolves this with
a freeze loop; kui's run did not.

**Outcome.** `distribute_run` loops: each pass shares the remainder
among the unfrozen grow children, and a child whose clamped size is
not its share is frozen at the clamp with its size moved into `used`;
the loop ends on a pass that freezes nothing, at most one pass per
grow child. A `min` that holds a child past its share takes from its
siblings the same way. One layout test,
`a_grow_childs_clamp_is_its_siblings_room`: a 600 column with a
capped-at-100 child gives the other 500; a 300 row with a `min` 200
and a `max` 20 child leaves the third 80, in one layout, in place. The
Tree tab's list now runs to the inspector. A view that padded the hole
with a spacer of its own can take it out (the changelog says so under
Fixed, since the result moves).

### `~` F72 — The devtools strip breaks tab labels, and the theme, accent and menu toggles are icons away from the facts they change — **done (2026-09-16)**

**Found.** The strip was a plain `Grow(1)` row: five tabs wider than a
340 dock made the text nodes wrap inside their tabs, so a tab read as
two lines of a broken word, where ADR 0032 had written that the strip
"wraps as the tab row already does" — it never had. At the row's end
sat the base, accent and menus icons (ADR 0024, decision 16), each a
click that cycled to the next choice, with the choice itself readable
only from the tooltip or the Facts tab three rows down. Moved into
Facts as an `app` row of three icons, they were still a row of glyphs
apart from `theme`, `accent` and `menus`, the very facts they change,
and cycling is the wrong verb for a choice of three or six.

**Outcome.** Three parts.

*The strip.* `.wrap()` with a 2 px `cross_gap`, and every label
`nowrap()`: a tab is one unit and the row breaks between tabs. The
toggles are gone from it, so it has the whole width to wrap in.

*The widget.* `widgets::select(ui, label, &options, current)` — a
field showing the choice in force, `sunken` with a hairline, the
accent border while open or focused, a small triangle for the
disclosure — that, clicked, opens the core's own menu of the options
under its bottom-left corner with the current one checked. The menu is
the one a right-click opens (`Core::open_menu`): drawn in the frame by
`build_menu`, or the platform's where the host shows menus itself;
dismissed by Escape or a press outside; its rows a `menu` composite for
the arrows and a reader. So the app holds no open state, and hears the
choice as the `menu` event a menu row posts, on the field's key:
`{kind: "menu", role: "custom", item: <the option>}`. The plumbing: the
field's click carries a `select` tag and the widget declares its rows
into the core's per-frame `selects`; `consume_select_events`, first in
`handle_input`'s outbound chain, takes the click back and opens the
menu targeted at the field, at the rect the last frame laid it out at.
`select_items` takes `MenuItem`s (a choice posts the item's `id`);
`select_with` takes the spec and the text style; `select_spec` is the
stock one. Access: a button named by the field, described by its
choice (`value` is a slider's and an editor's), `expanded` while open.
Tests in `tests/select.rs`: the click opens under the field and
reaches the app as nothing, a row is one event on the field naming the
option and the field then describes itself by it, Escape closes it
silently, a field the view stopped drawing opens nothing.
`examples/rust/widgets/select.rs` drives both forms headless. Rust
only: F73 files the other three bindings.

*The panel.* The Facts rows `theme`, `accent` and `menus` each end in
a compact `select_with` of the choices — `app (dark)` / `light` /
`dark`; `app` and the five named accents; `platform` / `native` /
`drawn` — pushed to the row's right edge past the value. The fact is
what the app has, the select what the panel holds it to. Each choice
is an action of its own (`base:light`, `accent:2`, `menus:drawn`),
beside the cycling ones the chords keep. The devtools consumer reads a
menu row's `item.dt` as it reads a click's `dt`. The five icons and the
two icon helpers the toggles used are gone from `icons.rs` and
`panel.rs`; the placement buttons and the picker keep theirs.

Found on the way: `activate_menu_item` — a host's own menu answering —
returned its events without the pass `handle_input` gives every batch
on the way out, so a devtools select chosen from a macOS menu posted
its action to the app instead of the panel. `Core::outbound` is that
pass now, and both doors leave through it (the second devtools test,
`a_select_chosen_from_a_native_menu_is_the_panels_action_too`, pins
it). Two more from the first look in kawoosh's window: the *drawn*
menu of a select in the dock was pushed left into the app — a float of
the core's menu took the host viewport as every host float does, and
under a right dock that is the window less the dock; the menu's nodes
(`OriginId::MENU`) now float against the window, as the panel's own
do, and `widgets::context_menu` adds `dt_shift` to the host's point
for it. And `platform` in the menus select put nothing back: the
override's `None` left the core in the mode set last; `dt_menus`
remembers the host's own (menus and bar) when the override goes on
and `restore_menus` puts it back when it is lifted or the panel goes
off — `the_platform_menu_choice_restores_the_hosts_own_mode`.
Checked in kawoosh's window: five tabs on one line at the default
dock width, the selects on their rows, the native menu under the
field with the check on the current choice, the choice applied; and
`Ctrl+Shift+T` still cycles. One thing seen and not chased, since
neither this change nor the chord path made it: in kawoosh's window the
base override changes the state — the fact reads `light · pinned`, the
select `light`, and a probe in kawoosh's `view` sees `ui.theme()`
light — but the pixels stay dark, panel included, while the `counter`
example repaints light under the same chord and kawoosh's headless
harness resolves a light palette. Somewhere between kawoosh's tree and
the window; noted for the next kawoosh round.

### `.` F73 — `select` is a Rust widget only: no `<select>`, no Lua `select {}`, no `kui_select` — **done (2026-09-17)**

**Found.** F72 built `widgets::select` / `select_items` / `select_with`
for the devtools' Facts rows and for a Rust app. A Node, Lua or C view
that wanted the same field built a button and answered its click with
`openMenu` — which works, and is the one thing the widget saves an app
from writing. The stock field, the text input and the button each have
their three forms, and the select should have the same, lowering to the
one Rust widget so no binding grows a select of its own.

**Outcome.** Asked for the next day and built. The element is
`select` in `schema::ELEMENTS` — own props `label`, `options`,
`current`; rows `Some(&[])`, since its look is its own and it has none
of the box's to read (`diag::unknown_prop` now says "takes none of
them" for an empty row list, instead of "takes only ,"). The options
are read by one door, `MenuItem::options_from_value`: a list of strings
(an option by its label, posting it) or the item maps `openMenu` takes
(posting `id`, `enabled = false` for a dead row, a separator a
separator).

*Node.* `<select label options current/>`: op 22, frame v15 (a new op,
so the bump is for an encoder that emits one to an addon without it);
the encoder refuses a label absent with no `key` to stand in for it
(`label ?? key`, as `<input>` reads it — the type has both optional; the
"refuses a missing label" this said until RG14 was the no-key case
only) or missing options, an empty-string option, a negative or
fractional `current`, and writes label, the options as one
JSON blob, the index plus one; the addon reads it through
`select_options_of` into `widgets::select_items`. `IntrinsicElements`
gains the row; `current` counts from 0. A test drives the loop through
`access('language', 'click')` and `activateMenuItem`, and the four
refusals.

*Lua.* `dropdown { label =, options = {…}, current = }` — `dropdown`,
since `select` is Lua's own and a prelude that shadowed it would break
every script using it; `current` counts from 1 as a Lua list does, and
0 is refused by name. `element_of("dropdown")` is `select`, so the
allow-list and the warnings are the element's. A test drives the same
loop and the two refusals; `every_node_type_lowers` declares one.

*C.* `kui_select(ctx, label, items, count, current)`: the rows are the
`KuiMenuItem`s `kui_open_menu` takes (`to_core` shared), `current` is
an `int64_t` with `-1` for none, and it returns the node key — 0 for no
label, no rows, or a role this build does not know. The prototype in
`kui.h`, the ABI row generated as every export's is, ABI still 18; the
`schema_parity` element list and the surface walk call it, and an ffi
test drives the loop with a real click and `kui_activate_menu_item`.

*The corpus.* One scene, `select`: a field over four options — three
by label and one an item with an `id`, disabled — the second in force;
three clicks (open, choose the first row, open again) and one event,
the frame kept with the field and the menu under it, the current row
checked, the dead one dimmed. Built in all four adapters
(`build_select`, `SCENE_TREES.select`, the Lua source, `conf_select`)
and green in each against one dump; coverage is derived from the
`select` tag the field's click carries, since nothing else declares it.

*Docs and examples.* The props.md element row (regenerated), the howto
entry rewritten for four bindings, the JSX type, and
`examples/node/widgets/select.tsx` beside the Rust one, headless in the
smoke list. The C example is the surface walk's row.

## From the kawoosh session report (2026-09-17)

kawoosh saves its session — the tabs, the splits, each pane's file and
caret — on `:q`, and restores it on the next bare launch. Closed with
the window's red button, or with ⌘Q, it saved nothing: the first path
returns from `run` with the app dropped on the way out, the second ends
the process inside `applicationWillTerminate` and `run` never returns.
Neither is a key the app sees, and a `Drop` on the app would have caught
only the first. One entry, F74, **built 2026-09-17**, the day it was
filed.

### `!` F74 — An app never hears its window go: the close button drops it, ⌘Q ends the process, and neither is a key — **done (2026-09-17)**

**Found.** `App` has `setup` (once, before the window, with the waker),
`view` and `on_event`, and nothing for the end. The runner's
`CloseRequested` on the main window calls `event_loop.exit()`; winit
sends `LoopExiting` and `run_app` returns; the shell, and the app in it,
drop. On macOS a Quit — the menu item, ⌘Q, the dock — goes through
`terminate:`, whose `applicationWillTerminate` sends `LoopExiting` and
then the process exits: `run` never returns, and no `Drop` runs. An app
with something to save at the end (kawoosh's session; a draft; a
position) had one path that dropped it silently and one that did not
even do that.

**Outcome.** `App::teardown(&mut self)`, default empty: called once as
the main window goes for good, before `run` returns or the process
exits. Two doors into one `teardown_once` on the shell: the
`ApplicationHandler`'s `exiting` — the loop's last word, which both the
close button (through `exit_main`) and the OS's Quit (through
`applicationWillTerminate`) reach — and a pumped runner's `retire`,
since a pumped loop is never exited (backlog F58) and its runner ending
is the same moment; a `torn_down` flag keeps it to one. There is no `Ui`
by then and nothing draws; a crash does not reach it *(under `run` —
on the pumped path a panic unwinding through the host drops the
runner, and `impl Drop for PumpRunner` → `retire` → `teardown_once`
reaches it, a `teardown` that panics there aborting; RG1 wrote that
into the trait's doc and the changelog, RG14 here)*. `kui-devtools`'
`Harness` forwards it, so an example's own runs; the `waker` example
prints once more as it goes. Checked in the window on macOS: the
example closing itself (`WindowCommand::Close`) and the example quit
through the application menu's Quit both print the line before the
process ends. kawoosh saves its session in it. Rust only: the C and Node
hosts own their loops, and their `pump` returning false is the same
moment for them. *(Amended 2026-09-19 under RG1: that last sentence was
wrong — on a Mac a Quit ends the process inside the pump and no pump
returns, so neither host heard ⌘Q until RG1 gave them the door.)*

## From the kawoosh devtools-tables report (2026-09-17)

Every key/value list in the devtools — the panel's Facts, tokens,
legend and inspector groups, kawoosh's Perf and Settings tabs — lined
its values up behind a label box of a width picked by hand: 70, 90, 52,
110 and 180 px, six numbers, each the longest label that list had on
the day it was written and each wrong the day a longer one arrived
(`syntax rows` past 110 in the Perf tab; a settings path as long as the
user's dotted key). The report asked for a table in kui, in every
binding, and for the hand-rolled lists in both devtools to be it. One
entry, F75, **built 2026-09-17**, the day it was filed.

### `~` F75 — Every key/value list in the devtools lines its values up behind a label box of a width picked by hand — **done (2026-09-17)**

**Found.** A key/value row is a row of two texts, and left to flex each
row's value starts where its own key ends. The fix every list wrote was
a fixed-width box around the key — `NodeSpec::row().width(Fixed(70.0))`
in the Facts rows, 90 in the tokens and the legend, 52 in the
inspector's groups, 110 in kawoosh's Perf tab, 180 in its Settings tab
— which lines the values up at whatever width the author guessed. The
`measure_text` door exists for this ("sizing a column to its widest
label") and none of the six used it, because measuring every label
every frame to pick a box width is more code than the box. Nothing in
kui lined two rows' children up by column, in any binding.

**Outcome.** A table in the layout, not a widget
([ADR 0033](../adr/0033-a-table-is-a-column-whose-cells-align.md)).
`LayoutSpec.table` on a column — `NodeSpec::table()` — says its rows'
children line up in columns: the nth in-flow child of every in-flow
row is a cell of column n, and a column is as wide as its widest cell.
A cell's `width` says how its column sizes: `Fit` and `Fixed` are
content the column's fit is the max of, any `Grow` cell makes the
column grow (the largest factor), else any `Percent` makes it a percent
column, and a column's `min_w` / `max_w` are the strictest its cells
declared; a fixed column is never shrunk. Resolved inside the five
passes: `fit_widths` reaches the table after its rows and cells and
sets every column to its fit (`table_fit`), re-fitting the `Fit` rows
so the table's own fit is the aligned one; `grow_widths` reaches it
before its rows and resolves the columns once against the widest row
(`table_resolve`) — percent columns take their cut, fit and fixed
columns sit at their fit, grow columns split the rest in the same
freeze loop `distribute_run` runs (F71), and the fit columns are
compressed largest first when the fits overflow a table that does not
scroll x — then writes each column's width into its cells and sizes the
`Fit` rows to them; a row of a table skips its main-axis distribution,
its cells being final. `fit_heights` keeps the width pass 2 gave a text
whose parent is a table row, so a bare text is a cell held to its
column; a row of a table never wraps (`wrap-ignored` names the reason).
Gated on `Tree::any_table`, so a frame with no table runs as before.
`NodeInfo` carries `table`, and the Tree tab's inspector prints
`column · table`.

*The bindings.* One door each, all the `dir` one: `dir="table"` in JSX
(the encoder writes 2, the decoder reads `NodeSpec::table()`), `grid
{ }` in Lua (`table` is Lua's own; `element_of("grid")` is `table`) and
`KUI_TABLE = 2` as the third `dir` in C (`spec_of` maps it) — no new
prop id, no new op, no new struct field: ABI 18, frame v15. A `table`
row in `schema::ELEMENTS`; the corpus derives it from the flag. One
scene, `table` — a fit header row of two bare texts and three grow rows
of a bare text, a fixed box (30/50/20 wide: the column is 50) and a
grow box, in a 200 px table — built in all four adapters and green
against one dump. Tests: eighteen through `layout::compute` with the
stub measurer (`tests/table_layout.rs`: the widest cell, fit cells and
a fit table, a grow column, two grow columns with a clamp frozen, a
percent column, a fixed floor, a short row, a bare text cell, a text
wrapping inside its grown column, the shrink and its floor and a
scrolling table's overflow, a row's own padding and gap, a fit row
beside grow rows, a float in a row, a table in a cell, the warning and
its absence); Lua's `a_grid_lines_its_rows_cells_up`, Node's `<box
dir="table">` test, the ffi's `a_table_lines_its_rows_cells_up`
through `kui_layout_of`.

*The lists.* The panel's Facts rows are a table with the select in a
third column, the value column growing (the grow spacer is gone); the
tokens a table per origin (the swatches one cell, so a length's empty
one still lines the names up); the legend and each inspector group a
table; kawoosh's Perf and Settings tabs the same. The six widths are
gone.

*Found on the way.* A `Min::FIT` floor inside a node-anchored float
(a devtools tab's content, ADR 0032) was the *first* run's measurement:
the floor is written back into the spec as a number, and the sixth pass
re-ran over the number, measured before the float had a width. The
floors a frame declares are remembered in `compute` and put back before
`anchored` re-runs; pinned by
`a_fit_floor_in_a_node_anchored_float_is_measured_at_the_anchor`.

*Docs and examples.* ADR 0033; the howto entry (and the `measureText`
entry no longer recommending it for a column); the changelog's Added
with what you can delete; the props.md row regenerated;
`examples/rust/widgets/table.rs` (a file list: clickable rows, a
right-aligned size, headers that sort) and
`examples/node/widgets/table.tsx`, both headless in the smoke rosters.
Declined: a `widgets::table` over strings — every consumer that asked
needed cells that are not strings — and a column spec on the table,
until a table wants a column no cell declares.

## From the regression pass of 2026-09-19

The pass over F67–F75 filed RG1–RG15 (`BACKLOG.md` has the rest); RG1, its headline, was **built 2026-09-19**, the day it was filed; RG2 and RG12, the two on the devtools' menus select, **built 2026-09-20**, RG3, RG6, RG7, RG8 and RG11 — the five on the table's layout and the one regression among them — the same day, RG4, the left dock defeating F70's deferral, the same day too, RG5, the freeze loop's sign rule and the round's other regression, the same day again, RG9 and RG10, the select's disabled row through the door and its unchecked options and `current`, the same day as well, RG15, F69 checked in a window, the same day — the pin does not hold — RG14, the ten nits and the two devtools defects taken with them, the same day, and RG16, the decision RG15 left, the same day: the door is removed. Nothing of the pass is left in `BACKLOG.md`.

### `!` RG1 — A Node or C app still never hears ⌘Q: F74's `teardown` is a Rust `App` method, and the loops the other hosts "own" end the same way — **done (2026-09-19)**

**Outcome.** Both hosts have the door, and both were checked under ⌘Q.
Node: `teardown(model)` in `runWindowed`'s config beside `init` /
`update` / `view`, run once with the model as it stands. The addon's
`TreeApp` gained the `App::teardown` it lacked: it calls back into JS
through a `FunctionRef` the new `KuiWindow.onTeardown(cb)` door holds,
from inside the `pump()` / `pumpUntil()` that saw the window go —
those two now take the `Env` of the call in flight and hand it to the
app for the duration, since a runner retired between pumps (dropped by
the collector) has no JS frame to call from and runs nothing. The
driver registers `app.teardown()` with the window before the first
pump, and also runs it at the pump that returned false (once either
way, for a stand-in surface without the door); a throw out of it
rejects `runWindowed` — on the real path too: napi clears a pending
exception and hands it back as the call's `Err`, which the first cut
dropped on the floor (found in review), so the app keeps it and the
pump in flight returns it, the original JS value; probed with a real
window whose callback threw, the same object came out of `pump()`. `createApp` takes the same field and
`app.teardown()` runs it, so a headless drive asserts on what the app
would have kept; the Node harness forwards an example's `teardown`
both ways, as the Rust `Harness` does. C: `kui_on_teardown(fn)` before
`kui_run` / `kui_run_with`, called once with the run's `user`. The free
setter and not a `KuiRunConfig` append: `kui_run`'s app is three
arguments and not a struct, so a field would reach `kui_run_with`
alone; the config is the window, not the app; and no other [in] append
is pending to share the bump with — so ABI stays 18, as
`kui_press_and_hold` kept it. The run takes the setting, so the next
run starts with none. The C counter prints its count as it goes, which
is the surface walk's call (AR46); the verb table has the
`App::teardown` row (`kui_on_teardown` / `KuiWindow.onTeardown` /
guest), which the Node B1a pin required to be a method cell. The Rust
test F74 never got: a counting `App` in a `PumpRunner` built without a
loop (winit's is main-thread only), once across `request_exit`,
`retire`, a second `retire`, `teardown_once` and the drop — and the
drop alone. Written down in `App::teardown`'s doc, the changelog and
the howto: `impl Drop for PumpRunner` → `retire` → `teardown_once`, so
a panic unwinding through a pumped host *does* reach `teardown`, and a
`teardown` that panics there aborts; the F74 "a crash does not reach
it" holds for `run`. The changelog's "Rust only" sentence is corrected
in place and the F74 archive outcome amended below.

**Probe, re-run.** Node: a `.tsx` with `console.log` before and after
`await runWindowed(...)`, a `teardown` that logs the model and a
`process.on('exit')` hook; run, made frontmost through System Events,
`keystroke "q" using command down`. Before: the log held the line
before the call only. After: `probe: before runWindowed`, `probe:
teardown with n=7`, and nothing else — the process is gone, and as the
entry said, nothing after the call runs, the exit hook included. The
same script with the window's close button pressed through AX: the
teardown line once, then `probe: after runWindowed n=7`, then the exit
hook. C: `./target/debug/counter` quit with ⌘Q prints `teardown at
count 0` and nothing else; closed by its button it prints that line
and then the shell's `after kui_run: exit 0`. Tests: 174 Node (four
new: the callback from inside the pump before the promise resolves,
the stand-in surface without the door, a throwing teardown rejecting,
the headless `app.teardown()` once), `teardown_runs_once_across_retire_and_drop`
in kui, the ffi parity and every-entry-point-called pins.


**Found.** The F74 changelog says "Rust only: the C and Node hosts own
their loops and their `pump` returning false is the same moment." It
is not. `kui_run` is `Launcher::run` (`crates/kui-ffi/src/run.rs:141`),
kui's blocking loop over a `CApp` whose `impl App` has no `teardown`;
Node's `TreeApp` (`crates/kui-node/src/lib.rs:1463`) has none either,
and its pumped loop is never exited (F58). On ⌘Q winit's delegate has no
`applicationShouldTerminate`, so `applicationWillTerminate` runs
`Shell::exiting` — which calls `teardown_once` on an app with nothing
to run — and the process ends there. Probed on this tree: a Node
script with `console.log` after `await runWindowed(...)` and a
`process.on('exit')` hook, quit with ⌘Q through System Events — the
process is gone, the log holds only the line before the call, and
neither the line after nor the exit hook printed. A C host on
`kui_run` gets the same by reading: nothing after `kui_run` runs.

**Do.** Give the two hosts the door F74 gave Rust. Node: a `teardown`
(or `onExit`) function in `runWindowed`'s config beside `init`/
`update`/`view`, called from `TreeApp::teardown` on the JS thread with
the model, synchronously, before the process goes — the pumped path's
`retire` reaches it too; `createApp` gets the same field so a headless
drive can assert it ran. C: `kui_run_with` takes a `KuiApp` already —
a `teardown` callback is an [in] struct append (ABI 19 by AR50), or a
free `kui_on_teardown(fn, userdata)` setter before `kui_run`, as
`kui_press_and_hold` was, which keeps ABI 18; pick by whether the app
struct has other pending appends. Correct the changelog sentence and
the F74 archive outcome, and add the test F74 never got: a counting
`App` through `Launcher::open` + `request_exit`, once across `exiting`
and `retire`. Also worth writing down: `impl Drop for PumpRunner`
calls `retire` → `teardown_once`, so on the pumped path a panic
unwinding through the host *does* reach `teardown` (the changelog says
a crash does not) and a `teardown` that panics there aborts.

### `!` RG2 — A devtools select's menu outlives the panel, and its choice reaches the app as a `menu` event from the devtools origin — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** Both halves, as written. The panel's own menu — one whose
`Menu.origin` is `OriginId::DEVTOOLS`, which a Facts select's is since
`open_menu_raw` takes the origin of the node the menu is about — is
closed at `devtools_begin_frame` whenever this window is not building
the panel this frame: the panel off, its dock `Off`, or the dock
`Window` while this is the main window (the field is in the other
one). Derived from the state at the frame rather than hooked into each
door, so `set_devtools(false)`, the header's `off`, `Ctrl+Shift+D`'s
and `set_devtools_dock` all take the same path; an app's own menu is
not the panel's and stays. And `devtools_consume`'s early return on
`!on && !dt_window` now drops every event of the devtools origin
before returning, without acting on it — a control still in the tree
between the door and the frame it owes is nobody's, and the panel it
would act on is gone. The probe is the test,
`a_selects_menu_goes_with_the_panel_and_its_rows_never_reach_the_app`:
the app's door with the row clicked before the frame (no event, no
override, then no menu and no `MenuItem` row), `dock:off` and
`dock:window` after their frame (the dock going is a `resize` to the
app, drained first), and an app menu open across `set_devtools(false)`
still there. Changelog Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16.

**Found.** Open the Facts tab's `theme` select (its menu drawn), turn
the panel off — `Ctrl+Shift+D` cycles to `off`, or the app's
`set_devtools(false)` — and draw a frame: `core.menu()` is still
`Some`, the `light` row is still in the access tree, and an AX click on
it returns to the host `{kind: "menu", role: "custom", item: {dt:
"base:light"}}` with origin `65535`, the base unchanged. Probed on this
tree through `devtools/tests.rs`' harness. Mechanism:
`devtools_consume` (`runtime/devtools/mod.rs:1775`) returns early on
`!on && !dt_window`, nothing closes the menu when `on` flips, `Ui::finish`
builds any `self.menu`, and `Extensions::route` hands an event of an
origin nothing answers to the host.

**Do.** `close_menu()` when the panel goes off (and when the dock moves
to `Window`, where the field it hangs under is gone), and consume
`OriginId::DEVTOOLS` events unconditionally — the app can never mean
to hear one. A test in `devtools/tests.rs` from the probe above.

### `~` RG12 — `Ctrl+Shift+M` is a two-way toggle from a compile-time default; the select beside it has three choices — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** `act("menus")` walks the select's `Option<bool>` round —
`None` → `Some(true)` → `Some(false)` → `None`, platform → native →
drawn → platform — and nothing else changed: the state is the one
the select's `menus:*` rows write, and `devtools_begin_frame` already
applies it through `dt_menus` / `restore_menus`, so the chord's
`platform` restores the host's own mode and bar the way the select's
row does. The `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` guess is gone. A
`menus_name()` beside `base_name()` gives both the chord's and the
select's note the select's spelling (`menus: platform`, where the
select's note used to print the row's id, `default`). Test
`the_menus_chord_cycles_the_selects_three_choices_from_the_hosts_own_mode`,
beside the select's: on a native host and on a drawn one, three
presses through `handle_input(chord('M'))` land on native, drawn and
the host's own mode again (menus and bar both), a `menus:drawn` from
the select followed by one press is platform, and the stream's notes
read in that order. The module doc's `M toggles native menus` and
the changelog's `still cycle the same choices` (F72, under Changed)
now say the chord cycles the select's three in its order; the fix is
under Fixed.

**Found.** The changelog says the three chords "still cycle the same
choices". `act("menus")` (`runtime/devtools/mod.rs:588`) sets
`native_menus = Some(!current)` from `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` when
unset, never `None`: on a drawn-menu host on macOS the first press
sets `Some(false)` (nothing visible changes, the select reads `drawn`
where it read `platform`), and no number of presses returns to
`platform`. `T` and `A` do cycle through their `app` choice. It also
ignores `dt_menus`, the host mode the select remembers.

**Do.** The chord cycles `platform → native → drawn → platform` over
the same `dt_menus`/`restore_menus` the select uses; a test beside
`the_platform_menu_choice_restores_the_hosts_own_mode`.

### `!` RG4 — F70's deferral is defeated by a left dock: the panel is built at `begin_frame`, so a mid-frame dock move or panel-off draws the stale panel and asks for no frame — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** As written, and the tab with it. A left panel is built
at `begin_frame`, from the state then, and is in the tree for good
that frame; `dt_built` now remembers the tab it was built on show for
(an `Option<Shown>` where it was a `bool`), and `devtools_finish`,
before honouring it, compares `on`, the dock and `shown()` against
what `begin_frame` built from — turned off, moved, or put on another
tab since, the frame asks for the next one, as the right and bottom
docks' deferral does. The panel drawn is this frame's, as it was; the
next frame is right, and with the idle loop quiet since F68 nothing
else would have drawn it. The same tab set again mid-frame changes
nothing and owes nothing; the right and bottom docks, built at
`finish`, take the path they took.
`a_panel_turned_on_mid_frame_is_built_from_the_next_frame` gained the
left-dock legs from the probe: a settled left dock owes nothing; moved
to `Right` mid-frame the panel drawn is the left one and the frame is
owed, the next has it at the right and is quiet; `set_devtools_tab
("tree")` mid-frame owes a frame (and the tree tab's own list one
more on its first frame, as it always did), the same tab again owes
none; off mid-frame the panel is drawn once more and the frame
without it is owed. The check mutated away fails the move leg first.
Changelog Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16, after F70's.

**Found.** `devtools_begin_frame` builds a `Dock::Left` panel at once
and sets `dt_built` (`runtime/devtools/mod.rs:1466`), and
`devtools_finish` returns on `dt_built` (`:1577`) before the branch
that defers and requests (`:1588`). Probed: with a left dock,
`set_devtools_dock(Right)` from inside a frame leaves the left panel
drawn and `owed().requested == false`; `set_devtools(false)` from
inside a frame draws the panel although it is off, and requests
nothing. With the idle loop quiet since F68 each stays until the next
input. `set_devtools_tab` mid-frame with a left dock leaves the strip
stale the same way.

**Do.** In `finish`, check `on` and `dock` against what `begin_frame`
built before honouring `dt_built`: a change since the frame began
requests the next frame as the right/bottom path does (the left panel
already in the tree is this frame's; the next frame is right). Extend
`a_panel_turned_on_mid_frame_is_built_from_the_next_frame` with the
left-dock leg.

### `!` RG3 — A `scrollX` table with grow rows cannot scroll: the overflow it keeps is clipped, and `scroll_max.x` is 0 — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** As written, in `table_apply`: when the table scrolls x,
pass 2 widens every row to its columns if they overflow it —
`table_row_fit`, which is the cells at the columns' widths plus the
row's own gaps and padding — so a `grow` row of a `scrollX` table is
as wide as its content, `positions` measures the table's content from
it, and `scroll_max.x` is the overflow. A table that does not scroll
leaves its rows' boxes alone (fixed columns that overflow it overflow
the row, as fixed children overflow any row). Tests beside
`a_scrolling_table_overflows_instead`:
`a_scrolling_table_of_grow_rows_can_scroll_to_its_columns` (the probe:
80 + 40 in a 100 px table → the row 120, `scroll_max[0].x` 20; and
with `pad_xy(5, 0).gap(10)` → 140 and 40) and
`a_table_that_does_not_scroll_keeps_its_grow_rows_at_its_width`. ADR
0033 decision 4 amended; changelog Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16; the
howto and the `table` element row say a `scrollX` table's rows are at
least as wide as its columns.

**Found.** `table_resolve` skips the largest-first compression when
the table scrolls x (`layout.rs:315`) so the cells overflow the row —
but the rows the howto and both examples give are `width: grow`, so
`row.w == table content`, and `positions` sizes the table's content
from its widest *row*, not the row's cells: `scroll_max.x = 0`, and
`scroll_x` also clips. Probed: `NodeSpec::table().width(100).scroll_x()`
over a grow row of fit cells 80 + 40 → `scroll_max.x = 0`; the same
with a `Fit` row → 20. So the documented escape from compression
works only for rows nobody writes.

**Do.** A table's content width is its columns' resolved sum plus the
row chrome (the widest row's padding and gaps), written where the
