column widths are; a test beside `a_scrolling_table_overflows_instead`
asserting `scroll_max[table].x` for grow rows.

### `!` RG5 — The F71 freeze loop freezes min and max violators in the same pass, and a plain sibling can get nothing — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** As written, flexbox's rule (CSS Flexible Box §9.7,
step 6): a pass of `distribute_run` sets every unfrozen grow child to
its share clamped, marks each one the clamp moved as a `min` or a
`max` violator by the sign of the move, sums the moves, and freezes
only the violators of the dominant sign — `min` when the sum is
positive, `max` when negative — each at its clamp; the rest, the other
sign's violators included, re-share what is left in the next pass,
until a pass's moves cancel (nothing to freeze: every unfrozen child
is at its share, or the clamps add up). The probe's 600 over `max
100`, `min 500`, plain is 50 / 500 / 50; its mirror, `max 100`, `min
210`, plain — a negative sum, so the `min` violator is not frozen and
its re-share of 250 clears its floor — is 100 / 250 / 250, where the
old loop froze it at 210 and gave the plain row 290. A run with
violators of one sign only, F71's own case, lays out as it did, and
the 40-scene corpus dump is byte-identical to the old loop's. The
frozen set is a byte per in-flow child of the run — `FROZEN`, or this
pass's `MIN_VIOLATOR` / `MAX_VIOLATOR` — in `Tree::grow_scratch`, a
`Vec<u8>` sized per run and never freed, indexed by the child's place
in the run, so a pass costs its children whichever way the earlier
ones went; the `Vec<u32>` + `contains` is gone. Test
`a_pass_freezes_only_the_violators_of_the_dominant_sign` beside
`a_grow_childs_clamp_is_its_siblings_room` in `layout.rs`'s own
tests (the F71 test lives there, not in `table_layout.rs`), both
legs; it failed on the old loop at A = 100. Bench row
`frame_1k_grow_rows_capped` — a column of 1k grow rows with
`max_height` 0.5 + 0.002·i px in 1080, four passes, 348 rows frozen —
115 µs on the old loop, 80 µs now, in the README table; not on the
guard list, since the guard reads a row's own base and the row has
none before this. 1270 workspace tests over 97 suites, clippy and fmt
clean, the C adapter and 175 Node tests over the regenerated dump.
Changelog Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16 after F71's, the moving results
spelled out, marked a regression of the cycle.

**Found.** `distribute_run` (`layout.rs:1052`) freezes every clamped
child in a pass, whichever way it was clamped; flexbox freezes only
the dominant sign (min violators when the sum of violations is
positive, max when negative) and re-shares. Probed: a 600 px column
of three `Grow(1)` rows, A `max 100`, B `min 500`, C plain → A 100,
B 500, C **0**; flexbox gives 50 / 500 / 50. No overflow, but C is
empty while A sits at a cap. Also `frozen: Vec` + `contains` per child
per pass is quadratic in the frozen count; a staircase of `max`es
freezes one per pass.

**Do.** The sign rule: sum the violations of the pass, freeze only
the violators of that sign, re-share; a `frozen` bitset or a flag in a
scratch `Vec<u8>` indexed by child. Add the probe as a test beside
`a_grow_childs_clamp_is_its_siblings_room`, and a bench row (a column
of 1k grow rows with `max_height`) for the guard.

### `!` RG6 — F75's floor fix drops a node-anchored float root's own `min: fit`: the sixth pass clamps it with a floor of 0 — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** As written: `anchored` reads `tree.specs[c].layout`
after `fit_widths` for the width clamp and after `fit_heights` for the
height clamp, where a `Min::FIT` of its own has just resolved to its
number; the copy taken before the re-run is gone. Test
`a_node_anchored_floats_own_fit_floor_holds_against_its_anchor` beside
the floor test: a `FloatAnchor::Node` float with `width: grow` +
`min_width: fit` and `height: grow` + `min_height: fit` around a
400 × 250 box, anchored to a 300 × 200 node → 400 × 250 (was 300 × 200
on this tree, 400 × 250 at alpha.15). Changelog Fixed under
0.1.0-alpha.16, marked a regression of the round.

**Found.** `anchored` re-declares the floors, copies the root's spec
(`layout.rs:574`), and runs `fit_widths` over the float — which
resolves `tree.specs[c].layout.min_w` to its number — but the copy
still holds `Min::FIT` (−1), and `spec.clamp_w`/`clamp_h` (`:576`,
`:585`) read `resolved()` = 0. Before F75 the copy carried the first
run's number. Probed: a `FloatAnchor::Node` float with `width: grow`
+ `min_width: fit` around a 400 px box, anchored to a 300 px node →
300, was 400. The devtools' own tab float declares no floor, so only
a Rust app's own node-anchored float sees it — but it is a regression
of this round.

**Do.** Read `tree.specs[c].layout` again after `fit_widths` (and after
`fit_heights` for the height) before clamping; the probe as a test in
`table_layout.rs` beside the floor test.

### `!` RG7 — Any in-flow container straight under a table is a row: a column wrapper's stacked children become cells and widen the columns — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** The first half, as written, and no diagnostic:
`is_table_row` requires `dir == Row`, so a `column` straight under the
table — a heading over its own rows, the grouped-settings shape — is
what a text there is, a child with its own width, and its children
are its own; a table straight under a table keeps its rows. Allowed
rather than refused because the shape is the one a settings list
wants, and it lays out as the author meant. For the flag on a row,
`LayoutSpec::is_table()` — `table && dir == Column` — is what every
reader asks now (the solver's five reads, `diag::check_wrap`,
`NodeInfo`, the corpus's `observe`, `Tree::note`'s `any_table`), so
`LayoutSpec { dir: Row, table: true }` is a row everywhere and never
runs pass 1 without pass 2; no warning, since no binding can spell it
(Node's `2 => NodeSpec::table()`, Lua's `grid`, C's `KUI_TABLE` all
lower to the constructor) and a code costs `schema`, `kui.h`,
`types.rs` and the d.ts. Tests:
`a_column_under_a_table_is_not_a_row_and_its_children_are_not_cells`
(the probe: the second row's cells at 30 and 20, the section 100 wide
at its own content),
`a_table_straight_under_a_table_keeps_its_own_rows`, and
`the_table_flag_on_a_row_is_a_row`. ADR 0033 decision 2 amended and
decision 9 added; the howto, the `table` element row and the field's
doc say which children are rows.

**Found.** `is_table_row` (`layout.rs:102`) asks for a container that
is in flow under a `table`; it never asks for `dir == Row`. A
`column` section under the table — a heading text over a row, the
natural shape for a grouped settings list — has its heading and its
inner row taken as cells 0 and 1: probed, `table(300)` over
`column{ text(80 wide); row{ box 100 } }` and `row{ box 30, box 20 }`
puts the second row's cells at 80 and 100. A table straight under a
table has its rows taken as the outer's cells. A leaf under the table
is left alone by design; a container was not thought of. Also
`LayoutSpec.table` on a `Dir::Row` (reachable from Rust's pub fields
only) runs pass 1 and never pass 2, its grow cells at 0.

**Do.** `is_table_row` requires `dir == Row` (a `column` under a table
is then what a text is: a child with its own width), and the table
flag requires `dir == Column` or warns. A `table-child-not-a-row`
diagnostic is the cheaper half if a section wrapper is meant to be
refused instead. Tests for both shapes.

### `~` RG8 — An image cell is stretched to its column and re-aspected — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** The first shape, with the height fixed and the paint left
to the image's own `fit` row: an image cell's box is its column wide
(`positions` advances by the cell's box, and a narrower one would close
the cells after it up — the reason a text cell holds its column too)
and its `Fit` height is its aspect at its *own* width — the number a
`Fixed` image declared, the intrinsic one of a `Fit` image, the
column's only for a `Grow` or percent image that asked for it —
recomputed in `fit_height`'s image branch from the spec and the
measurer, so no per-node storage. A 16 px icon in a 200 px column is a
200 × 16 box now; `fit: fill` stretches the pixels across it, `contain`
draws them at their size centred, and an icon that must keep its width
and sit at the column's start is a box around the image. The painter
is not taught which image is a cell — the box is the cell, as for
every other leaf, and the hit region and access rect are the box. The
stub measurer gained `image_size` (the id's low bits are the width, a
square). Test `an_image_cell_keeps_its_own_height`: a `Fit` 16 px
image and a `Fixed` 32 px one, each in a column another row's 200 px
box widens → 200 × 16 and 200 × 32, the row 16 tall. ADR 0033
decision 5 amended; changelog Fixed; the howto and the `table` element
row say to box an icon.

**Found.** `table_apply` writes the column width into every cell, and
`fit_height`'s image branch (`layout.rs:748`) derives a `Fit` height
from `tree.size[i].w` — so a 16 px icon in a column whose widest cell
is 200 becomes 200 × 200. A box cell just has room beside its content;
an image scales. By reading; the stub measurer has no images, so a
test needs `image_size` on the stub.

**Do.** A cell holds its column's width the way a `Fit` box does —
the column is the cell's *outer* width and an image keeps its
intrinsic width inside it (aligned by the row's `cross_align`), or the
cell of an image column is the image's own width and the column is
its max. Test with an image stub.

### `~` RG11 — A `Fit` table of grow rows collapses to 0, and the howto's only snippet builds exactly that — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** The first, as written — a line in `table_apply`, which
`table_fit` calls with `fitting`: in pass 1 every row but a `Fixed`
one is sized to the aligned columns, the `grow` and percent rows
included (their own pass-1 width is 0 by C10's rule), so the table's
fit width, read next, is its columns' — and pass 2 sizes the `grow`
rows for good as before, so the number is only what the table reads. A
`Fit` table of `grow` rows is the aligned key/value list the ADR
describes and the howto's snippet builds, and a `min: fit` floor on a
`grow` table reads the same width. Tests
`a_fit_table_of_grow_rows_is_as_wide_as_its_columns` (the probe:
texts 5 + 3 and 2 + 7 with `gap(8)` → the table and both rows 128,
every text one line, the values at 58; and a `grow` + `min: fit` table
growing to the viewport) and
`a_fit_table_in_a_narrow_parent_holds_its_columns_floor` (a 50 px
parent, the table at its columns' 100). ADR 0033 decision 4 amended
and a consequence added; the howto says the table needs no width;
changelog Fixed.

**Found.** `NodeSpec::table()` and `<box dir="table">` are `Fit` wide,
a `Fit` column counts a `Grow` child as 0 (the rule since C10), and
the howto's snippet (`docs/howto.md:69`) gives the rows `width="grow"`
and the table nothing: probed, `table()` over `row(grow){ text(50),
text(30) }` → table 0 wide, both texts folded to one glyph a line.
Every shipped example gives the table a width, so only a reader of the
howto meets it.

**Do.** Either the table's fit is its columns' fit regardless of the
rows' `Grow` (the rows are the table's, not the app's, and `table_fit`
already knows the columns), or the howto and props row say the table
needs a width or a `grow` parent. The first is a line in `table_fit`
and makes a `Fit` table the aligned key/value list the ADR describes.

### `!` RG9 — `activate_menu_item` posts a disabled option's choice — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** In the core, so every door agrees: `Core::activate_menu_item`
answers `Option<Vec<UiEvent>>` — `None` when nothing was taken, which
is no menu open or a row that cannot be chosen (`MenuItem::selectable`:
disabled, or a separator), in which case the menu stays open and
nothing is posted; `Some(events)` for a row taken, and `Some` and empty
for an index past the end, which closes the menu as it did. The two
doors map that to their bool — `activateMenuItem(i)` and
`kui_activate_menu_item` are `false` for a refusal where they were
`false` only for no menu — and the Rust runner (`crates/kui-native/src/menus.rs`)
closes the core's menu on a refusal, since the platform's own is gone
either way and a menu left open would be presented again. Lua has no
door (a script is a guest in the host's frame), so its twin drives
`core.activate_menu_item` as its F73 test does. Tests: the probe as
filed in Node (`activateMenuItem(1)` over the disabled Latin → `false`,
`pollEvents()` empty, the menu still two rows; the enabled row then
taken; no menu → `false`), the Lua and C twins in their select tests,
and `a_disabled_row_or_a_separator_is_refused_and_the_menu_stays_open`
in `tests/menu.rs` over a plain `open_menu` — the three cases through
one door. The door-table row, the header, the d.ts and the changelog
say so. One claim of the finding was off: Lua has no
`env.activate_menu_item` (`docs/props.md`'s verb table says why).

**Found.** `perform_menu_item` never reads `item.enabled`
(`runtime/menu_api.rs:79`); the drawn row has no `on_click` so the
pointer cannot reach it, but a host's native menu report, Node's
`ctx.activateMenuItem(i)`, Lua's `env.activate_menu_item` and the C door
can. Probed in Node: a select over `['English', {label: 'Latin', id:
'la', enabled: false}]`, `activateMenuItem(1)` returns `true` and
`pollEvents()` holds `{kind: "menu", item: "la"}`. Pre-existing for
`openMenu`; F73 ships disabled options as a feature (the corpus scene's
`Latin`), so an app now hears a row it disabled.

**Do.** `activate_menu_item` on a disabled row returns false, posts
nothing and leaves the menu open (a native menu never sends it; the
door should match). A Node test from the probe, and the Lua/C twins.

### `~` RG10 — A select's `options` and `current` are checked by C and not by Node or Lua: an empty menu, a blank field, a check on a separator — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** Four checks, each where every binding passes. (1)
`MenuItem::options_from_value` refuses an empty list ("a select needs
at least one option"): Node's `frame` and Lua's `dropdown` throw it.
C's `count == 0` check is **not** redundant and stays — the finding
assumed C's rows go through the reader, and they go through
`KuiMenuItem::to_core` one at a time, so the door's refusal is the
door's own; a Rust caller of `widgets::select` with `&[]` is not
refused (no error channel) and draws the field as before. (2) A
`current` past the options or on a separator is none — described by
nothing, no row checked — with a new `select-current-ignored` warning
keyed by the field (once per field), raised in `widgets::select_with`,
which Rust, C, Lua and Node all reach; the message says "names option
N counted from 0", since Lua's `current` counts from 1 and the core
sees the index it was handed. C's door used to clamp an out-of-range
`current` to none *before* the core, so it never warned — it passes
the index through now (a negative is still none). The devtools' own
selects pass an index the panel computes, unaffected. (3) A key of an
option object no row reads is an `unknown-prop` warning with the row's
own wording (`diag::unknown_menu_item_key`: "`disabled` is not a key
of a menu item: a row takes `label`, `role`, `enabled`, `checked`,
`id`, `accel` … did you mean `enabled: false`?"; a case/underscore
misspelling gets the nearest key, as `schema::suggest` does, nothing
fuzzier), keyed by the name so it costs one line. `MenuItem::KEYS`
pins the six and `MenuItem::NAME` (`"menuItem"`) the name the pair is
reported under; `diag::unknown_prop` routes that name to the row's
wording, so Node's `warnUnknownProps` needed no new door — the encoder
checks a `<select>`'s option objects against `protocol().menuItem.keys`
and pushes `[menuItem, key]` pairs; Lua's `dropdown` walks its row
tables against `KEYS` under the diagnostics gate. `openMenu`'s items
and the menu bar's are not checked — the finding named the select's
`MenuItemInput`, and that is what was built. (4) Lua's `dropdown`
without a `label` (or with an empty one) says "dropdown needs a label
(its key and accessible name)". Tests per binding: `tests/select.rs`
(`a_current_that_names_no_option_is_none_and_warned_once`, the reader's
refusal and `KEYS`), `diag.rs`'s own (the routing and the three hints),
the Lua test `a_dropdowns_bad_rows_and_current_are_warned_and_a_disabled_option_is_refused`
(the warnings in order, the separator, the refusal), the ffi's
`a_selects_disabled_option_is_refused_and_a_bad_current_is_warned`,
and the Node test from the probe (all four, plus once-per-field and
once-per-key, and `protocol().menuItem` pinned). The corpus `select`
scene is unchanged (its `current` is in range); the 40-scene dump
regenerated and the C adapter and 176 Node tests agree with it. 1277
workspace tests over 97 suites, clippy and fmt clean. `npm run gen`
moved the `WarningCode` union, the warning table and the door row of
`docs/props.md`, and the `select` element's doc; the howto's select
entry lists what is checked. Changelog Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16,
after RG4's, the two entries.

**Found.** Probed in Node with diagnostics on, no warning in any case:
`options: []` frames, and the click opens a modal menu of zero rows
(`ctx.menu().items.length === 0`) that only Escape or an outside press
leaves; `current: 9` over two options frames with the field's
description `""` and nothing checked; `current` on a `{role:
"separator"}` describes `""` and lands the check on the separator
(`checked = [false, true, false]`) so every row grows a check gutter
for a mark that is never drawn. Lua accepts the same (`lib.rs:1305`,
`:1316`); C returns 0 for `count == 0` and treats an out-of-range
`current` as none (`widgets.rs:126`). Also: `{label, disabled: true}`
in a `MenuItemInput` is silently ignored — the field is `enabled` —
with no unknown-key warning, which is how the probe first missed RG9.
And Lua's `dropdown` without `label` fails with mlua's "error
converting Lua nil to String" where `options` gets a named refusal.

**Do.** Refuse `[]` in the one shared reader, `MenuItem::options_from_value`
(then C's check is redundant and can go); `current` past the end or on
a separator is an `unknown-prop`-style warning and none; an unknown
key on a `MenuItemInput` warns in the encoder as an unknown prop does.
Lua's missing label gets the message its options have. Tests per
binding.

### `~` RG13 — An AX click on a control behind a modal fires: a second click on an open select's field re-opens it instead of dismissing — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** Half the finding held. Nothing behind a modal *fires*: an
inert node emits no hit region (ADR 0003 decision 5), so `click_node`
finds none, and `handle_access` refuses every request naming a node
outside the modal at the AR18 gate before `click_node` is reached — so
there was no predicate to hoist, and the probe's second click was a
refusal, not a re-open. What was missing is the other half of the
pointer's press: a press on the inert field finds no region and asks
the modal to go away (decision 6), which closes the select's menu,
where the reader's click was dropped on the floor and the menu stayed.
Now `handle_access` treats a `Click` naming a node outside the modal as
that press — `dismiss(modal, "outside")` on the modal, nothing on the
node, focus untouched — before the gate's return; every other request
behind a modal still does nothing, a disabled node behind the modal is
outside like any other (the pointer finds no region there either), and
window chrome stays live as `interactive` already made it. Tests:
`a_readers_second_click_on_an_open_selects_field_closes_its_menu` in
`runtime/devtools/tests.rs` (the probe as filed, with the pointer's
press beside it as the twin, no row chosen, no row drawn, a third click
opens again) and `a_click_behind_a_modal_fires_nothing_and_asks_the_modal_to_go`
in `tests/access.rs` (a `save` button behind `modal`: `dismiss` with
`reason: "outside"` and the tag, focus kept in the modal; a disabled
one likewise; the modal's own `ok` clicks; a window button issues its
command; the modal's own background is not outside; the same click
without the modal is the button's). ADR 0003 decision 6 amended,
`AccessAction::Click`'s doc says so. Probe artifact worth knowing: two
inputs with no frame between — a click that closes the menu and a press
on the field after it — leak the stale modal's `dismiss` to the app,
with the pointer alone as much as with the reader, since the modal is
the last frame's; a frame is owed after the first and every host draws
it. The verify round of 2026-09-20 found one thing the chip's suite
could not: `examples/rust/features/modal.rs`'s headless drive still
asserted the old silence for a reader's click on the form and on the
dialog under the confirm, and `smoke --headless` failed on it — the
drives run through the smoke binary only, not `cargo test`. The drive
now asserts the amended rule (one `dismiss` with `reason: "outside"`
tagged by the modal in effect, nothing on the node, the app answering
as it answers Escape); a chip that changes input semantics runs the
headless smoke too.

**Found.** `click_node` (`runtime/dispatch.rs:1235`) finds the region
by key with no `inert` check, unlike `hit_at`. Probed: open the Facts
`theme` select through `access_click`, frame, `access_click` the field
again → no events and the menu still open (replaced), where the
pointer path — the field inert behind the menu's modal — dismisses. Any
control behind a modal fires on an AX click, against ADR 0003's rule;
pre-existing, made easy to reach by the select.

**Do.** `click_node` refuses an inert region as `hit_at` does — a
modal's own nodes excepted — and the select field's AX click while its
menu is open dismisses, as the pointer's does. Test from the probe.

### `.` RG14 — Docs and parity nits from the pass, all one line each — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** Nine of the ten as written, one wrong as filed, one
already done, and the two small devtools defects the build order named
beside it taken here. (a) `kui.h`'s ABI history — and `abi.rs`'s, which
had also counted the drop zone's five functions as four — names
`kui_select`, `KUI_TABLE` and `KUI_VALUE_CARET_SOLID` among what is
"still at 18". (b) The F73 archive says what `encoder.js` does:
`label ?? key`, as `<input>` reads it, refusing only both absent — the
type has both optional and is right. (c) Already "five doors" since
RG1 (`teardown`, `pressAndHold`, `hasCaret`, `setDevtoolsTab`,
`devtoolsCurrentTab`); nothing to do. (d) ADR 0033 counts five reads
by name — `wraps`, `fit_widths`, `distribute_axis` twice,
`fit_heights`, each behind `any_table` first, `check_wrap` in `diag.rs`
off the solver's path — in the consequence that said two and the
option that said four. (e) "The nth *in-flow* child of every row is
column n (a float in a row is not a cell)" in the changelog, README,
howto, the JSX `dir` doc, ADR 0033's summary and context, and the
`table` element row (`schema.rs`, so props.md). (f) Wrong as filed:
there is no `KuiNodeInfo` — C's `kui_nodes` serialises
`NodeInfo::to_value`, the same map Node and Lua read, and `table` has
been in it since F75 — so no reader and no ABI note; the ffi table test
now reads `kui_nodes` and pins `table: true` on the one `dir:
"column"` map and false on its rows. (g) Decided as the code has it and
recorded as ADR 0033 decision 10: a percent column's basis is the
widest row's content less its gaps — the room the columns are laid
across, the same number the grow columns share what is left of — so two
`50%` columns with a gap fill the row exactly; a percent child of a
plain row is its cut of the content box with the gap on top, as CSS
resolves a flex item's percentage, and overflows by the gap, which is
CSS's answer too. `a_percent_column_takes_its_cut_of_the_row` pins
both (90 / 90 at gap 20 in 200; 100 / 100 in the plain row). (h) The
"Build next" paragraph's tail narrates the rounds since the alpha.14
tag — the regression pass, alpha.16's nine, the three before the
alpha.15 tag — before the F62–F66 sentence it stopped at. (i) RG1 had
corrected the trait's doc, the changelog and the howto; the F74
archive sentence itself now carries it: a crash under `run` does not
reach `teardown`, and on the pumped path the runner's drop retires it,
so a panic unwinding through the host does. (j) By design, and written
into F68's entry: the runner's clock owns the phase only of a caret it
blinks, so a solid caret's `caret_visible` stays `true` in a window
without the keyboard, where a blinking one is hidden — hiding the bar
is the clock's business because the bar is the clock's; what a solid
block looks like unfocused (hollow, dimmed, gone) is the view's from
`env.focused`, which every view has, and parking it off in the runner
would have chosen "gone" for every app over an IME anchor and an access
caret still declared. `modal_editor` draws its block hollow unfocused
(`Caret::Hollow`, an outline over the cell) and its drive checks the
phase on and one outlined cell more; the runner's comment and the
changelog's F68 entry say it.

The two beside it. `set_devtools_pick` keyed "keep the tab up" on
`d.custom.is_some()`, and since F67 a name no frame has declared is
kept in `custom` with the strip falling back — so
`set_devtools_tab("syntax")` before any frame and then the pick left
`pick_keep_tab` true over a tab that was not up, the panel on the
events tab and the picker's landing meant for nowhere. It keys on
`d.lists(name)` now: a pending name is no tab up, the pick is the
chord's and shows the tree (and drops the pending name, as the chord
does). And `Tab::parse` compares `eq_ignore_ascii_case`, so
`set_devtools_tab("Tree")` — the strip's own spelling — is the tree tab
where it was refused and kept as a declared name nobody would list; a
declared tab's name is the app's spelling and matched exactly. Two
probes in `tests/devtools_tab.rs`, both failing on the old code: the
pick over a pending name lands in the tree, `Facts` / `TREE` / `Syntax`
(refused). The docs at the core, C, Node and verb-table doors say "in
any case" and "a tab named but not declared yet included". Changelog
Fixed under 0.1.0-alpha.16, two entries after RG13's.


**Found.** (a) `kui.h`'s ABI-history comment (`:226`) lists the ABI 18
additions without `kui_select`, `KUI_TABLE` and `KUI_VALUE_CARET_SOLID`.
(b) The F73 archive outcome and commit say the encoder "refuses a
missing label"; `encoder.js:751` falls back to `key`, and the type
agrees. (c) The changelog's "three doors" undercounts (`teardown`,
Node's `hasCaret`). (d) ADR 0033 says the flag is read "in the two
places"; it is five. (e) The changelog, README, howto and JSX type say
"the nth child of every row"; the rule is the nth *in-flow* child.
(f) `NodeInfo.table` reaches Node and Lua; `KuiNodeInfo` has no field
for it, so C's `kui_nodes` shows a table as a column. (g) A percent
column's basis subtracts the row's gaps (`layout.rs:270`); a percent
child of a plain row does not (`:882`). (h) The "Build next" paragraph
under "After alpha.14" stops at F62–F66. (i) F74's "a crash does not
reach it" — see RG1. (j) A solid caret stays `caret_visible` in an
unfocused window where a blinking one hides (`lib.rs:2185`); if by
design, say so in the F68 entry.

**Do.** Each as written; (f) waits for the next ABI bump or takes a
reader (`kui_node_is_table`), (g) is a decision to record in ADR 0033
either way.

### `.` RG15 — F69 was not checked in a window: the pin is proven against `NSUserDefaults`, not against AppKit's read — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** The suspicion held, and further than filed: the pin does
not reach AppKit's read, and neither does the fallback this entry
named. The reader is HIToolbox's `AreTSMAndEventOKForPressAndHold`
under `TSMProcessRawKeyEvent`, and its read — caught by interposing
`CFPreferencesCopyValue` into a bare AppKit probe — is
`CFPreferencesCopyValue("ApplePressAndHoldEnabled",
kCFPreferencesAnyApplication, kCFPreferencesCurrentUser,
kCFPreferencesAnyHost)`: the user's global domain by name, which no
per-process default reaches — not the argument domain (F69's pin), not
the registration domain (GLFW's `registerDefaults`), not the app's own
domain (`CFPreferencesSetAppValue` at launch, the fallback named here,
or `defaults write <bundle id>` before it, bare or bundled — the app
domain is never read at all). Five bundle ids compiled into HIToolbox
beside the key get repeats regardless (MacVim, Vico, Aquamacs, Emacs,
Terminal). Method and numbers are in F69's entry: global default here
was the string `"0"` (off), so the check ran the other way round — the
global written `true`, then each variant driven with 53 autorepeat
`keyDown`s through CGEvent (a synthetic hold does not repeat by itself,
at the HID tap or through `IOHIDPostEvent`) — one insert through
`interpretKeyEvents` for every per-process variant, 54 with the global
off. `modal_editor` moved 54 lines pinned and unpinned alike, since
winit forwards a repeat that produced no IME input, so the motion count
this entry asked for is a physical key's and the real-hold half stands
on kawoosh's report — say so: CGEvent-driven, not the keyboard. Built:
the door's docs in four places, the howto and the changelog say it is
inert on macOS 27 and what works instead (`defaults write -g`); the
test reads the global domain the way HIToolbox does and pins the pin
not landing; `press_and_hold` on the harness's `Window`, and
`modal_editor` says `false` — the example the door was made for, ready
for whichever mechanism RG16 picks. Not built: the named fallback, since
it is inert too; and writing the user's global domain, which F69 decided
against and is the user's call — RG16. The global default was put back
as found and verified off.

**Found.** `set_press_and_hold` writes `NSArgumentDomain`
(`macos_text_input.rs:183`) and the test reads it back through
`-[NSUserDefaults boolForKey:]`. The commit reports no window check.
If AppKit's press-and-hold path reads through `CFPreferences`, which
does not consult the argument domain, the pin is invisible to it.

**Do.** On a Mac where `defaults read -g ApplePressAndHoldEnabled` is
1 or absent: run `examples/rust/apps/modal_editor`, hold `j`, count
the motions; then `defaults write -g ApplePressAndHoldEnabled -bool
false` as the control, and put it back. Write the result into the F69
archive entry; if the pin does not hold, `CFPreferencesSetAppValue`
on the app's own domain at launch is the fallback (it persists, so it
needs a matching reset on exit, or the changelog sentence changes).

### `!` RG16 — F69's door is inert: HIToolbox reads the user's global domain by name, and which mechanism replaces the pin, if any, is a decision — **done (2026-09-20)**

**Outcome.** Option (c), the user's choice: the door is removed.
`Launcher::press_and_hold`, the free `kui::press_and_hold`,
`macos_text_input::set_press_and_hold` and its two test readers,
`pressAndHold` in Node's `WindowOptions` (the addon's read, the JS
`windowOptions` pass-through, the d.ts field, the three test lines),
`kui_press_and_hold` (the prototype, the runner's export, the parity
list's row, the ABI-history comments in `kui.h` and `abi.rs`), the
DOORS row, the harness `Window::press_and_hold` and `modal_editor`'s
use of it, the C counter's call, and objc2-foundation's `NSUserDefaults`
/ `NSDictionary` / `NSValue` features the pin alone needed. alpha.16 is
unreleased, so nothing shipped: the changelog's Added entry is gone and
its door count is four, and the F74 entry's "a free function like
`kui_press_and_hold`" aside with it; ABI stays 18 with one fewer
function than its history comment listed. The howto entry stays under
its question and answers it the only true way: the user's `defaults
write -g ApplePressAndHoldEnabled -bool false`, with why no app-side
setting can do it. Not taken: (a), the global write for the app's
lifetime — the one knob that works, but the user's system preference
changed under every app and left changed by a crash, which F69 ruled
out on purpose; (b), repeating in the app — half a fix (`e` still opens
the picker) needing a physical-key session first. The physical-key
check RG15 could not do stays open to anyone with the keyboard: global
written on, `modal_editor`, hold `j`, one motion is the prediction,
then `-string 0` back on this machine.


**Found.** RG15's check (2026-09-20, in F69's archive entry): the read
that decides press-and-hold is `CFPreferencesCopyValue("ApplePressAndHoldEnabled",
kCFPreferencesAnyApplication, kCFPreferencesCurrentUser,
kCFPreferencesAnyHost)` in HIToolbox's `AreTSMAndEventOKForPressAndHold`,
per key event. No per-process default reaches it — not the argument
domain the door pins, not a registered default, not the app's own
domain, which is never read. `Launcher::press_and_hold(false)` /
`pressAndHold` / `kui_press_and_hold` therefore do nothing on macOS 27,
and the docs say so since RG15. What reaches the read: the user's
`defaults write -g` (the global domain, persisted, every app), and five
bundle ids compiled into HIToolbox.

**Do.** Decide, then build or remove:

(a) *Write the global domain for the app's lifetime* —
`CFPreferencesSetValue` on `kCFPreferencesAnyApplication` at launch,
the user's prior value (or its absence) restored in `teardown`. Works
for a real key (the only knob that does); but it is the user's own
preference changed under every app while a kui app runs, a crash or
SIGKILL leaves it changed, two kui apps race on the "prior" value, and
F69 chose "never written to the user's preferences" on purpose. If
chosen, the door's contract sentence changes to say exactly this.

(b) *Repeat in the app* — the runner arms a timer on a `KeyDown` while
the door says `false` (`InitialKeyRepeat` / `KeyRepeat` from the user's
defaults, 15 ms ticks) and posts repeat `KeyDown`s until the `KeyUp` or
a real repeat arrives. Honest for `j`; for `e` the accent picker still
opens (AppKit's, out of the app's hands) over the repeats, so half of
F69's report stays. Needs a real-key session to settle whether a held
key's repeats are absent or merely swallowed when press-and-hold is on
— RG15 could only drive synthetic ones.

(c) *Remove the door* — alpha.16 is unreleased, so `kui_press_and_hold`,
`pressAndHold` and `Launcher::press_and_hold` can go without a
compatibility note; the howto entry then says what the user does
(`defaults write -g`), which is what kawoosh's author did.

Either way, the physical-key check RG15 could not do: with the global
written on, run `modal_editor`, hold `j`, count — one motion is the
prediction — then `defaults write -g ApplePressAndHoldEnabled -string 0`
back on this machine.

**From the kawoosh secrets report (2026-09-23).** Two entries, F84
and F85, both **built 2026-09-23**, the day they were filed.

### `~` F84 — A paste cannot say its pasteboard marked it a secret, and a secret cannot be copied marked — **done (2026-09-23)**

**Outcome.** The runner answers a paste with `InputEvent::Paste { text,
marks }`, a new variant routed exactly as `Commit` is — the dispatch
folds it into the commit arm, so a focused editor types it and a sink
hears `text` — and `ClipboardMarks { concealed, transient }` rides
into the sink's payload as `concealed: true` / `transient: true`, each
only when set, so a sink that never heard of them sees the payload it
always did. A variant and not two fields on `Commit` so that every
match on a commit still compiles, and a host that answers with a bare
`Commit` — every C and Node host before this — is still an answer: both
clear AR34's one-ask gate. The markers are read in `kui::clipboard`
after the text: on macOS the general pasteboard's `types` for
`org.nspasteboard.ConcealedType` and `TransientType` (nspasteboard.org,
the convention 1Password, Bitwarden and KeePassXC follow); on Windows
the registered format `ExcludeClipboardContentFromMonitorProcessing`
present, and `CanIncludeInClipboardHistory` holding 0; on Linux not
yet, arboard listing no MIME types, so a paste there arrives unmarked.
The menu's Paste is the same answer, so it carries them too. The
other way, `Ui::set_clipboard_secret(text)` / `Core::` queues
`MenuAction::SetClipboardSecret`, which the runner writes as a
password manager does: on macOS the string and both types declared in
one `declareTypes:owner:`, on Windows arboard's exclusion from
monitoring, history and the cloud clipboard, on Linux arboard's
`exclude_from_history` (KDE's `x-kde-passwordManagerHint: secret`).
`set_clipboard(text, html)` is unchanged. The doors: C
`kui_input_paste(ctx, text, KUI_PASTE_*)` and
`kui_set_clipboard_secret`, drained as a new
`KUI_MENU_ACTION_SET_CLIPBOARD_SECRET` kind — a kind rather than a
`flags` field so `KuiMenuAction` and the ABI stay at 18, and a host
that does not know it drops the copy, the safe way for a secret to
fail; Node `ctx.paste(text, {concealed, transient})` and
`setClipboardSecret`, a `{kind:"setClipboardSecret", text}` action and
`TextMsg`'s two optional fields; Lua `env.set_clipboard_secret`. A
`DOORS` row, and the `text` row of `EVENTS` says the markers. Tests:
`a_paste_carries_the_pasteboards_markers_to_the_sink`
(`tests/sink_pointer.rs`); the C, Node and Lua clipboard tests each
answer a marked paste and take a secret's action; and a `paste` scene
in the four adapters' corpus — the `ime` scene's two editors, the sink
answered concealed-and-transient, transient, and with a bare commit,
the stock editor taking a concealed paste as typing — whose event
lines carry the markers (`text ed concealed transient`), so a binding
that dropped them on the wire disagrees there. And on macOS,
`a_secret_written_is_read_back_marked` in `kui::clipboard` writes a
secret to a private pasteboard (`pasteboardWithUniqueName`, so the
user's clipboard is never touched) and reads both markers and the text
back, and a plain string back unmarked. Not checked by hand against a
password manager's own copy, nor on Windows, where the reading is
compiled (`cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`) and not run.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-23), building its secrets work: its
sink heard a password pasted from a password manager as
`{kind:"text"}` exactly as it heard a paragraph, so nothing kept the
paste out of the registers and the working memory it must never
reach. The pasteboard had said so — the runner
read the text through arboard and never asked what else was there —
and the app had no way to put a secret on the clipboard marked either,
arboard's own macOS exclusion writing only the concealed type.

### `~` F85 — A window at a password prompt cannot ask for secure keyboard entry — **done (2026-09-23)**

**Outcome.** `Ui::secure_input(bool)` / `Core::set_secure_input`
declares that this frame wants the keyboard kept from every other
process while the window has it — frame state in `always_on_top`'s
shape, cleared at `begin_frame`, so the frame that stops asking is what
turns it off and nothing has to remember to undo it; `Core::secure_input`
reads the ask back. The runner owns the platform call and its balance
(`kui::secure_input`): `EnableSecureEventInput` is process-wide and
counted, so one `SecureInput` guard holds at most one count however
many windows ask, and at the end of every batch — after
`settle_focus`, for the reason that is derived there: mid-move no
window claims the keyboard — it is moved to whether a window whose
last frame asked has the keyboard by its settled `env.focused` (an
owner keeps it while its popup holds the keyboard, the keys still
being ours). A window that loses the keyboard, closes (it leaves
`panes`) or stops asking gives the count back; `teardown_once` gives
it back on every exit path, before the app's teardown, and the guard's
`Drop` on an unwinding runner — Apple's rule, only while needed and
only while active. Nothing on Windows or Linux, which have no such
switch; the calls there are empty. The doors: a root prop
`secureInput` (`P_SECURE_INPUT`, 106, a `CUSTOM` row — no frame
version, as `alwaysOnTop`'s was none) with Node's `Ctx.secureInput()`
reading the ask, Lua's root `secure_input = true`, C
`kui_set_secure_input` / `kui_secure_input_get` (new functions, ABI
still 18) for a host with its own loop, which makes the call itself;
two `DOORS` rows. Tests: `secure_input_follows_the_asking_window_s_focus_and_stays_balanced`
in `kui::secure_input`, the OS calls behind a `SecureInputOs` trait so a
counting fake — which fails on a disable with nothing to balance —
walks asked-in-the-background (nothing), focused (one enable, and
frames after calling nothing), focus away and back, the ask stopping
with the window focused, two windows with focus moving between the
asking and the other and between two asking (one count, not two), the
asking window closing, and the guard dropped while held; with
`a_guard_that_never_held_calls_nothing_on_drop`. The core's
`secure_input_is_frame_scoped_and_defaults_off` (`tests/env.rs`), the
C `secure_input_is_asked_per_frame`, Node's `secureInput is asked per
frame from the root`, Lua's every-node test declaring it; and the
`chrome` / `chrome-inset` scenes declare it in all four adapters, the
report gaining a `secure-input <0|1>` line beside `always-on-top`, so a
binding that dropped the prop disagrees there. The platform effect
itself is not observable headlessly and is not in the corpus; the
Carbon calls link and run, and whether the keyboard was actually
closed to other processes was not checked by hand (this machine had
secure input on from another process throughout).

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-23): its terminal pane, at a password
prompt, wanted what Terminal.app and iTerm offer there — Secure
Keyboard Entry — so a process with an event tap cannot read the
password as it is typed, and kui had no way to ask for it. The call is one line; its
balance is not: process-wide, counted, and wrong in both directions
(an enable left behind closes the keyboard to every other process
until the app quits, including after the prompt is gone and while the
app is in the background), which is why the runner holds it and the
app only declares.

**From the kawoosh big-font report (2026-09-23).** One entry, F83,
**built 2026-09-23**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F83 — A glyph working set between one atlas page and two resets once a frame and never grows: every frame samples an overwritten page — **done (2026-09-23)**

**Outcome.** `GlyphAtlas` remembers the glyph keys a reset for room
dropped (`dropped`) and whether one of them has been rasterized again
since (`returned`); `alloc_or_make_room` grows the page when it fills
with `returned` set, as it already did on a second fill in one frame
(AR19). A set larger than one page and smaller than two therefore
grows on its second frame and holds from the third; a set that turns
over — new keys each frame, the case AR19's test pins — never returns
a dropped key and keeps resetting. `clear` (a raster-mode change) and
a growth forget the dropped set. Test:
`a_set_between_one_page_and_two_grows_on_its_second_frame` in
`atlas.rs` — six glyphs to a four-glyph page: one reset in the first
frame, grown in the second, still from then on. Checked in kawoosh at
66 px on a 2× display: the caret's row and its neighbours draw whole.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-23): a 66 px code view, the row under
the caret drawing fragments of other glyphs and rows around it
flickering. AR19 grew the page on its second fill *in one frame*,
reasoning that one fill is a working set turning over; a set between
one page and two fills it exactly once a frame, forever, and every
frame's reset landed mid-emit after the caret's row — redrawn every
frame for the blink — had taken its UVs.

**From the kawoosh tab-strip report (2026-09-23).** One entry, F82,
**built 2026-09-23**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F82 — Two reveals in one frame into different scroll containers: the first is dropped — **done (2026-09-23)**

**Outcome.** `Core::reveal` pushes onto a list of pending keys where it
replaced one, and `apply_pending_reveal` resolves them after layout by
the container each would move — its nearest scrolling ancestor, the
only one a reveal nudges: the last ask per container is kept, in the
order asked, and each kept one is scrolled into view with the same
smooth nudge as before (a `transition` on the container still eases
it, F80). A key the frame does not declare, or one nothing above it
scrolls, is still spent as a no-op. Test:
`reveals_into_two_containers_in_one_frame_both_land` in
`crates/kui-core/tests/scroll.rs` — two lists side by side, a reveal
into each in one frame, both offsets where a lone reveal would put
them; two into one list, the last standing.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-23). Its tab strip became a `scroll_x`
row revealing the active tab on the frame it changes, and the pane
ribbon under it reveals its focused column on the frame the tab
changes too — so every tab switch asked two reveals, the ribbon's
later in the build, and the strip never moved: the doc said "two
reveals before one frame are contradictory, so the last wins", true of
one container and not of two. Asking the strip's reveal again on the
next frame hid it, and would have been in every app with two scrollers.

**From the kawoosh Lua-types report (2026-09-22).** One entry, F81,
**built 2026-09-22**, the day it was filed.

### `~` F81 — Nothing describes the Lua DSL to lua-language-server: `row`, `text` and every prop are undefined to it — **done (2026-09-22)**

**Outcome.** `kui_lua::luals_meta()` (`crates/kui-lua/src/meta.rs`)
returns a `---@meta kui` file: aliases for a colour, a length, a sizing
and a min; a `kui.Props` class with a field per schema row under its
Lua name (`snake_name`), typed by its `Kind` — an enum as the union of
its names, a flag as a boolean, a sizing as `kui.Sizing` — and carrying
its doc, the `CUSTOM` composites by their Lua names, and children at
integer keys; a class per element with own props (`kui.edit`'s
`initial`, `multiline`, `autofocus`); a `kui.Event` whose `kind` is the
union of `EVENTS`; and every global function the prelude defines, the
comment block above it as its doc, `t` and `opts` typed by the element
whose `lua` column names that constructor. All of it is read from the
schema and the prelude at call time, so a prop added to `PROPS` is in
the next file written. Test: `the_meta_covers_the_schema_and_the_prelude`
in `meta.rs` — every `PROPS` row and every prelude function present,
`row` typed by the box, `edit` by its own class. Checked by hand with
lua-language-server 3.19 over a kawoosh plugin: `row`, `column` and
`text` resolve, and an undefined global beside them is still reported.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-22): its Lua is edited in kawoosh with
lua-language-server attached, and a view's constructors were
undefined globals there — the JSX half has had `index.d.ts` since
alpha.1, the Lua half had nothing, and a host that wanted completion
would have kept a meta file of the prelude by hand and let it fall
behind the schema.

**From the kawoosh scrolling-tab report (2026-09-22).** Two entries,
F79 and F80, **built 2026-09-22**, the day they were filed.

### `~` F80 — A programmatic scroll can only jump: there is no way to ask a container to glide to where a reveal puts it — **done (2026-09-22)**

**Outcome.** A scroll container that declares a `transition` eases the
offset a `reveal` or a `Core::set_scroll` moves it to, over that
transition's duration and easing. The store keeps the target in
`offset` as before and the drawn place in `laid`, and `resolve` — the
one point layout reads an offset through — samples between them while
a leg runs (`ScrollStore::scroll_by_smooth` / `set_smooth` mark the
programmatic writes; `Core::set_time` feeds the same clock the
animation store reads). The hand's own scrolling never eases and
always wins: the wheel, the thumb and a drag held past the edge go
through the plain writes, and any of them drops a leg in flight and
takes the content where it stands. `Core::scroll_offset` answers the
target (a view's arithmetic is unchanged) and `Core::scroll_geometry`
answers the drawn offset while a leg runs, so a virtual list slices by
what is on screen; `Owed::scroll` is the new bit of the owed reading.
No new prop, no ABI change. Test:
`a_programmatic_scroll_eases_where_the_container_asks_and_the_wheel_never_does`
in `crates/kui-core/tests/scroll.rs` — a four-box ribbon in a one-box
window: the reveal's offset is the target at once and the content
still at the start, halfway along at half the duration, landed at the
end, nothing owed after; a wheel notch lands whole; a container with
no transition jumps as it always did.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-22), the same round as F79. The
scrolling tab's ribbon reveals the focused column, and the columns
carried `slide` so the ribbon would glide — but `slide` eases a node's
place in its parent's layout, and a scroll offset moves the content
outside that, so the ribbon jumped while the columns' own boxes eased:
the two motions disagreed, and the one the eye follows was the one
that snapped. Owning the offset frame by frame in the app was the
alternative, which rebuilds what the store already retains, fights the
swipe, and is what every app on kui would write for itself.



### `!` F79 — A focused key sink drawn outside its container's clip hears nothing: the keyboard asks the hit list, where only what a point can reach is — **done (2026-09-22)**

**Outcome.** `route_key`'s delivery and its `key_up` opt-in resolve the
sink from the frame's tree (`Core::sink_node`) rather than from the
pointer's hit regions: the node `key` names, when it declares `on_key`,
is not `disabled`, and is not shut out by a modal — the two refusals
ADR 0011 decisions 1 and 6 already made, kept. Every other step of the
walk (`key_target`, `enclosing_sink`, the claim) read the tree
already; the delivery was the one that did not. The pointer's rule is
untouched — `emit_node` still skips a culled node, so a click past the
clip finds nothing — and nothing is added to `PROPS`, the payloads or
the ABI. Tests:
`a_focused_sink_outside_the_clip_still_hears_the_keyboard` in
`crates/kui-core/tests/keys.rs` (two 400-wide sinks in a 400-wide
scrolling row: the second, wholly past the edge and with nothing
scrolled to bring it back, takes its key with its tag, while a click
out there still finds nothing) and
`a_sink_a_modal_shuts_out_hears_nothing_wherever_it_is_drawn`. ADR 0011
gains decision 9.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-22), building the scrolling tab — a
strip of columns on a ribbon wider than the window. `<C-w>l` moves the
keyboard to the next column and reveals it; the columns carried
`slide`, so for the 200ms of the glide the one that had just taken
focus was still drawn where it came from, off the viewport, and a `q`
typed into it in that window went nowhere. The same for a column
entering from an `enter { dx }` offset. Focus was plainly on the pane
and the key vanished with no warning and no event, which is the part
that cost the afternoon: nothing in the model says "focused but
unreachable". kawoosh worked around it by taking every position
animation off the strip — which is why the fix is worth having: the
animation is the thing the workaround had to give up.

**From the kawoosh virtual-list report (2026-09-22).** Two entries, F77
and F78, **built 2026-09-22**, the day they were filed.

### `!` F78 — A stroke or a polygon in a scrolled row spills past the scroller: a float escapes every ancestor's clip, the one it was drawn inside included — **done (2026-09-22)**

**Outcome.** In the emit pass that hands every node its clip, a `line`
or `polygon` whose float the core made — anchored in its parent's box
space, which is every one but a stroke declared `float="viewport"` —
takes its parent's clip as an in-flow child would: the parent's own
box when the parent clips or scrolls, else what the parent inherited.
It still paints in the float pass, above its parent's in-flow content
and among the other floats in tree order (ADR 0010 decision 5 holds),
and its hit region carries the same clip, so a polygon scrolled out of
its list is neither drawn nor clicked. A declared float — a tooltip, a
menu, a card floated with an offset — escapes as before. Test:
`strokes_in_a_scrolled_row_are_clipped_by_the_container` in
`crates/kui-core/tests/scroll.rs` — a strip over a scrolled list whose
rows each draw a stroke and a polygon; after a scroll no quad over the
strip names a clip that reaches it.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-22): the undo pane draws its tree as a
graph — the lanes through each row and the state's dot, a `line` and a
`polygon` inside the row — and once the list scrolled to the end, the
rows that had gone past the top kept their dots and lanes on screen,
over the "22 changes · 2 branches" strip above, while their text was
cut as it should be. ADR 0010 makes a stroke a float so it takes no
room in a row; the emit pass makes every float escape its ancestors'
clips, which is right for the floats a view declares (a tooltip must
not be cut by the card it belongs to) and wrong for the one the core
made for a stroke, which is drawn *in* the parent and means to stay
there.

### `!` F77 — A virtual list sliced by a geometry that moved stays a frame behind until the next event: five rows in a tall pane, a blank edge under the wheel — **done (2026-09-22)**

**Outcome.** `ScrollStore::geometry` records, for the build that read
it, the box size and the clamped offset it handed out (`reads`, a
`RefCell` since a read is a read, cleared at `begin_frame`), and
`ScrollStore::resliced` says after layout whether any container so
read was placed otherwise — a box half a pixel taller or wider, an
offset elsewhere, or not laid out as a container at all.
`layout_frame` asks for one more frame when it was, after the caret
nudge and the reveal have had their say, so the frame after builds
against what is on screen and asks for nothing: a resize, a split
sliding open, a reveal, a list shrinking under its offset all settle in
two frames. A wheel notch already sliced right the first time —
`geometry`'s offset is the retained one clamped, which carries the
notch — and still does, owing nothing. Test:
`a_virtual_column_owes_a_frame_when_its_box_moved` in
`crates/kui-core/tests/scroll.rs` — the blind first frame, a settled
second, the window tripled (the old box's rows and a frame owed, then
the new box's rows and none), a wheel owing nothing, the rows cut
under the offset (a frame owed, the clamp settled).

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-22): the memory pane, opened into a
split, drew five rows of a hundred and seventeen and left the rest of
the pane blank; the undo pane the same; and scrolling either showed a
blank band at the edge that the next notch filled. The widget's
documented rule — the geometry it slices by is the previous frame's,
a resize one frame late and covered by the two rows of overscan —
holds only if a frame follows the one that moved the geometry, and in
an app that renders on events none did: the pane's split settled with
the list sliced for the box it had a frame earlier, and stayed so until
the wheel. The overscan covers a row or two of drift, not a box that
grew fourfold. The core is the one party that knows both what the view
was handed and what layout resolved, so the ask lives there and every
adapter's virtual list gets it.

**From the kawoosh Russian-layout report (2026-09-21).** One entry, F76,
**built 2026-09-21**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F76 — Shift is lost under the layout fallback: `J` on a Russian layout is `j`, and Shift on the key printed `;` is `;` — **done (2026-09-21)**

**Outcome.** In the core, so every driver agrees: `KeyPress::from_layout`
stands in, for a layout key that is not ASCII (or names nothing), what
US-QWERTY prints at that position *for the same press* — with Shift
held, the upper-case letter, the symbol above the digit, the pair on
the punctuation key (`us_shifted` in `input.rs`, the one table); without
it, the position as before. The layout's own ASCII is untouched either
way (Russian's `:` on Shift+6 is `:`; a Latin layout's `J` is `J`), and
`physical` stays the unshifted position a window reports. The winit
runner's Alt path reads the logical key with every modifier stripped,
Shift included, so it resolves the fallback with Shift off and puts the
modifiers back on the press — ⌥⇧ on the key printed J is `j` on a
Russian layout as it is on a US one, rather than `J` on the one alone.
C and Node pass `physical` into the same function and get the fold.
Tests: `shift_under_the_fallback_is_the_us_shifted_symbol` in
`crates/kui-core/tests/keys.rs` — seven shifted keys of a Russian
layout (`О`→`J`, `Ж`→`:`, `Ё`→`~`, `Б`→`<`, `Ю`→`>`, `Х`→`{`, `№`→`#`),
a dead key under Shift naming its slot's shifted symbol, the plain
fallback unchanged, and the two that must not move. ADR 0002 decision
11 says the stand-in is Shift-aware; the changelog's Fixed entry and
its "What breaks" line name it; the C header, the d.ts and the FFI
comment say "key as Shift prints it" where they said "letter".

**Found.** By kawoosh, checking its roadmap's "`:` in a Russian layout"
against the code (2026-09-21). The hybrid rule was built (kui.md D5,
kawoosh keys.md) and pinned by a test that pressed `о`, `ч`, `ш`, `п`
and ctrl-`ц` — every one unshifted. `from_layout` reads

```rust
let code = match layout {
    KeyCode::Char(c) if !c.is_ascii() => physical,
    KeyCode::Unknown => physical,
    named_or_ascii => named_or_ascii,
};
```

and `physical` comes from `physical_code`, a table that never sees
Shift (F65 made that the rule for injected presses too). So on a
Russian layout, ⇧ on the key printed J gives layout `О`, physical `j`,
code `j` with `shift: true` — and a modal keymap, which spells a
character's shift as the character (`J`, not `<S-j>`), moves down where
it should join. Every shifted key whose layout character is not ASCII
is affected: the letters, `~` `{` `}` `<` `>` `"` `:` `|` on the
punctuation keys, `#` on Shift+3 (Russian prints `№` there). The one a
vim hand meets first is `:` on the `;` key — Russian has its own `:`
on Shift+6, which works, but the hand goes where the key is on US, and
gets `;`, the repeat of a find. Not a kawoosh bug: kawoosh reads `code`
as the rule says to, and the rule dropped Shift on the way through.

## From the regression pass of 2026-09-25

The pre-tag pass over the seventeen commits after the alpha.16 tag — F76–F85 and the Windows device-loss round — filed RG17–RG37; RG17–RG36 were **built 2026-09-25**, the day they were filed, before the alpha.17 tag, and RG37 the same day after it.

### `!` RG17 — A delta mid-glide is measured from the drawn place and added to the target: a reveal overshoots, the wheel jumps forward — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `scroll_by_from` rebases a leg in flight onto its drawn place before adding (and ends the leg; a programmatic ask starts a new one there at the next layout). Tests `the_wheel_mid_glide_moves_from_the_drawn_place`, `a_reveal_mid_glide_lands_the_node_it_asked_for`.

**Finding.** F80 kept the target in `offset` and the drawn place in `laid`; `scroll_by_from` added a delta to the target, while every delta was measured against what is on screen — the wheel's notch, `scroll_rect_into_view`'s gap from `tree.pos`. Mid-leg (drawn 150, target 300) a reveal of the box at 200..300 added +54 to 300 and clamped there, off the node it asked for; a notch back of 40 landed at 260, 110 forward while the hand scrolled back. The anchor correction, the drag autoscroll and the caret nudge took the same path.

### `!` RG18 — `virtual_rows`' height correction is a `set_scroll`: eased on a transition container, and a long glide ends a screen along — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `ScrollStore::shift(key, drawn, target)` (behind `Ui::shift_scroll`, crate-private) moves the drawn place, a leg's start and the target by the content that moved under each, with no ease asked or ended; `virtual_rows` keeps a second anchor for the target, so the row under the target stays the target. One ask lands on the row. Test `a_long_glide_over_variable_rows_reaches_the_row_asked_for`; `a_list_left_alone_keeps_its_rows_not_its_place_in_the_travel` caught the first cut, which shifted a raw `f32::MAX` and stayed at the end.

**Finding.** The correction that keeps rows still while they are measured wrote `ui.set_scroll`, which F80 made the smooth write: on a container with a `transition` the uncorrected frame the correction exists to hide was shown, and mid-glide it moved the target to where the content stood, so `set_scroll` to row 400 of a variable list stalled near row 122.

### `~` RG19 — Geometry during a glide is the previous frame's place: a long glide slices a band short every frame — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The leg keeps its transition and `geometry` samples it at the clock the coming layout reads, clamped as `resolve` clamps. Test `geometry_mid_glide_is_where_the_coming_frame_draws`.

**Finding.** `geometry` returned `laid` while a leg ran — where the last frame drew — so a view slicing a virtual list by it built the rows one frame behind, a blank band on every frame of a glide faster than the overscan.

### `!` RG20 — `Owed::scroll` reaches neither C, Node nor `beyond_cycles`: a host scheduling on the bits freezes mid-glide — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `KUI_OWED_SCROLL = 32` (header, `types.rs`, the parity list), `owed().scroll` and the d.ts field, `quiet()` waits on it, `beyond_cycles` includes it. Test `an_eased_scroll_is_its_own_owed_bit` (kui-ffi).

**Finding.** F80 added the field to `Owed` and `any()` only. `kui_owed` had no bit (so the header's `kui_animating == (kui_owed != 0)` was false mid-glide), Node's `owed()` no key and `quiet()` resolved mid-glide, and `beyond_cycles` left it out.

### `~` RG21 — The thumb, the access tree and the inspector show the glide's target, not the content — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `ScrollStore::drawn` for the three readers (and the `auto` bar's quiet clock); `set_scroll_axis` keeps the other axis where it stands (`ScrollStore::standing`). Test `the_access_tree_mid_glide_reports_the_drawn_offset`.

**Finding.** `emit` painted the thumb from `scroll.offset`, the access tree reported it as the scroll value and the inspector showed it: during a leg the thumb sat at the destination while the content slid. A thumb drag on one axis also set the other to its target, cutting that axis's leg short with a jump.

### `~` RG22 — A leg's start is never clamped: content that shrinks under a glide is drawn past its end — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The start is clamped with the target in `resolve` and in `geometry`. Test `a_leg_over_content_that_shrank_stays_inside_it`.

**Finding.** `resolve` clamped the target but not the leg's start, so a leg from 300 to 0 over content that shrank to a travel of 100 drew 150 — past the end — for half the leg, and `geometry` reported an offset above `max_offset`.

### `!` RG23 — F83's thrash signal never expires: once a dropped glyph comes back, every later fill doubles the page — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The dropped set is forgotten once a whole frame has begun without a fill (`frames_since_reset`), so only a page that fills again on the frame after its reset — F83's thrash — grows. Test `a_fill_long_after_a_reset_resets_even_with_a_dropped_glyph_back`; AR19's and F83's still pass.

**Finding.** `returned` was set when any glyph the last reset dropped came back and stayed set until the next reset or growth. A common glyph — the chrome's letters — comes back at once, so the next fill, minutes later and of a set that fits one page, grew 1024 → 2048 → 4096 and never shrank: 64 MB of pixels per window.

### `!` RG24 — F77's reads are cleared at `begin_frame`, after Node's view already read: the owed frame never fires in Node — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The reads are drained after layout (`take_resliced`), not cleared at the start, so a read between two frames is the coming frame's. Tests `a_geometry_read_before_the_frame_begins_is_that_frames_read` and Node's `a virtualColumn read before the frame begins still owes the frame that re-slices it (RG24)`.

**Finding.** Node's `virtualColumn` reads `scrollGeometry` while the JS view builds its tree, before `frameBinary` begins the frame, and `begin_frame` cleared the reads — so `resliced()` always saw none and a Node list whose window tripled kept five rows until the next event. The changelog said every binding had it. A C host reading before `kui_frame_begin` had the same hole.

### `~` RG25 — A container read but not laid out asks for a frame every frame while its tab is hidden — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** An entry is compared only if this frame's layout resolved it (`laid_frame`). Test `a_read_of_a_container_not_laid_out_owes_no_frame`.

**Finding.** `resliced` compared a read with the entry's last placement whether or not this frame laid it out, so a view that read a hidden pane's geometry after a `set_scroll` to it compared a stale `laid` with the new offset and asked for a frame on every frame until the pane came back — an app that never idled.

### `~` RG26 — F78's clip is not applied to exit ghosts: a departing row's strokes spill past the scroller — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The ghost pass applies the same rule: a `line` or `polygon` anchored in its parent's box takes the parent's clip. Test `strokes_in_a_departing_scrolled_row_are_clipped_by_the_container`.

**Finding.** The ghost pass kept the old rule — any float escapes — and its comment "same rule as the live pass" was no longer true: a panel playing its exit cut its rows at the list's edge and drew their strokes over the strip above.

### `!` RG27 — ⌥⇧ folds differently through the C and Node doors than through the runner — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The Alt rule is in `from_layout` and the runner calls it rather than keeping a copy; `mods` still reports Shift, and ⌘⇧/⌃⇧ still shift. ADR 0002 decision 11 amended. Tests `alt_keeps_the_stand_in_unshifted` (core), `the_stand_in_is_for_the_keymap_and_alt_keeps_it_unshifted` (C), the Node test of the same name.

**Finding.** F76 made the stand-in follow Shift; the winit runner stripped Shift under Alt, but `KeyPress::from_layout`, which the C and Node doors call, did not — ⌥⇧J over `Ô` was `J` for a C or Node host and `j` for the runner, where before F76 both said `j`.

### `~` RG28 — The C and Node doors type the US stand-in, not the layout's character, when no text is given — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `KeyCode::typed(mods)` on the key the layout named — the character, a space for Space, nothing for named keys or under Ctrl, Alt or Super — in both doors. Test `a_key_types_what_the_layout_named` and the door tests above. Caps Lock is not modelled, and the `from_layout` doc says so.

**Finding.** With `text` empty the doors derived it from the resolved `code`, so Node's `keyDown("Ж", {shift: true}, ";")` typed `:` into a focused editor (`;` before F76), where the runner types the layout's character.

### `!` RG29 — A failed device reopen is retried only when something else asks for a frame — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `Shell::reopen_owed` remembers the ask and `about_to_wait` runs it when `reopen_due` allows, else sets a wake for that moment merged with its deadline; a window waiting for a device asks for no animation frames (`Pane::awaits_device`). Test `a_reopen_waits_out_the_second_after_the_last_try`. Compiled and linted for Windows; no device was lost on a machine. Run on Windows on 2026-09-26: a lost device reopens, and one that cannot is tried once a second and recovers; the animation timer's frames during the wait were RG40.

**Finding.** The lost-device arm set `reopen`, and a reopen throttled within a second of the last try returned without scheduling a wake: an idle app stayed frozen until input, an animating one spun a core with nothing presented until the second passed. The changelog promised once a second.

### `~` RG30 — The surface retry counter overflows, and a surface that never recovers busy-loops — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `surface_refused` saturates, gives the surface up after three tries with one line, and asks for no more frames, waiting for the timed reopen. Test `a_refused_surface_is_retried_then_given_up_once_and_the_count_saturates`.

**Finding.** `surface_tries: u8` rose once a frame while a reopen was throttled and could overflow (a debug panic), and a surface answering `Validation` forever requested a redraw every frame with no present, and a device teardown each second.

### `~` RG31 — `report_faults` reports first-chance exceptions a driver or V8 handles, and spends its one report on them — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The report comes from the unhandled-exception filter, chaining to any filter installed before it; a vectored handler registered last only records each thread's last fault, which names the fault inside a `0xC000041D`. The line is built in a stack buffer and written to the stderr handle. Tests `a_fault_line_names_the_code_the_address_and_the_module`, `a_fault_line_too_long_is_cut_at_a_character`. **Not run on Windows**: whether a `0xC000041D` reaches the filter rather than a fail-fast is the hand check left, with 0ded2e6's fault binary. Checked on 2026-09-26 (the Windows regression round): a fault in a view reaches the filter as itself, before it escapes, and is named with its module; the process then ends `0xC000041D`.

**Finding.** A vectored handler registered first sees every exception before SEH does, so a driver's or V8's wasm trap handler's recovered access violation printed a fault line and used up the kind's once-only report; the real crash later was not named. The handler also allocated inside a fault.

### `.` RG32 — Subpixel text is decided once, from the first device — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `reopen_device` decides again and applies it to every window's core (`subpixel_on`, `wanted_text_aa`, shared with the first window). Test `subpixel_text_is_decided_by_each_device`.

**Finding.** `set_subpixel_text` was decided from the first device's dual-source support; a reopen onto an adapter without it kept LCD masks for a pipeline that cannot blend them.

### `!` RG33 — F81's meta file disagrees with the parser: `percent`, `repeat`, `key` as a number, every table optional — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** Types from the parser's own kinds, names by `LUA_ALIASES` minus keywords, optional only where the prelude defaults or checks (read from its source), a `kui.VirtualColumn` class, `text(n)` takes a number; the prelude reads `pad` as a number or its `t`/`y`/`all` edge. Tests run a value of every annotated type through the parser and every parsed probe through the type (`every_annotated_type_parses`, `every_parsed_value_is_admitted` and four more); checked with lua-language-server 3.19.1.

**Finding.** The sizing alias offered `{ percent = n }` where `parse_sizing` reads `pct`; `key` was `integer|string` where it must be a string, `index` and `row_count` `integer|string` where they must be numbers, `size` a bare number where `"$token"` parses; the field was written as `repeat`, a Lua keyword no table can write, and `direction` was missing; every constructor's `t`/`opts` was optional where thirteen index it unchecked. The prelude's `virtual_column` crashed on a table `pad`.

### `!` RG34 — The devtools' events tab shows a concealed paste's text — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** The stream keeps a concealed paste's markers and its length (`‹concealed, 7 chars›`), never its text. Test `the_stream_never_keeps_a_concealed_pastes_text`.

**Finding.** `devtools_log` cloned every payload into the stream, so a password pasted into a terminal pane with the panel open was shown in the events tab in plain view and kept for the stream's life — the one thing F84 exists to keep out of memory.

### `.` RG35 — `arboard = "3"` admits versions without F84's `exclude_from_*` — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `arboard = "3.6"`. The lockfile did not move.

**Finding.** The Windows and Linux secret writes call arboard's `exclude_from_*`, built and tested against 3.6.1; the requirement let a downstream resolver keep an older 3.x.

### `.` RG36 — The docs the round left: the missing breaks and what-you-can-delete lines, stale docs after F76 and F80, the console claims, README's "Opt in" — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** Each written. The preamble's "What breaks." as a bullet list is still not what the last two sections do; left as it is, since alpha.16 shipped the same shape.

**Finding.** The alpha.17 section's What breaks omitted F80's meaning for an existing `transition`, `RenderError::DeviceLost`, `Owed::scroll`, `PropsOut::secure_input`, the DX12 default and the no-longer-panicking wgpu errors; F76, the lost device and the console bullet had no what-you-can-delete; `scroll_offset`/`scroll_geometry` still said "the clamped offset"; the F76 key docs said "the position's US letter"; the howto promised a C host's `printf` the terminal; the changelog said a surface error is never a panic; README's "Opt in" read as opting in to secure input; this file's preamble stopped at F76.

### `~` RG37 — ⌘V with a selection in a `cells` or `selectable` scope bypasses a focused key sink — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** Half wrong as filed, and the fix line with it — checked in
a window before anything shipped. A focused key sink hears the raw ⌘V
press before the runner's chord handling runs, with a selection or
without one, so a sink that binds ⌘V (the only way a sink ever pasted:
with no selection the runner never takes the chord) already pasted by
its own `request_paste`. What the runner's `Text` did was go nowhere —
or into a focused list's type-ahead — and, with a stock editor focused,
type the clipboard without F84's markers. The fix line as written, a
`request_paste` for every ⌘V in a scope, pasted **twice** into a sink
that binds ⌘V (a probe window: a root sink over a selectable `cells`
grid asking for the paste on its own ⌘V, two `text` events). Built:
`edit_chord`'s `'v'` pastes only into a focused editor, through
`request_paste` and `apply_menu_actions`, so the answer is the menu's
`Paste` with the pasteboard's markers; with no editor it does nothing
and ⌘V is the sink's chord. Checked in windows through a Node probe on
the Rust runner: the binding sink over a selection got one paste, with
`concealed` and `transient` for a password-manager copy (`pbsecret`);
the old code, same drive, got its own paste and nothing else; a focused
`<edit>` took a concealed paste once. Test
`a_sink_hears_the_paste_chord_and_never_a_bare_text`
(`tests/sink_pointer.rs`) pins the core half the runner now relies on:
the chord reaches the sink, a bare `Text` never does.

**Finding.** Read, not probed. `edit_chord` (`crates/kui-native/src/keys.rs`)
takes ⌘V whenever the window has a selection scope, not only when an
editor is focused, and answers it with `InputEvent::Text` from the
clipboard — which a focused `on_key` sink does not hear, and which
carries none of F84's markers. A terminal pane with text selected
therefore pastes nowhere, where the menu's Paste reaches the sink.
Older than this round; F84 only made the loss of the markers visible.
**Fix line.** With no stock editor focused, ⌘V is `request_paste`,
whose answer is the `Paste` that routes to the sink with its marks.

**From the kawoosh window-icon report (2026-09-25).** One entry, F86,
**built 2026-09-25**, the day it was filed.

### `~` F86 — A window shows the platform's default icon, and the launcher cannot give it the app's — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `Launcher::icon(rgba, width, height)` — straight RGBA,
row by row, what winit's `Icon::from_rgba` takes — and
`Launcher::icon_resource(id)`, the Windows executable's own icon
resource, with `Launcher::try_icon` saying why pixels that are not the
size were refused (`icon` panics, as `extension_as` does, on what is a
programming error at startup). The new `kui::icon` module makes them
into winit icons once, when the launcher becomes the shell, and
`Shell::window_attrs` hands them to every window it creates — the main
one, a declared one and a popup alike. On Windows the resource wins
over the pixels and is loaded twice: the small icon (`ICON_SMALL`, the
title bar) at `SM_CXSMICON` and the large (`ICON_BIG`, Alt-Tab and the
taskbar) at the system's default, so each takes the frame the `.ico`
drew for its size rather than one frame scaled; a resource the
executable lacks is said once on stderr and the pixels are used. X11
gets the pixels as `_NET_WM_ICON`. macOS and Wayland have no window
icon — the Dock draws the bundle's `.icns`, a Wayland compositor the
`.desktop` file's — and winit ignores it there, as the docs say. The
doors: Node's `WindowOptions.icon` — `{rgba, width, height, resource}`,
the `rgba` a `Uint8Array` taken out as bytes before the rest of the
options become JSON (N-API's JSON conversion reads a typed array into
a map of index keys, a quarter of a million for a 256 px icon), each
malformed half refused by the constructor before anything opens; C's
`kui_set_icon(rgba, width, height, resource)`, a free function the
next `kui_run` / `kui_run_with` takes, as `kui_on_teardown` is and for
its reason — `kui_run` takes no config, and a `KuiRunConfig` append
would have cost the ABI bump a function does not (ABI still 18). Lua
has no launcher; its host's is the one. One `DOORS` row. Tests:
`pixels_that_are_not_the_size_are_refused_with_the_reason` and
`every_window_is_created_with_the_launcher_s_icon_and_none_without` in
`kui::icon`; the ffi's `an_icon_is_pixels_of_its_size_and_a_16_bit_resource`;
Node's `a window's icon reaches the constructor, and pixels that are
not the size are refused (F86)`; the C counter's headless drive checks
the two refusals and its window sets a drawn disc as its icon. The
corpus is headless scenes and an icon is nothing a frame carries, so
it has no scene. The Windows path is compiled and linted for
`x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (`cargo xwin clippy`) and not run; the X11
path is winit's generic one and not run either; on macOS winit
ignores it, and kawoosh's window opened with both passed.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-25): its icon is linked into
`kawoosh.exe` as `1 ICON`, where Explorer and the Start menu's shortcut
draw it, and the window's title bar and Alt-Tab still showed Windows'
default. winit 0.30 registers its window class with no icon (`hIcon:
0`), so a program's resource never reaches a window unless the window
is given one — alacritty passes `Icon::from_resource(1, None)` for the
same reason — and kui creates every window itself, in `window_attrs`,
without ever calling `with_window_icon`, so no app on kui could.
