**From the alpha.14, alpha.16 and alpha.18 upgrade reports (2026-09-25).**
Five entries filed together, F88–F92, all **built 2026-09-25**, the day
they were filed, each on its own branch and merged the same day.

### `!` F88 — A hovered control's accessible name reads its tooltip — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** Built the second way the entry offered, the hint marked
where it is built, not every float skipped — the corpus was checked
first, as the entry asked, and no scene names a control from a float's
text (its floats are paint, a modal and a popover with labels of their
own, and the tooltip scene's group is not named from content), so the
corpus did not decide it. Two things did. A live region is named by
`content_name` too, and a live region whose message is a floated toast
would have gone silent under the first rule; and a float is where a
node is drawn, not what it is — a badge floated onto a button's corner
is the button's content. What makes the hint not content is that the
`tooltip` prop has already put the same string in the description.

So `widgets::hover_hint` (crate-private) is `tooltip`'s chrome and text
under `role="none"`, and it is what `Core::close` floats for the
prop, what the stock button floats in its body, and what the devtools'
three prop-spelled buttons float; `content_name` skips a `role="none"`
subtree below the node it names, as `lines_under` and `custom_editor`
already did for a custom editor's text. The `tooltip` *element* —
`widgets::tooltip` / `tooltip_with`, `<tooltip>`, `kui_tooltip` — is
unchanged: it can be drawn with no description behind it, and its text
stays content. The stock button, handed a hint with no description on
its spec (a Rust caller that skipped `apply_tooltip`; the bindings set
both), takes the hint as its description, so no hint goes unsaid. The
hint is out of the access tree whole, which also
ends a second reading the entry did not name: under a node that is not
named from content — the corpus's tooltip group — the hint was a static
text of its own after the description. That is the one line the corpus
dump changed (`node 2 … staticText … a hint`), the tooltip scene's
expected access lines with it, and the corpus coverage gained a float
guard so the hint's `role="none"` does not count as `latencyGraph`.

Test: `a_hovered_control_keeps_its_name_and_its_hint_is_the_description`
(`tests/access.rs`) — a box lowered through `open_from` with `onClick`,
`tooltip="KEY S"` and the text `SKIP`, a `role="group"` box with a
tooltip, and a stock button given only the hint `Starts it`, the
pointer moved over each in turn: each frame draws the hint, every name
stays (`SKIP`, `Go`, the group unnamed), every description is the hint,
and the only static text in the tree is the group's own. With the skip taken out it
fails on the first hover, `SKIP KEY S`. The C round, Node's 180 and the
Lua adapter run the tooltip scene through the prop against the new dump.

**Found** (pomodoro, alpha.16 wish 4 and carried in alpha.18: "a focused
control with a `tooltip` reads the tooltip's text inside its `name`,
`SKIP KEY S`"). The symptom is real, but the trigger is hover, not focus.
The `tooltip` prop builds its hint as the node's last child while the
node is hovered (`runtime/builder.rs:507-518`, `is_hovered(key)` alone),
and a role named from content — a button included — joins every
`Text` in its subtree (`access.rs:911`, `content_name`), the hint's
among them. Probed headless on alpha.18 with a box that has `onClick`,
`tooltip="KEY S"` and the text `SKIP`: idle `SKIP`, pointer over it
`SKIP KEY S`, clicked `SKIP KEY S`, pointer moved to another control
with SKIP still focused `SKIP`, Tab-focused with no hover `SKIP`. The
stock `<button tooltip>` leaks the same way (`widgets.rs:780-786` floats
its hint inside the body): `Go Starts it`. In a window, every control
with a tooltip changes its name as the mouse crosses it, and a screen
reader re-reads the changed name. The `description` is right
(`spec.rs:1271-1273`).

**Do.** Name-from-content skips the tooltip hint's subtree: either every
float subtree (a float is a layer of its own, not the control's
content), or only the hint, marked when `close()` builds it. Skipping
every float is the simpler rule and the one ARIA's
name-from-content would pick for a popup. Check that no corpus scene
names a control from a float's text before choosing it. Test: a hovered
box with a `tooltip`, and a hovered stock button with one, keep their
names; the description still carries the hint.

### `.` F89 — Five Node and reference docs a release or more behind the code — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** Each sentence rewritten after checking its line again;
every citation held. (1) `cursorShape()` on `Ctx` and `KuiWindow`
(`kui-node/src/lib.rs`, generated into both classes of `index.d.ts`)
says what `input.rs`'s `cursor_shape` and `implied_shape` do: the
topmost node's declared `cursor`, else `'text'` over an editor or a
`selectable` scope and `'default'` over everything else, an `onClick`,
`focusable` or `onDrag` node included; a captured drag keeps the
dragged node's shape. `packages/kui/README.md`'s bullet is now "**The
pointer shape is declared**", with `cursor="pointer"` / `"grab"` /
`"ewResize"` as the examples and the stock `<button>` declaring its own
hand. (2) `frame()`'s doc in `index.d.ts` and `index.js`, the
`drainWaiters` comment, and both howto answers (the settled-frame one
and the real-window one, whose "counts presented frames" became "counts
pumps") say it resolves after the next pump whether or not it drew, and
that `win.frameStats().framesTotal` moving is the paint — `frameStats`
is on the window only, so the sentence names `win`. (3) `frames` is
"the ring's fill, one per painted frame up to 120", in the Rust doc and
in the hand-written `FrameTiming` interface of `index.d.ts`, which said
the same thing as the Rust one and was not generated from it. (4) The
`line` and `polygon` element docs in `schema.rs` (so `props.md`) and
both JSX docs in `jsx-runtime.d.ts` — the polygon's said "always a
float" too — say a stroke in its parent's box space is held by the
parent's clip as a child is, its hit region with it, and a
`float="viewport"` one escapes (ADR 0010 decision 5's amendment). (5)
The howto's one-font answer gains a paragraph: headless text is shaped
against the machine's installed fonts, a text naming no font gets the
first installed family of a per-OS list, and a suite comparing against
numbers taken elsewhere loads its font file (`loadFontFile`, or
`loadFontsDir` and `addSystemFont`) and names it on every text. Addon
rebuilt, `rm -rf target/napi-type-defs && npm run gen` (286 members, no
duplicates), `git diff --exit-code` clean after a second gen, `cargo
test -p kui-core --test docs` and the 180 Node tests green (two skipped
for want of `examples/c`'s build in a fresh target).

**Finding.** All doc sentences, one commit. Each one was checked against its line:

1. **`cursorShape()` describes the derived pointer alpha.14 removed**
   (mind map, alpha.14 "filed against the release"; and the cause of the
   pomodoro's wish 1). The doc on `Ctx` and `KuiWindow`
   (`kui-node/src/lib.rs:2896`, generated to `index.d.ts:2505` and
   `:3573`) says "an `onClick` or `focusable` node `'pointer'`, an
   `onDrag` node `'grab'`"; `input.rs:1820` (`implied_shape`) reads none
   of them, since alpha.14 made both shapes declared. `packages/kui/README.md:183`
   says "**The pointer shape is derived**, not declared", while the
   root README says the opposite. Rewrite both to `props.md:27`'s rule.
2. **`frame()` "resolves after the next pump has painted"**
   (`index.d.ts:4450`, `index.js:716`, `howto.md:1000` and `:1044`). It
   resolves after the next pump, drawn or not: `drainWaiters()` answers
   every `frame` waiter at the end of every `[STEP]()` (`index.js:638`),
   and a waiter asks for no paint. The pomodoro read it as "the next frame
   the loop asks for", which is also wrong, and wished for a `nextPaint()`
   (see *Wishes*). The sentence to write: "after the next pump, whether
   or not it drew — `frameStats().framesTotal` moving is the paint".
3. **`FrameTiming.frames` "climbs to 120 in the first two seconds"**
   (`kui-node/src/lib.rs:1942`, generated to `index.d.ts:1048`; mind
   map, alpha.14). That holds only for a window that paints every frame.
   The mind map's idle window read `frames=4` after 3 s. Say it is the
   ring's fill, one per painted frame up to 120. `stats.rs` has it right.
4. **The `line` and `polygon` rows still say only "always a float in
   its parent's box space"** (mind map, alpha.18 "filed against the
   release": `schema.rs:1615` and `:1667`, so `props.md:144`/`:147`, and
   `jsx-runtime.d.ts:682`). The float row they point to says a float
   escapes its ancestors' clips, and F78 made a `parent`-anchored stroke
   the exception. That is written only in ADR 0010's amendment and the
   changelog. Add a sentence to each: the parent's clip holds it, as it
   holds a child.
5. **Headless text widths are the machine's fonts** (mind map, the
   alpha.16 commit: 43.35 against 43.68 px on one toolbar label between
   two machines). Theirs, correctly diagnosed: `text.rs:880-930` takes
   the first installed family of a per-OS list. `howto.md:1108` says one
   font per suite, but nothing says why a baseline compared across
   machines needs it. Add one sentence there: load the font file and
   name it on every text.

After the edits, rebuild the addon, then `rm -rf target/napi-type-defs && npm run gen`
(see `kui-generated-files`).

### `~` F90 — A declared float cannot take its parent's clip — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `FloatConfig` carries `clip: bool`, spelled `clip` in the
float object in JSX and Lua (`float={{ anchor: 'parent', clip: true }}`,
`float = { anchor = "parent", clip = true }`), `float_clip` in C and
`FloatConfig::clipped()` in Rust, and `FloatConfig::build` takes it
beside `fit`. The bit is read only with the parent anchor
(`FloatConfig::clipped_by_parent`). `below` and `above` anchor to the
parent and so read it, and a viewport or node anchor escapes whether
the bit is set or not. Both paint passes in `runtime/emit.rs`, the live
`drawn_in_parent` and the ghost pass's `escapes`, now test that bit and
no longer look at the content. F78's rule is the same bit:
`float_box_for` sets `clip` on every `line` and `polygon` it floats,
so a stroke in its parent's box is clipped as before and a
`float="viewport"` one still escapes. `scroll.rs`'s F78 and RG26 tests
pass unchanged. The hit regions needed nothing, since they take the
clip a node was emitted under. Paint order is unchanged: the float is
still its own layer over its in-flow siblings and is only cut.

Where it crosses a boundary: C appends `uint32_t float_clip` to
`KuiSpec`, which bumps the ABI to 19 (an [in] append under the amended
rule, as ABI 18's was), and `kui_spec_float_preset` clears it with the
other float fields. Node's float stanza keeps its width, but its last
slot, `fit` as 0 or 1 until now, becomes a flags word (1 `fit`,
2 `clip`). That bumps the frame to v16, because an addon that read
`== 1` would lose `fit` whenever `clip` came with it. The schema's
`float` row (so `props.md`), the JSX `FloatProp` and the header say
what the bit does. ADR 0010 decision 5 is amended again, and a closing
amendment says F78's stroke rule is now this bit set by default for
strokes.

Tests: `tests/float.rs` builds the mind map's shape, a toolbar over a
`clip` canvas with a node panned 20 px past the canvas's top.
`a_clipped_float_is_cut_by_its_parent_and_not_hit_past_it` checks that
the node's quad names the canvas's clip and still draws after the
toolbar, that a press over the toolbar where the cut half would be
reaches the toolbar, and that a press on the half inside the canvas
reaches the node over the in-flow box under it.
`an_unclipped_float_still_escapes_its_parents_clip` covers the report's
case, the node drawn over the toolbar and taking the press.
`clip_is_read_with_the_parent_anchor_only` covers a viewport float with
the bit set, and `a_departing_clipped_float_is_cut_by_its_parent` checks
that the exit ghost keeps the cut. The first and the last fail with the
rule disabled. `composites.rs` checks that `build` ORs the bit in and
that a viewport float does not read it. The ffi's schema parity round
trips `float_clip`, and kui-node checks the flags word bit by bit.
Corpus: `clip-float` (42 scenes) has two nodes on a clipping canvas,
panned half past its top edge, one clipped and one not. The events are
`toolbar`, `node` and `free`, so a binding that drops the bit sends the
first press to the node. It is re-expressed in Lua, C and Node.

**Found** (mind map, alpha.18 "asked of kui"). The mind map is the
"canvas of floats" `props.md` names twice: a `clip` canvas whose nodes
are `float: 'parent'` and whose connectors are `<line>`s. F78 put the
connectors inside the canvas's clip; the nodes still escape it. The
report measured it: panned 60 px under the toolbar, a node draws over
the toolbar, takes a click at y=2, and has lost its connector. The rule
is in `runtime/emit.rs:699` (`drawn_in_parent`) and again in the ghost
pass at `:1083`, gated on the node's content being `Line` or `Polygon`.
Hit regions already carry the ancestor clip (`input.rs:948`, "a point
must be inside both to hit"), so a float the rule admitted would be
clipped for input as well as paint. `FloatProp` has no clip, and there
is no in-flow way to place a box at a point, so the app has nothing to
reach for.

**Do.** An opt-in on the float: `float={{ anchor: 'parent', clip: true
}}` (or a `clipped` bit beside `anchor`), meaningful only with the
`parent` anchor, which ORs into `drawn_in_parent` in both passes. It
touches the float row in four bindings, the schema doc, the corpus (one
scene: a clipped float panned half past its parent's edge, cut and
unhittable past it), and ADR 0010's amendment, which should record that
F78's stroke rule is now this bit set by default for strokes. Paint
order is unchanged: a clipped float is still drawn in the float layer
above its in-flow siblings, only cut.

### `~` F91 — A headless `Ctx` does not know its size before its first frame — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `Ctx.size()` answers the `WindowSize` `KuiWindow.size()`
does, so the `S` that `init`, `view`, `update` and an effect handler
are handed has one shape under both drivers. It lives in the JS package
(`Ctx.prototype.size`, `packages/kui/index.js`), not in kui-node's Rust
`Ctx`: the one answer the addon cannot give, the size before the first
frame, is `createApp`'s options, which only `transport()` holds, and a
Rust door to hand them over would have been a setter for a reading.
Before the first frame it is the `width` / `height` / `scale`
`createApp` frames at (800×600 at 1 by default), set by `transport()`
on the surface unless a frame has already run on it — so a `Ctx` the
app framed before handing it over answers that frame until the loop's
first frame replaces it, and a bare one nothing framed answers 0×0 at
1, as `env().viewport` does. After a frame it *is* `env().viewport`:
the frame's size less a docked devtools pane, F43's reading — so the
`frame()` wrapper keeps it in sync by marking the context framed, not
by keeping a copy, and a docked pane comes off as a window's does.
`frameBinary` called directly, below the wrapper, does not mark it;
that door is the encoder's own. The `init` and `view` docs say which
answer each driver gives and that no `resize` follows the first frame;
`Ctx.size`'s own doc and the npm README's size bullet say the headless
half. No ABI or protocol change, and no `DOORS` row: `size` was already
named as plumbing in Node's verb-table pin. Test: `a headless Ctx
answers size() before its first frame and after (F91)` — `init: (s) =>
s.size()` under `createApp({width: 1100, height: 760})` reads 1100×760
and `view` reads the same, a later `frame(640, 480, 2, …)` answers
640×480 at 2, the 800×600 default, the answer a copy, the bare and
handed-over contexts, and a right dock taken off after a frame.

**Found** (mind map, alpha.18; wrong in the app since at least
alpha.14). `LoopConfig.init`'s doc (`index.d.ts:4117`) says the function
form is "how a first model gets the real `size()` … instead of constants
it corrects on the first `resize`". Under `createApp`, neither is
available. `Ctx` has no `size()` (only `KuiWindow` does, `index.d.ts:3088`),
and `createApp`'s `width`/`height` exist only inside `transport()`
(`index.js:243`), which passes them to `surface.frame(...)`. The first
frame establishes the viewport rather than reporting it, so no `resize`
follows. `env().viewport` is 0×0 until then, as its doc says. The mind
map's suite fitted its map to a 1000×700 fallback and drew it in an
1100×760 frame for four releases. Nothing caught it because the view and
the checks read the same wrong numbers. `view`'s doc also offers `size()`
"to pick the tier", which a headless view cannot call.

**Do.** Give `Ctx` a `size()` that answers what the next `frame()` will
lay out in: the size of the last frame, and before the first frame the
`width`/`height`/`scale` that `createApp` hands the surface. `transport()`
can set these on the `Ctx` it made, since a `Ctx` the app passes in is
the app's. Keep the `WindowSize` shape so `S` is the same for both
drivers, and state in the `init` doc which answer a headless loop gets.
Test: `init: (s) => s.size()` under `createApp({width: 1100, height:
760})` reads 1100×760.

### `.` F92 — Node cannot tell the app's quads from the dock's — **done (2026-09-25)**

**Outcome.** `Core::host_rect()`: the rect the current frame laid the
host out in, window logical px — `Core::viewport()` with its origin,
the `dt_area` every host coordinate was already relative to. The
frame's reading, as `viewport()` is, so it is zero before the first
frame (`host_area(window)` stays the pre-frame size) and it is the rect
the frame's quads were drawn against, which is what a filter needs —
a window resized since would give a pre-frame answer another frame's.
The doors: Node's `hostArea()` on both classes, `{x, y, w, h}` (the
`Rect` shape `caretRect()` has), beside `size()`; C's
`kui_host_rect(ctx, KuiLayoutRect *)`, a new function writing the
[out] struct `kui_layout_of` already had (ABI still 18, noted in the
header's ABI block); Lua `No(GUEST)` — a script lays out in its own
viewport and reads nothing back. One `DOORS` row, the Node pin taking
it through the row and not the plumbing list. `env().viewport` was not
given an origin: every coordinate the host is handed is in its own
viewport (ADR 0024's `devtools_translate`), where the origin is zero,
so an origin there would have been the one window-coordinate field in
the reading. The quads are physical px, so the filter is the rect
times the scale, and every doc says so. One thing the filter does not
split, found by the Node test and pinned rather than changed: an app's
root `bg` is the window's background as well as the container's —
`devtools_configure_root` gives it to both — so under a dock it paints
the whole window beneath the pane, and that one quad of the app's
falls outside the rect; each door's doc names it. Tests:
`the_host_rect_places_the_app_and_separates_its_quads_from_the_dock_s`
in the core (left and bottom docks, and left at scale 2: the rect is
the dock's leftover, the fill and both cards are inside it, nothing
else is but the container's copy of the root background, and the
window-wide root fill is the one app quad outside), the ffi's
`the_host_rect_is_the_frame_s_viewport_with_its_origin` (zeros before
the frame, the pane's width under a left dock, the host's fill at the
rect in physical px, the strip under a bottom dock, a NULL out), and
Node's `hostArea() places the app beside the dock and filters its
quads (F92)`, the same split through `decodeQuads`. A `KuiWindow`
answers from the same core and needs a display, so the headless `Ctx`
pins it; the class has the method, which the verb-table test checks.
The `quads({ app: true })` split by layer was not built — the rect
serves the report's use.

**Found** (pomodoro, alpha.16 wish 5, carried in alpha.18). F43 fixed
half of this: `size()` and `env().viewport` answer what the dock leaves
(`devtools/mod.rs:1277`, `host_area`). The other half is open.
`quads()` copies the whole display list (`kui-node/src/lib.rs:2302`),
no `Quad` field says which layer drew it, and the host area's origin
(`x` = the pane's width under a left dock, `devtools/mod.rs:1324-1345`)
reaches only the Rust runner (`crates/kui-native/src/lib.rs:1683`,
`devtools_inset`). A test can size itself to the host area but cannot
say "nothing of mine overflows it". The pomodoro's smoke test, run with
`KUI_DEVTOOLS=1`, is what hit this.

**Do.** Expose the host area as a rect, for example `win.hostArea()`
returning `{x, y, w, h}` beside `size()`, or give `env().viewport` an
origin. That is enough for a test to filter `quads()` by rect. A
`quads({ app: true })` split by layer is the larger alternative and
wants a use the rect cannot serve.

## From the Windows regression round of 2026-09-26

The Windows halves since the device-loss round of 2026-09-20, run on a Windows machine for the first time (Windows 11 Pro 26200, an RTX 5080 at 239.76 Hz, 150%) at `0853588`: RG38–RG46, all nine **built 2026-09-26**, the day they were filed, and each re-probed on the machine after. The round and what held are in `docs/BACKLOG.md`'s section of the same name.

### `.` RG38 — The `underlines` scene's segment count follows the installed fonts: the corpus fails on Windows — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `Expect` gains `segments_follow_text`; where it is set, `segments` is a lower bound, as `glyphs_min` is for glyphs. The `underlines` scene sets it with a bound of 3, one piece for each run that is not solid. The other three adapters still match this machine's reference report exactly, and `deco.rs`'s tests pin the pieces a width makes. `every_scene_matches_its_expectations` passes on Windows.

**Finding.** `cargo test --workspace` failed one test on Windows: `underlines: segment quads`, 30 against the 32 checked in. A wave is a piece per three strokes of the run's width and a dotted line one per four, and both the width (the mono face's advance) and the stroke (the face's underline thickness) are the installed font's — Cascadia Mono here, the Mac's mono there. The corpus's checked-in half is meant to hold on any machine, and K4's scene (2026-09-15) put a font-dependent count in it; it has failed on every Windows run since.

### `.` RG39 — The Node F55 test finds no message type in a CRLF checkout — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The test reads `index.d.ts` with `\r\n` folded to `\n` before any line is matched. Passes on the CRLF file, and on the mixed one `npm run gen` leaves (it splices LF regions into a CRLF file, which Git normalises on commit).

**Finding.** Git for Windows checks out with `core.autocrlf=true` by default and the repo has no `.gitattributes`, so `index.d.ts` is CRLF on a Windows checkout. The test split it on `\n` and matched each line against `\{$`, which a trailing `\r` never matches: no block was found and every event kind was reported untyped. Only the test was wrong; the file's types were right. A `.gitattributes` with `eol=lf` was the other fix and was not taken: it changes every Windows contributor's working tree for one test.

### `.` RG40 — The Windows animation timer builds 64 views a second while a window waits for a device — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The timer is armed on `animating() && !awaits_device` (`lib.rs`, the `RedrawRequested` arm), as `about_to_wait` already asked. The reopen's `request_redraw` arms it again. Re-probed: no views at all between the loss and the reopen.

**Finding.** RG29 stopped `about_to_wait` asking a window that waits for a device for animation frames, and left W3's `windows_anim` timer, which is armed from `core.animating()` after every redraw. The redraw with no renderer still ends in that arm, so the timer's 10 ms `WM_TIMER` kept the frames coming. Probed by setting `WGPU_BACKEND=metal` under a running animating app before `KUI_LOSE_DEVICE` fired, so the reopen failed for three seconds: 31–34 views per half second throughout, each for no renderer, where RG29 promised none. The once-a-second retry and the recovery were right.

### `!` RG41 — An icon resource is looked up in the module kui is linked into: Node and C windows never get the executable's icon — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `AppIcon` loads a resource from the executable itself — `LoadImageW(GetModuleHandleW(NULL), id, …)` at the system's small and large sizes — and each window creation sends `WM_SETICON` for both, since a winit `Icon` cannot be made from an `HICON`. The pixels still go through winit. windows-sys gains `Win32_System_LibraryLoader`. Re-probed: a Node window asking for `resource: 1` shows `node.exe`'s icon (24 px small, 48 px large); a missing id is still said once, with the pixels used. Test: the test binary's missing resource is refused with the OS's reason.

**Finding.** winit's `Icon::from_resource` loads from `get_instance_handle()`, its own `__ImageBase` — the module winit is linked into. That is the program for a Rust app, `kui_node.dll` under Node and `kui_ffi.dll` for a C host. A Node window asking for `resource: 1` printed `icon resource 1 is not in the executable (… did not contain a resource section)` and fell back to the pixels, though `node.exe` carries icon group 1. F86's docs promise the executable's icon in all three doors.

### `!` RG42 — A slider reads as writable to UI Automation and drops every value set — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** A slider's access node supports `SetValue`, and `Core::set_slider` answers it: with `on_change`, the number snapped to the step and clamped, proposed as `change` with `phase: "end"` (nothing when it is where it is, or when the text is not a number — the Value pattern hands over a `value_text` like "25 minutes"); without, `{kind: "access", action: "setValue", value}` beside the increments. The `access` event's row and `AccessMsg` gain `value`. Test `a_reader_s_set_value_is_proposed_snapped_and_clamped`, and the nudge test's `setValue` half.

**Finding.** UIA's RangeValue pattern has no increment: a client moves a slider by `SetValue(n)`, which AccessKit turns into `Action::SetValue` with a number. kui advertised only `Increment` / `Decrement` on a slider, and `SetValue` reached only editors. So in the accessibility and controls examples every slider reported `RangeValue.IsReadOnly = False` and ignored every `SetValue`, silently. The keyboard moved them. C45's check — "the reader's increments arriving as proposals" — was macOS's, where VoiceOver increments.

### `!` RG43 — A multi-select Open answers with a typed name that does not exist — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The runner keeps only what exists in an Open (`is_file`) or folder (`is_dir`) answer. A typed name that is not there is left out, and an answer with nothing left is the cancel's empty list. Re-probed. The howto says so.

**Finding.** rfd's Windows multi-select and folder panels call `SetOptions(FOS_ALLOWMULTISELECT | …)` or `FOS_PICKFOLDERS`, which replaces the dialog's defaults, `FOS_FILEMUSTEXIST | FOS_PATHMUSTEXIST` among them. Typing `nonexistent.txt` into the drop example's multi-select Open and pressing Open answered with that path. The single-file Open refused it, as Windows does.

### `.` RG44 — A dotted extension in Rust's `FileDialog::filter` lists nothing on Windows — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The builder drops a leading dot, as `from_value` (Node, Lua) and the ffi already did. Test `an_extension_is_taken_without_its_dot_by_every_door`.

**Finding.** The doc says "without the dot", but the other three doors strip it and the Rust builder did not. `filter("Text", &[".txt"])` became rfd's `*..txt`, and the Open panel listed no file in a folder of `.txt` files.

### `.` RG45 — A minimized animating window draws every frame at the display's rate — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** On Windows, `Pane::minimized` (`is_minimized()`) keeps `about_to_wait` and the animation timer from asking for frames. Restoring is a `WM_SIZE`, and its `Resized` asks for the frame that picks the animation up at its clock. Re-probed: a debug build's animating probe went from ~26% of a core minimized to 0.3%, and back to 240 fps on restore. macOS is left as it was: whether deminiaturizing asks for a frame there was not checked.

**Finding.** A window whose view keeps asking for frames built 240 of them a second while minimized, each presented into a surface nobody saw. It is older than this round, and it is the reviewer's "may spin while minimized" suspicion, measured.

### `.` RG46 — Two queued frames on Windows buy no vsync and cost one — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `DEFAULT_FRAME_LATENCY` is 1 on Windows and 2 elsewhere. README, the howto, `kui.h`, the Node options and the changelog's What breaks say so. `frame_latency(2)` and `KUI_FRAME_LATENCY` still ask for two.

**Finding.** C47 raised the default from 1 to 2 on macOS evidence: one queued drawable missed 1–6% of vsyncs at light load. It won the frame back with a display-link pacer that exists only on macOS. On Windows, D3D12's flip-model swapchain waits on its frame-latency object. A release probe rebuilding N boxes every frame drew 2,400 frames in 10 s at 240 Hz at 100, 2,500 and 10,000 boxes, twice each, with one queued frame and with two, and the release counter's 2,390-frame smoke run took the same time either way. So the second frame was a vsync of latency while frames run back to back, 4.2 ms here, for nothing. Linux is unmeasured and keeps C47's two.

## From the Linux round under WSLg (2026-09-26)

The same tree, patched with the Windows round's fixes, on the same machine's WSL 2 (Ubuntu 24.04, rustc 1.96.1, WSLg's Weston and XWayland, Mesa's llvmpipe — Ubuntu ships no D3D12 Vulkan driver): RG47–RG49, all three **built 2026-09-26**, the day they were filed. The round and what held are in `docs/BACKLOG.md`'s section of the same name.

### `!` RG47 — The main window's failure to open is a panic, and aborts a Node process — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `resumed` answers a window or renderer that cannot be made with `fail_open`: the reason is kept in `startup_error` and the runner ends. `run` exits winit's loop; a pumped loop is parked for the next runner, as when a window closes. `run` and `open` return the reason as their error. A Node window's constructor throws `failed to open window: cannot draw in the window: …`, and the next `new KuiWindow` opens. Checked on Windows and on Linux with `WGPU_BACKEND=metal`, which leaves no backend, set before the first window and cleared before the second.

**Finding.** Both halves were `expect`s: `create window` and `init renderer`. Under WSLg, `node:relaunch` opened its second window after Weston had crashed (below). winit refused it with a broken pipe, the `expect` panicked inside the addon, and the unwind could not cross the boundary: `fatal runtime error: failed to initiate panic, error 5, aborting`. A Rust app got a panic where `run` returns a `Result`. A declared window already refuses its pane with a line (`windows.rs`); the main window never did.

### `.` RG48 — Nothing says what a Linux window needs, and a missing `libxkbcommon-x11` is a panic — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** README's Crates section says what the build wants — `pkg-config` and ALSA's headers — and what a window loads at run time: xkbcommon, and xkbcommon-x11 on X11; X11's four libraries or `libwayland-client`; Vulkan with a driver or EGL. It also says a missing portal answers every dialog as cancelled, and that there is no system accent.

**Finding.** Under X11 (`WAYLAND_DISPLAY` unset) every window panicked in `xkbcommon-dl`: `Library libxkbcommon-x11.so could not be loaded`. Ubuntu 24.04 with the X11 and xkbcommon `-dev` packages does not have it. It is winit's dlopen, and no page of the repo named a run-time library at all. The panic is winit's and was not changed.

### `.` RG49 — Clippy 1.96 refuses a conformance test's assertion — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** One negation over the two cases, `!((a && b) || (c && d))`, accepted by 1.96.1 and 1.98.1.

**Finding.** `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` failed on rustc 1.96.1 with `nonminimal_bool` at `tests/conformance.rs:256`, a line from 2026-09-04. 1.98.1 does not flag it, so every round run on a newer toolchain passed.

## From the custom chrome example (2026-09-26)

The titlebar example, which looked wrong to the user during the Windows round's windowed smoke run, screenshotted on the machine: RG50–RG52, all three **built 2026-09-26**, the day they were filed — RG52 from the Node relaunch example, seen in the smoke round that followed.

### `!` RG50 — `window_buttons` alone in a fitted row is 0 px tall, its glyphs hanging out of it — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The cluster's row keeps `Grow(1.0)` and gains `min_height(titlebar_height)`. In `titlebar_with`'s strip it still grows to the strip, which is the same height. Alone, it is a titlebar tall: 32 px on Windows, each glyph centred and clickable. Test `the_cluster_alone_in_a_fitted_row_is_a_titlebar_tall` (the row 12 px without the floor, 44 with it, and Close clicked at its middle). Screenshotted on both bases.

**Finding.** The titlebar example's second use of the cluster, alone in a padded row that fits its content, drew a 12 px pill: minimize on its top edge, maximize and close hanging below it. Layout was as documented: a grow child adds nothing to a fit parent's height unless it declares a floor (`Min::FIT`). The cluster's row and all three buttons were `grow` with no floor, so all were 0 px tall, and the glyphs, centred on nothing, overflowed. The same at alpha.16 and alpha.18, so older than the layout work of 2026-09-25; the strip, whose height is fixed, never showed it.

### `.` RG51 — A Windows window with its own titlebar is square and flat — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `Chrome::Custom` on Windows adds winit's `with_undecorated_shadow(true)` and `with_corner_preference(Round)`. The window has Windows 11's rounded corners and 1 px system border, as every other window does. Maximized, its client area is exactly the work area (the frame's 11 px overhang is off screen, as a native window's is), and the restore and close glyphs are whole. `WM_NCHITTEST` still answers the drag strip, a tab, the three buttons, the top and left edges, a corner and the content as before. `Chrome::Borderless` and popups are unchanged: they ask for no chrome.

**Finding.** `with_decorations(false)` is what custom chrome is on Windows, and Windows 11 draws such a window with square corners, no border and no shadow. Against a dark window behind it the app's edge disappears. macOS's custom chrome keeps both, as `undecorated`'s doc says; Windows' never had them.

### `.` RG52 — The Node relaunch example's custom-chrome window has its titlebar mid-window — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `examples/node/features/relaunch.tsx` builds its titlebar as `window.tsx` does. The strip is the root's first row with the padded content below it, and the custom-chrome launch docks the devtools at the bottom, as every custom-chrome example does. Screenshotted on the machine: the strip spans the window's top edge and the buttons are in its top-right corner.

**Finding.** Seen by the user in the windowed smoke round. The second launch put `<titlebar>` inside the content's `pad={24}` box, so the strip was 24 px in from every edge. The devtools dock took the right side, as every example's does by default. The minimize, maximize and close cluster therefore sat in the middle of the window, with the dock's panel in the corner where the buttons belong. The Rust custom-chrome examples and `window.tsx` dock at the bottom "so the strip stays the window's top edge"; this example missed both rules.

## From the alpha.19 upgrade reports (2026-09-26)

The mind map's and the LCARS pomodoro's reports on alpha.19: F93–F96,
filed and **built 2026-09-26**, the day they were filed. The round, its
wishes and what is theirs are in `docs/BACKLOG.md`'s section of the same
name.

### `!` F93 — A clipped node's access rect is its whole box: assistive technology finds and highlights it past the clip — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** Built as the entry said. `access::Sources` carries the
clips emission already keeps per node (`Core::clips`, ancestors only,
the rect every hit region is tested against), so a `clip` float takes
its parent's and an escaping float none, with no second copy of the
float rule. `node_rect` cuts each rect to it through `access::clipped`.
A node wholly past the clip, or on its edge with nothing inside, which
is emission's cull, becomes a zero-size rect on the clip's edge nearest
it. It stays in reading order with its actions, and AccessKit's hit
test, which contains a point only in a rect with extent, never finds it.
An axis a rect never had extent on is not cut away, so a caret-wide
empty run inside keeps its height. Text runs are cut the same way:
a built-in editor's by the node's clip and, for a field that does not
fold, its content box across, as emission cuts its glyphs (F41). A
custom editor's runs are cut by the clip of the text or line node they
came from. Each run's `char_positions` move by what its x moved, so a
character's rect is still where it is drawn. The access cache hashes
the cut rect, and an editor's run clip, so a clip that moved rebuilds
the tree (`access_cache::a_parents_clip_moves_the_tree`). The runner
needed nothing, and `access_bridge.rs` says why it sets no
`clips_children`.

Pinned in `tests/access_clip.rs`: the mind map's canvas with a node
straddling its top, one wholly past it (a point on the edge, still
clickable and scrollable into view) and one escaping (its whole box),
a child of a plain `clip` box, rows half and wholly out of a scroller
before and after a scroll, a scrolled document's run with its
characters where they were, and a custom editor's line past its
scroller. Across the bindings, the corpus report's `node` line carries
the rect as f32 bits after the scroll flag. So every scene's rects are
now compared in Lua, C and Node against the reference, not only the new
one. That scene, `clip-access`, is the canvas and the scroller together,
with its rects checked in by `the_clip_access_scene_cuts_its_rects`.
The Lua and Node copies were each checked to go red with `clip` dropped
from the node wholly past the edge. The C adapter passes over 45
scenes, and Node's `npm test` passes 190 with the reference required.

Chromium's reading, unclipped bounds plus an offscreen flag, was not
taken, for the entry's reason: AccessKit 0.25 has no offscreen state,
so it would leave the hit test as it was.

**Found** by the mind map, taking F90. A node panned 28 px under the
toolbar is cut at the canvas's edge, and a click there misses it. Its
access node still reads `y=17.7 h=36.6`, so "a reader's highlight is
drawn over the toolbar where the node no longer is".

**In the tree:** `access::build` gives every node
`Rect::from_pos_size(tree.pos[i], tree.size[i])` (`access.rs:1141-1145`)
and never looks at a clip. So the same is true of a row scrolled half
out of a scroller, and of anything in a `clip` box. None of it is new
with F90, but F90 is the first release that promised "cannot be hit
past it". The runner hands the rect to AccessKit as the node's bounds
(`access_bridge.rs:312`) and sets `clips_children` on nothing.
AccessKit's `hit_test` (accesskit_consumer 0.39, `node.rs:344`) reads
raw bounds, children last-to-first. VoiceOver's mouse-over, Narrator's
hover and a Windows `ElementFromPoint` can then name a clipped node over
whatever sits there, when it comes later in the tree than the toolbar.
AccessKit's clip model can't be used as is. `clips_children` on the
scroller would also cut a tooltip that escapes it (a float's semantic
parent is the node that declared it, not the layer it paints in), and a
plain `clip` box is elided from the access tree, so there is no node to
set it on.

**Do.** Clip the access rect in the core by the node's effective clip:
the one its hit region already carries (`HitRegion::clip`), which a
`clip` float takes and an escaping float does not. A node wholly
clipped keeps a zero-size rect at the clip's edge. That leaves it
reachable in reading order and by its actions (a reader's "scroll into
view" is `scroll_rect_into_view` on the key), but it is never a hit.
Text runs are clipped the same way. The other reading, Chromium's
(unclipped bounds plus an offscreen flag), was considered. AccessKit
0.25 has no offscreen state for kui to set, so it would leave the hit
test as it is. Pin it headlessly in four bindings: the mind map's
canvas, a node straddling its edge, and a row half out of a scroller.

### `~` F94 — Nothing says what woke a pump: an idle-window test cannot tell the desktop from a regression — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `PumpRunner::woken_pumps()` in Rust and
`frameStats().wokenPumps` in Node: the pumps whose batch saw an OS
event or a wake. The count is taken where the runner already knew. When
`about_to_wait` takes `saw_event` and sets the zero deadline, it also
leaves a `woke` flag on the shell. Each pump ends by taking that flag
into the count (`PumpState::tally`), so a turn counts once and a quiet
turn after it does not. It is never more than `pumps`. The opening pump
is tallied too. It reads 0 on macOS, since the window's first events
arrive on the pumps after it. What counts is any window event but a
redraw (`window_event`'s rule) and anything through the loop's proxy: a
`Waker` wake, AccessKit's requests, a file dialog's answer
(`user_event`). The Node driver sends no wakes of its own. Its ticks and
`setView`s are redraws, so a stopped app's tick frame does not move the
count.

The name is `wokenPumps`, from the entry's own words ("what woke the
pump"), and it sits beside `pumps` in `FrameTiming`. The hand-written
interface, `KuiWindow.frameStats`'s generated doc, `pumps`' doc and
`runWindowed`'s pacing comment all name it. Each says that two
readings a second apart with it unmoved are a second the desktop left
the window alone, and that a test seeing it move should measure again.
C and Lua have no `pumps` today, since their hosts own the loop, so the
count is only where `pumps` is. The parked "frames by cause" stays
parked: a stopped window's second now reads "frames = ticks that drew"
whenever `wokenPumps` did not move.

Tests: `frame_stats_shape_before_and_after_frames` pins `wokenPumps` as
its own key and value (0 empty, 3 beside 7 pumps). The runner test
`a_woken_turn_is_counted_once` drives `tally` on a loopless runner: a
quiet turn is not counted, a woken turn is counted once and the flag is
taken, and the quiet turns after it are not counted. No windowed test
can drive the event itself. winit builds its loop on the main thread
only, so the runner's tests build theirs without one, and nothing in the
Node suite opens a real window. It was checked in a real window instead
(a scratch script over `runWindowed`, a 300×200 window with a
once-a-second tick, read each second). The opening second read 70–74
pumps, 1–3 woken and 1–3 frames. Each idle second after it read 30–31
pumps, 0 woken and 1 frame. The second in which a Swift script posted 21
`CGEvent` mouse moves across the window read 115 pumps, 20 woken and 21
frames. That is the pomodoro's burst, now attributed.

**Found** by both apps in the same release, independently. "A stopped
window paints about once a second" is the test for F57, a tick's frame
resetting the backoff. Read over one second it fails 2–3 runs in ten,
on alpha.18 as on alpha.19. A focus change is a repaint (the ring
goes), a pointer crossing the window is a hover transition, and a busy
WindowServer is a burst of events. Each resets the backoff exactly as
the regression would. The pomodoro now judges by the median of five
seconds. The mind map re-measures when `env().focused`, `owed()` or
`cursorShape()` moved in the second. Both asked for the count
directly: "what woke the pump" (pomodoro wish 4) and "a count of OS
input events beside `pumps` and `framesTotal`" (mind map).

**In the tree:** the runner already knows. `saw_event` is set for every
window event but a redraw and for every wake (`kui-native/src/lib.rs`,
`window_event` and `user_event`), and it is taken once per batch to set
the zero deadline the Node driver reads as "used". Nothing counts it.
`Runner::pumps()` counts turns, `FrameStats::total` counts frames.

**Do.** A third monotonic count beside them: the pumps whose batch saw
an OS event or a wake. `Runner` gets an accessor next to `pumps()`,
and Node gets a `frameStats()` field next to `pumps`, documented as
"two readings a second apart with this one unmoved are a second the
desktop left alone". The name is the chip's to pick. This is also the
use behind the parked "frames by cause" (pomodoro, alpha.14 wish 3,
carried again): with it, a stopped window's second is "frames
= ticks that drew" whenever the count did not move. So the split by
cause stays parked. C and Lua have no `pumps` today (their hosts own
the loop), so this is where `pumps` is and nowhere else.

### `.` F95 — Two docs behind the code: the stroke rows' contrast after F90, and `radio` without `radioGroup` — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Built 2026-09-26**, with F96. The `line` and `polygon` rows and the
hand-written `line` doc in `jsx-runtime.d.ts` now say a stroke in its
parent's box is held by the parent's clip, and that a declared float is
held that way only when it declares `clip` with a parent anchor. The
`role` row says a `radio` belongs inside a `radioGroup` and a `tab`
inside a `tabList`, labelled with what the choice is, and what the pair
buys (ADR 0007): one Tab stop, the arrows, Home and End moving the
choice as focus moves, and "2 of 3" read to a screen reader. It names
F96's warning for the item on its own, and says `menu` and `list` pair
the same way. `props.md` and the prop types regenerated.

**Found** by the mind map and by the pomodoro.
- **The stroke rows.** The `line` and `polygon` rows say "Unlike a
  declared float, which escapes every ancestor's clip" (`schema.rs`
  twice, so `props.md`, and the hand-written `jsx-runtime.d.ts:729`).
  Since F90 a declared float escapes unless it declares `clip` with a
  parent anchor. The float row says so, but these two sentences
  contradict it.
- **The `role` row.** It lists `radio` and `radioGroup` side by side and
  says nothing about how they relate. The pomodoro declared
  `role="radio"` on its three mode buttons in alpha.6 and had three Tab
  stops for ten releases. It found `radioGroup` in alpha.19 through the
  stock `<radio>` row. Wrapping the modes in `role="radioGroup"` gave
  one stop, arrows and "2 of 3". The `role` row should say that a
  `radio` belongs in a `radioGroup` and a `tab` in a `tabList`, and
  what the pair buys (ADR 0007).

**Do.** Edit the rows and regenerate. The `jsx-runtime.d.ts` text is
hand-written, so edit it by hand too.

### `.` F96 — A radio or a tab outside its container is a Tab stop of its own, silently — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Built 2026-09-26** as `item-outside-container`, one code for both
items, since the message names the pair. `Diagnostics::check_lone_items`
is one forward pass over the tree on the checks' cadence. It carries a
bit per `composite::PAIRS` container down from each parent, and it
reports a `radio` or `tab` whose container's bit is unset, once per key.
The pair's own container is the only one that counts: a radio in a
`tabList` is reported. Lua's meta file and `kui.h` list no codes (the
header names three as examples), so the generated `WarningCode` union
and `props.md` are the lists that changed.

**What tripped it:** the devtools, eight times. Its five dock buttons
were `radio`s with no group, and its tabs had no `tabList`. The four
placements are now a `radioGroup` labelled "Dock" and the strip is a
`tabList`. Close is not a placement, so it is a plain button after the
group, because an arrow that moved onto it would have closed the panel.
The corpus's `sampler` card is a lone `tab` on purpose (it shows
`selected` landing). It keeps the role and pins the warning, so all four
bindings are held to raising it. No example tripped it.

**Tests:** core (`composite_keyboard.rs`: a lone radio, tab and stock
radio reported once over three frames, the same nested in their
containers not, a lone `menuItem` and `listItem` not, a radio in a
`tabList` reported; the devtools raising nothing in every dock and on
every tab, popped out included), Node (a lone `<radio>` and tab against
grouped ones), and the corpus in Rust, Lua, C and Node.

**Found** with F95, and it is the half the doc can't reach. An app
that declares `role="radio"` and never reads the `role` row gets
radios that each take a Tab stop and answer no arrows, and a reader
announces no position in a set. Nothing warns. `composite::PAIRS`
(`composite.rs:24`) already knows each item role's container, and a
`Warning` is "a silent misconfiguration the core noticed"
(`diag.rs:60`). A `radio` with no `radioGroup` above it is one.

**Do.** A `radio-outside-group` warning (name to taste) for a `radio`
with no `radioGroup` ancestor and a `tab` with no `tabList` ancestor,
once per key as the other structural warnings are. The stock `<radio>`
included. Leave out `menuItem` and `listItem`: the menus build their
own containers, and a list item outside a list is only a looser
reading. The warning code goes in each binding's code list (the TS
union is generated; check the Lua meta file and `kui.h`'s list).

**From the kawoosh fonts-pane report (2026-09-26).** One entry, F97,
**built 2026-09-26**, the day it was filed.

### `.` F97 — The installed families come as bare names: a font picker measures glyphs to find the monospaced ones — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `Core::system_fonts()` returns one `SystemFont` per family
the font database holds, installed or loaded, sorted by name: `family`
(the name `add_system_font` takes), `monospaced` (every face's
`monospaced`, fontdb's reading of the `post` table's `isFixedPitch`),
`weights` (each face's `usWeightClass`, sorted, each once) and `italic`
(a face whose style is italic or oblique). It walks the face records
fontdb made when it scanned the files, so nothing is loaded or shaped:
687 families answer in under a millisecond on this Mac.
`system_font_families()` is now its names, so the two cannot disagree
on which families there are or their order. The type is exported at
the crate root (`kui_core::SystemFont`, so `kui_native::SystemFont`).
The doors: Node's `systemFonts()` on `Ctx` and `KuiWindow`, returning
`{family, monospaced, weights, italic}` objects (the hand-written
`SystemFont` interface in `index.d.ts`); C's
`kui_system_fonts(ctx, out, cap)`, filling `KuiSystemFont`s — `family`
and a borrowed `weights` array with its count, the two flags as 0 or 1
— the way `kui_font_families` fills names, a new [out[]] struct and
function that move nothing a host laid out (ABI still 19). Lua has no
`systemFontFamilies` (a script names the id its host registered), so it
has no `systemFonts` either. One `DOORS` row. Tests:
`system_fonts_say_what_each_family_is` stocks the database with
families of the fixture face — `testing::font_face(family, weight,
italic, fixed_pitch)`, the `liga_font` builder made to say another
family, weight and style — and pins a monospaced family in three faces,
a proportional one, and one whose faces disagree and so is not
monospaced; the ffi's `system_fonts_are_the_families_with_what_they_are`
holds the C array to the core's reading and `kui_font_families`' order;
Node's F97 test holds `systemFonts()` to `systemFontFamilies()` and,
where installed, Menlo, DejaVu Sans Mono and Consolas to monospaced and
Helvetica, DejaVu Sans and Arial to not; `surface.c` calls it. A
resource query draws nothing, so the corpus has no scene for it.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-26), building a fonts pane that lists
the installed families and puts the monospaced ones first.
`system_font_families()` sorted and deduplicated the first family name
of every face and dropped the rest of each record, so the app measured
glyph advances to tell monospaced from not: about 5 s for 613 families,
every font file loaded to shape, and a symbol font such as Webdings,
whose glyphs share an advance, called monospaced. fontdb's
`FaceInfo::monospaced` already had the answer: Cascadia, Fira Code,
Iosevka Term, Menlo and a Nerd Font's "Mono" variants say fixed-pitch,
its "Propo" variants and Webdings do not.

**From the kawoosh Han-in-mono report (2026-09-26).** One entry, F98,
**built 2026-09-26**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F98 — Han text in `mono` falls back to a face with no metrics: a debug build overflows, a release one draws the rest of the line at infinity — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** A face whose glyphs the shaper cannot measure is kept out
of the font database, so it is never a family to list, to name or to
fall back to. `text::measurable` reads, with skrifa (the reader the
shaper measures with, re-exported by cosmic-text), a `head` whose units
per em are in the spec's 16–16384, an `hhea` with at least one
horizontal metric and an `hmtx` as long as that says. It reads the
table directory and two fixed-size headers and nothing else, so it
cannot trip over the face it is there to catch, as swash's metrics do.
`text::keep_measurable` removes the faces that fail, and a face whose
file cannot be read, and returns the rest. It runs where a face comes
in. At the session's start, `new_font_system` takes cosmic-text's
database apart (`into_locale_and_db`), drops those faces and builds the
font system again over what is left, so cosmic-text's list of
monospaced faces, which `Mono`'s fallback walks, never names one.
`add_font_data` and `load_font_file` return `None` for data with no
face left, as they did for data with no face at all. `load_fonts_dir`
counts only the faces it kept. `system_fonts` and
`system_font_families` read the database, so a family of only such
faces is not listed: GB18030 Bitmap is gone from a Mac's list. Each
file is opened once for all its faces, and past 64 files the files are
spread over up to `available_parallelism` threads, since opening a
file is most of the cost. Over this Mac's 1312 faces in 850 files, the
check adds about 4.5 ms (release, warm) to the ~20 ms fontdb takes to
scan them; on one thread it was 15–22 ms. One face fails it: GB18030
Bitmap. No non-finite guard was added at `physical`. Past this check an
advance is a `u16` over a units per em of at least 16, so it is finite,
and a guard there would hide a face that had got past the check. Tests: the
fixture builder draws two more faces. `testing::han_face` maps 字
(U+5B57) besides ASCII. `testing::unmeasurable_face` is that face
fixed-pitch and without `head`, `hhea`, `hmtx` and its outlines, as
GB18030 Bitmap is. `text::unmeasurable_faces` builds a session font
system over the fixture faces alone: a monospaced family without Han
pinned as `Mono`, the unmeasurable face and the Han face. It holds
the unmeasurable face out of the database, and shapes "字 a" in `Mono`
with 字 from the Han face and the space and `a` from the monospaced
family at x = 0, 7 and 14, through `physical`. Before the fix the
same test read 字 as infinitely wide.
`tests/fonts.rs`'s `a_face_whose_advances_cannot_be_measured_is_refused`
holds `add_font_data`, `load_font_file`, `load_fonts_dir` (one face of
a folder of two), `system_font_families` and `add_system_font` to the
refusal. `han_in_mono_draws_at_finite_places` draws "字 a" in
`FontFamily::Mono` through a frame on the installed fonts and holds
every glyph quad to a finite place on the line, with 字 before the `a`
where a face maps it. On this Mac, before the fix, that test overflowed
in `physical`. Nothing changed at a door, so no binding, `DOORS` row
or corpus scene changed.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-26): text in its `mono` holding a Han
ideograph, "字 か" or "字 a", panicked in a debug build with
`attempt to add with overflow` in cosmic-text 0.19's
`SubpixelBin::new` (`trunc + 1`, `trunc` = `i32::MAX`), from
`LayoutGlyph::physical` under `text::build_templates`, `emit_entry`,
`TextSystem::emit`, `Painter::paint_leaf` and `Core::finish_frame`. A
release build did not panic and placed the glyphs after the ideograph
at x = ∞. Bare cosmic-text reproduced it with
`Attrs::new().family(Family::Monospace)`. The default monospaced face
(Menlo) has no 字, so the fallback took the monospaced faces that map
it, and the first was GB18030 Bitmap (`NISC18030.ttf`). That is a
bitmap-only face whose directory holds `OS/2`, `bdat`, `bhed`, `bloc`,
`cmap`, `maxp`, `meta`, `name` and `post`, and no `head`, `hhea` or
`hmtx`. fontdb took it, since it reads `name`, `OS/2` and `post`. The
shaper read its units per em as 0 and divided by it (`w = inf`), and
swash's `advance_width` on it underflows `numberOfHMetrics − 1`. Named
families and `sans` and `serif` never reached it.

**From the kawoosh fonts-pane scrolling report (2026-09-26).** One
entry, F99, **built 2026-09-26**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F99 — A glyph set that turns over fills the atlas mid-frame and the page resets under the quads already emitted: now and then one frame draws nearly every glyph blank — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The atlas page is never emptied while a frame is being
drawn. A slot handed out during a frame is not moved or overwritten
before that frame is presented, because the quads already emitted,
the text templates built and the cells tables filled all carry its
texel rect and nothing walks them again. Four rules in
`GlyphAtlas` (`atlas.rs`) keep that:

1. *A fill it did not see coming extends the page.* `alloc_or_make_room`
   doubles a full page with its pixels kept where they are
   (`extend_to`: the rows copied into the top-left of the bigger page,
   the shelves running on into the new width). Every rect handed out
   stays valid, since `uv` is in texels and a renderer divides by the
   page's size at draw time. The epoch still moves, so renderers
   upload the page whole (the FFI contract) and caches that stamped it
   look their slots up again and find them where they were. The
   extension is the frame's own: the next `begin_frame` empties the
   page back to its `base` size before anything is emitted.
2. *A fill it can see coming is emptied for first.* `begin_frame` reads
   the shelf rows the last frames opened (`rows_per_frame`: the last
   frame's, or a quarter less than the figure before, whichever is
   more; a frame that began on an empty page counts none, since it
   opened its whole set) and empties the page when fewer rows are free
   than two such frames would open. A set that turns over a little each
   frame, which is kawoosh's list, fills the page every so often. The
   rows foresee it, so the frame begins on an empty page and nothing
   extends.
3. *A page too small for its set grows.* A page that began a frame
   empty and filled during it holds that frame's set alone, which does
   not fit it, and keeps the extension (`fresh`; AR19's case). So does
   one whose single item is bigger than the base page. A page that has
   to be emptied again within two frames of the last time
   (`frames_since_reset`) is too small for its set and the rate it
   turns over at. It keeps an extension, or, when a fill is foreseen,
   doubles at `begin_frame` with its slots in place, so the set it
   holds is not rasterized again. That is F83's thrash, measured by what
   the page does instead of by which glyphs come back. A fill long after
   the last one, RG23's case, empties it as before. The `dropped`,
   `returned` and per-frame reset count that AR19, F83 and RG23 built
   are gone. `frames_since_reset` replaces them and answers all three.
4. *At `MAX_ATLAS_SIZE` the glyph waits a frame.* A page at 4096 cannot
   double. There a request that finds no room on a page holding
   earlier frames' glyphs is refused (`None`: the glyph is not drawn,
   an image draws from a texture of its own) and the atlas is `short`.
   `Core::emit_frame` asks for the next frame, which begins on an empty
   page. On a page that began the frame empty, a refusal is final, as
   one past 4096 always was, and nothing is asked for.

`Core` drops `atlas_epoch_seen`. A frame that moved the epoch used to
ask for the next one to rebuild what sampled the overwritten page
(AR19). A frame that grew is now right as presented, and only a `short`
frame asks. `clear` (a raster-mode change) runs between frames and
empties the page at its size. The FFI's `atlas_size` doc says it can go
down between frames.

The designs this beat. *Emit the frame again* on a reset, against the
fresh page, costs a second emit only on the rare frame. But
`emit_frame` is not a pure paint: it collects and replays departures,
places text for selection, flushes sounds, reconciles audio and swaps
the hit buffers. Running it twice would take splitting it into a paint
that can be repeated and the bookkeeping that cannot, across every
pass, for a rare frame. *Evict only what this frame has not used* needs
to know what it used. Steady text emission copies positioned
templates without touching the atlas (that is where the per-frame speed
of text comes from), so it would take a stamp per glyph per frame on
the common path. Evicting out of a shelf packer also leaves holes it
cannot reuse. *A second page* puts a page index on every quad, a
second texture binding in every backend and C's draw data: an ABI break
for a rare frame. *Resetting at `begin_frame` past a fixed high-water
mark* resets either too early or not early enough. A set filling 90%
of the page resets every frame, and a burst still fills mid-frame, so
it needs the extension anyway. The mark kept here is the page's own
rate, read from its rows, with the extension for what that misses.

Cost. The common frame is unchanged: a hit is the same hash lookup,
and `begin_frame` compares a few integers. A timing harness drew 400
frames of a six-card list at 2×, three lines of text a card in its own
size, scrolled one card a frame, on this Mac (release). Before, 59
frames reset mid-emit, each drawn wrong at ~0.5 ms and followed by a
~2.2 ms frame rebuilding what it dropped, and the page stayed at 1024.
After, 15 frames emptied the page at `begin_frame` at ~2.5 ms each
(the visible set rasterized again), none extended, and the page settled
at 2048 by rule 3, since at 1024 it needed emptying within two frames.
An ordinary scroll frame averaged 0.41 ms after and 0.70 ms before
(the rebuild frames), and all 399 took ~196 ms against ~272. An
extension, measured before rule 2 existed, costs the frame ~0.5–1 ms
over the reset it replaces at 1024 (a 16 MB page, 4 MB copied) and
~2–3 ms at 2048. For that frame the page holds four times the pixels,
and a renderer makes its texture twice.

Tests. `tests/atlas_turnover.rs`'s
`a_frame_that_fills_the_page_presents_every_quad_on_what_it_was_emitted_for`
runs a `Core` on a 256 page over 24 frames. Each frame draws two
sizes of a line no earlier frame drew, and every sixth a burst of
eight, with one new image. It draws each frame again on a fresh
`Core` with room to spare and holds every glyph and image quad of the
presented frame to the same texels as the reference's. Before the fix
it failed at frame 5, quad 0: its slot held another glyph. It also
asserts that the page filled at least three times and that a burst
extended it mid-frame. In `atlas.rs`,
`a_page_that_fills_mid_frame_keeps_the_frames_slots_until_it_ends`
checks after each insert that every slot the frame had used still
holds its own pixels, and that the page is its old size, emptied, at
the next frame.
`a_turnover_the_rows_foresee_is_emptied_between_frames` runs a row of
new glyphs a frame on an eight-row page for 40 frames: the size and
the epoch never move within a frame, and the page is emptied between
frames at least four times.
`a_page_emptied_again_within_two_frames_grows_with_its_slots` covers
rule 3 at `begin_frame`: the six rows that stay are not rasterized
again, and the page holds 512 for 40 frames after.
`a_full_page_at_the_largest_size_refuses_for_one_frame` covers rule 4.
AR19's, F83's and RG23's tests hold their cases to the new rules. A
set larger than the page grows and then holds. One between one page
and two grows on its second frame, the first having extended. A page's
worth of new keys a frame grows only until a page lasts past two
frames, then holds its size with no fill mid-frame. A later turnover
keeps the size. `tests/images.rs`'s AR19 test now asserts that the
frame that grew asks for no other. No door, schema or corpus scene
changed.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-26): its fonts pane, a `uniform_list`
of cards, each drawing its family's name and two lines of code in that
family (`font = id` from `add_system_font`), about six on screen, over
~600 installed families. While scrolling or filtering, now and then
one frame showed almost every glyph in the window missing or garbled,
the chrome's text included, and the next frame was right. In the
user's screen recording, one card's text is drawn in that frame and the
rest of the window's glyphs are blank or scraps. The cause was in
`GlyphAtlas::alloc_or_make_room`. A full page reset (every slot
dropped, the pixels zeroed, the epoch bumped) and retried. It grew
only when the page filled twice in one frame (AR19) or a glyph the last
reset dropped came back (F83, RG23). Its own comment granted that
"quads emitted earlier this frame may sample stale UVs for one frame".
Every card brings glyphs no earlier frame drew, so the set turns over
and neither rule grows the page. It filled every so often, and each
fill presented one such frame. Any app that scrolls through text in
varied fonts, sizes, CJK or emoji hit it.

**From the kawoosh Berkeley-bold report (2026-09-26).** One entry,
F100, **built 2026-09-26**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F100 — Bold of a family with no bold face draws in another family, and a variable face whose `wght` axis is off the CSS scale draws regular at its Bold — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** Bold, regular and italic of a registered family draw in
that family. kui asks for two weights, 400 and 700. cosmic-text 0.19
takes a family's face for a weight only when the face is that weight
or its `wght` axis spans it (`FontFallbackIter::default_font_match_key`
filters on `font_weight_diff == 0 || variable_weight_match`). Otherwise
it goes to the script and common fallback lists and then to every face
installed, sorted by weight. It has no synthetic bold. It also takes
the CSS number itself as the `wght` coordinate, to shape
(`Font::new`'s `location`) and to draw (`swash_image` clamps it to the
axis). Three pieces now stand between kui's two weights and that:

1. *A family is asked at weights it has* (`weights::Weights`, on each
   `FontEntry`). When a family is registered — `add_system_font`,
   `add_font_data`, `load_font_file` — and when faces of a registered
   family come into the database or leave it (`load_fonts_dir`,
   `remove_font`), `Weights::of` reads its faces: each one's `OS/2`
   weight and its `wght` axis's span. For 400 and for 700 it keeps the
   weight when cosmic-text's own test would take a face of the family
   for it. Otherwise it takes the weight of the family's nearest face,
   leaning heavier for bold and lighter for regular, which cosmic-text
   matches exactly. `Span::attrs` and the cells' `shape_one` build
   their `Attrs` through `Weights::apply`. A generic family, and an
   unknown or removed handle, is asked at the CSS weights as before.
2. *Bold of a lighter face is marked.* When bold resolves to a face
   lighter than semibold, the span's `Attrs` carry `SYNTHETIC_BOLD`, a
   bit of kui's own in `CacheKeyFlags` (bit 31; cosmic-text uses the
   low three, and `the_flag_is_none_of_cosmic_texts` holds that).
   cosmic-text copies a span's flags onto each of its glyphs' cache
   keys, so the mark reaches the atlas and keeps a bold glyph's slot
   apart from the regular one's. Shaped at the family's own weight, a
   bold span keeps the regular advances: a glyph to a cell in a
   terminal. The copy-as-HTML reader counts a marked span as bold.
3. *The rasterizer draws the weight asked.* `Raster::image` replaces
   cosmic-text's `SwashCache` for the alpha format as it already had
   for the subpixel one (the same sources, offset, hinting and fake
   italic). A face with a `wght` axis is read once (`WghtAxis`, cached
   by face id). On the CSS scale, where the axis's default is the
   face's `OS/2` weight, a weight is its own coordinate, as cosmic-text
   has it. Off it, the coordinate is read off the named instances whose
   subfamily names say a weight ("Regular", "Bold", "Semi Bold
   Italic"…), with the default standing for the `OS/2` weight, and is
   linear between two of them and flat past the ends. A marked glyph is
   drawn at the axis's coordinate for 700. When that is no heavier than
   the face's own, or the face has no axis, its outline is grown with
   swash's `embolden`: size/24 up to 9 px, size/32 from 36 px, between
   in between, Skia's fake-bold ratios.

Berkeley Mono Variable: `wght` 100–150, default 100, instances Regular
(100) and Bold (150), `OS/2` 400 on both files. Regular is asked at
400 and drawn at 100. Bold is asked at 400, marked, and drawn at 150.
Bold italic is its Italic file at 150. Before, regular was drawn at
150 (400 clamped to the axis) and bold went to `.SFNS` Bold. A static
family of one regular face (Monaco, or the fixture) draws bold as its
regular face emboldened, where it went to the fallback too. Italic of
a family with no italic face was already its regular face with
cosmic-text's fake italic, since the family test does not look at
style. Cascadia Code (`wght` 200–700, default 400, `OS/2` 400) is on
the scale and draws as it did, in text and in cells.

What it does not reach: a face off the CSS scale is still *shaped* at
cosmic-text's clamped coordinate, since `Font::new` takes the asked
weight as its location. Berkeley's advances do not vary with weight,
but a proportional face off the scale would be spaced at its heaviest
instance while drawn at the right one. A glyph from a fallback face in
a marked span (an icon, a Han ideograph) is emboldened too, its own
bold face not looked for.

The designs this beat. *Ask the shaper at the axis coordinate* (400 as
100, 700 as 150 for Berkeley) shapes and draws a variable face right
with no mark, but the same weight is what the fallback matches with,
so every glyph the family lacks would come from the faces nearest
weight 100 or 150: Han from a Thin face. It also does nothing for a
static family. *Alias faces in the font database* (a copy of the
regular face's record at 700, same data) makes the family match, but
the aliases show in `system_fonts`, and cosmic-text would still draw
at the coordinate 700 gives. *Patch cosmic-text* (a nearest-weight
default match, a fake-bold flag, an instance map) is the upstream fix
and a fork to carry. The family test, the mark and the coordinate are
kui's to hold without one.

Cost. Registering a family reads its faces' axes once: 686 installed
families registered in 38 ms against 11 ms (release, this Mac), about
40 µs a family. A registered name found again costs what it did.
Shaping looks the weights up with the family (a slotmap get), and a
glyph already in the atlas costs nothing new. A glyph rasterized looks
its face's axis up in a map.

Tests. `tests/fonts.rs`'s
`bold_of_a_variable_family_off_the_css_scale_is_its_bold_instance`
registers `testing::variable_face` (new: the fixture with `fvar` and
`gvar`, a `wght` axis of 100–150 with Regular at 100 and Bold at 150,
`OS/2` 400, a square 400 units wide at the Regular and 500 at the
Bold) upright and italic, beside a proportional family with a bold
face. At 20 px, regular, bold and bold italic draw four glyphs 10 px
apart in rich text and in cells, 8 px wide for regular and 10 for the
others. Before the fix, regular drew 10 px wide.
`bold_and_italic_of_a_family_without_them_are_synthesized_in_it`
registers one regular fixed-pitch face beside a family with a bold and
an italic face: bold and italic draw 10 px apart and wider than
regular, in text and in cells. Before, bold drew at `.SFNS` Bold's
advances on this Mac. In `weights.rs`, a family's weights for faces
at both, one, a light one, a CSS axis and Berkeley's; the mark on bold
of a lighter face only, kept beside `FAKE_ITALIC`; an axis off the
scale read off its instances (400 → 100, 700 → 150, 550 → 125, flat
past the ends, the default alone without a named bold); an axis on the
scale its own coordinate; instance names to weights. Checked by hand
with the installed Berkeley Mono Variable through `Core` (a scratch
example, deleted): "main v0.0.1 a::b" in cells, plain and bold, draws
each glyph at the same place 1–2 px wider, where bold had been `m` 22
px and `i` 5 at 2×.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-26): its terminal, a `cells` node in
Berkeley Mono Variable (`add_system_font`, `system_fonts()` saying
monospaced, one weight, an italic). `printf "\e[1m… main v0.0.1 a::b
ii.jj mini"` drew no slash in `0`, a squeezed `m`, gaps round `i` and
after `.`: another family's glyphs one to a cell. Plain, italic and dim
looked right, and wezterm drew the same font's bold right. The same
report asked about `&tsx::LANGUAGE` in the editor's rich rows, drawn
as `&tsx :: LANGUAGE`. That one is the font: Berkeley's contextual
alternates shape `::` as an empty glyph and a pair of dots centred on
the boundary between the two cells (glyphs 618 and 276 where a lone
`:` is 326), so the pair sits apart from its neighbours. Shaped whole
or in spans, the advances are the cell's. The cells node shapes a
cell alone and so draws two plain colons. A style that turns `calt`
off (`TextStyle::features`) draws plain colons in text too.
