**From the regression pass of 2026-09-26.** Seven entries, RG53–RG59,
**built 2026-09-26**, the day they were filed: RG53–RG55, RG57 and
RG58 before the alpha.21 tag, RG56 and RG59 after it.

### `!` RG53 — Every image updated between frames keeps a second full-size buffer for life — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `update_image_with` keeps the buffer an update replaced
only for an image updated before (not the pixels it was added with)
and only at the new update's size, and `Resources::release_spares`,
run from `begin_frame`, lets a spare go `SPARE_FRAMES` (30) frames
after the image's last update. A buffer reused at a smaller size is
shrunk when its capacity is more than twice what it holds. `update_image`
drops the spare. A running stream still alternates two buffers and
allocates nothing from its fourth update.

**What was wrong.** W20 kept the replaced buffer as the image's spare
on every update the display list still held, and nothing let it go.
Every Node `updateImage` and C `kui_image_update` goes that way, not
only a stream, so an image updated once held its old pixels beside
the new ones for as long as it lived: an 8K image updated once in a
Node viewer held ~264 MB where it had held ~132. A 4K stream updated
later at 100×100 kept both 33 MB buffers, the one in use resized in
place.

**Pinned by** `an_image_updated_once_keeps_no_spare`,
`a_stopped_stream_gives_its_spare_back` and
`a_shrunk_stream_drops_its_old_size` in `tests/images.rs`, each seen
failing with its condition taken out.

### `.` RG54 — Three edges of update_image_with: a Weak on the pixels panics, a fill that panics leaves size and length apart, and the C door slices a wrapped length — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The spare is reused only when no `Arc` and no `Weak`
holds it. The width, height, revision and backing are set before
`fill` runs, so a `fill` that panics leaves an image of its new size
holding stale bytes of that length. `kui_image_add` and
`kui_image_update` compute the byte count with `checked_mul` (and at
most `isize::MAX`) before making the slice.

**What was wrong.** The spare was taken on `strong_count == 1`, and a
Rust host that kept a `Weak` of pixels `image_pixels` gave it made
`Arc::get_mut` refuse the buffer on the next line: the update panicked
with "unshared: checked or replaced above". A caught panic in `fill`
left the entry at its old size with bytes of the new length, which the
emitter or a texture upload reads out of bounds. The C doors made a
slice of `w * h * 4` as `usize` before anything checked it, which
wraps near `u32::MAX` squared on a 64-bit host: undefined behaviour
even though `update_image_with` then refused the size unread.

**Pinned by** `a_weak_on_the_pixels_does_not_break_the_stream` and
`a_panicking_fill_leaves_a_consistent_image` in `tests/images.rs`. The
C door's check has no test: the old slice was never read.

### `.` RG55 — One burst of new glyphs that fits the page doubles it for good — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `GlyphAtlas::begin_frame` reads the page as filling only
once a frame since the last emptying began on a page holding glyphs
and opened rows on it (`turned`). Until then `rows_per_frame` is what
the frames before the emptying opened, which says nothing of how fast
the set after it turns over. F83's thrash still doubles the page, a
frame later than it did: the frame after the emptying has to turn a
row over first.

**What was wrong.** On a 1024 page holding 300 px of rows, one frame
that opened 600 px of new rows (a view of other fonts beside a steady
chrome) left `rows_per_frame` at 600. The next `begin_frame` saw 124 px
free, fewer than two frames' worth, and emptied the page. The frame
after, the decayed figure (450) still said filling, and since the page
had been emptied one frame before, that read as F83's thrash: the page
doubled to 2048 and kept it, 16 MB more a window on the CPU and the
GPU, for a set that fit 1024. Any single burst over ~40% of the page
did it.

**Pinned by** `a_burst_that_fits_does_not_grow_the_page` in
`atlas.rs`'s tests, seen failing before the change (2048 on the third
frame after the burst). `a_page_emptied_again_within_two_frames_grows_with_its_slots`
now turns a row over before it asks for the growth.

### `!` RG56 — A glyph refused on a 4096 page that began the frame empty stays blank until something else empties the page — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** The atlas keeps what it refused for room on a page that
began the frame empty (`refused`, with each one's texels), and until
the page is next emptied it measures every frame: `stamp`, which text
templates and cell tables now key their slots on instead of `epoch`,
moves each frame, so they look their glyphs up again, and each
distinct slot's texels — refused ones too — count once into `demand`.
A frame that began on a page holding something, wanted a refused
glyph, and whose demand fits in `held` (what the page held of the set
that refused) is `short`: it asks for the next frame, which begins on
an empty page. A view showing the whole set measures more than the
page held and keeps it, with nothing emptied or asked for. A frame
that began on an empty page is never read, since it was measured only
from its first refusal. So the reset after one comes once: the fresh
frame that follows it refuses again only if the set did not fit, and
then `held` is what it placed, below the demand.

**What the entry had right and wrong.** The measure it named was
right, but "counted on each hit" was not enough on its own: a text
entry's templates and a cell table keep their slots against the
atlas's epoch and look nothing up on a steady frame, so a frame drawing
cached text looked up nothing and could not be measured. The stamp is
the missing half. Renderers still re-upload on `epoch` alone.

**Pinned by** the entry's repro,
`a_glyph_refused_on_a_fresh_full_page_gets_room_once_its_set_fits`, and
`a_set_bigger_than_the_largest_page_keeps_it_still` in `atlas.rs`, and
end to end through the text cache by
`glyphs_refused_on_a_fresh_full_page_draw_once_the_view_shows_a_part_that_fits`
in `tests/atlas_turnover.rs` (thirty lines of one 1100 px "M" each on
a 4096 page, then the lines past the ones that fit). Seen failing with
`refit` forced false, with the text templates keyed on `epoch` again,
and with the three files reverted.


A set bigger than a `MAX_ATLAS_SIZE` page on its own (a frame of
large CJK or emoji at 2×, or big box-drawing cells: more than 16M
texels of distinct glyphs) fills the page on a frame that began it
empty. `alloc_or_make_room` refuses the glyphs that do not fit without
setting `short`, since the next frame would refuse them too, and
`get_or_insert` (and `get_or_insert_synth`, `get_or_insert_image`)
caches the refusal as `None`. When the app then scrolls to a subset
that holds those glyphs and opens no new row, the lookups hit the
cached `None`, nothing tries to allocate, nothing empties the page,
and the glyphs stay blank for as long as the page lives. Before F99 a
full 4096 page reset mid-frame and the glyph drew after one wrong
frame.

Not a one-line fix. Caching nothing retries (and rasterizes) the glyph
on every lookup, and uncaching the refusals at `begin_frame` makes the
retry on the next, non-fresh frame set `short`, empty the page, refuse
again on the fresh one, and ask for a frame every two frames for as
long as the big set is on screen. What is missing is a measure of how
much of the page the last frame used: the texels of the slots it
looked up, counted on each hit. A page whose last frame used less
than it holds, with refusals pending, can be emptied at `begin_frame`
so they get room; one whose last frame used all of it cannot.

Repro (a unit test in `atlas.rs`): `with_size(MAX_ATLAS_SIZE)`,
`begin_frame`, 17 items of 1000×1000 (the 17th refused, `short`
false), then frames that look up only the 17th: it never gets a slot.
Found reading F99 in the regression pass of 2026-09-26; not seen in
an app.

### `!` RG57 — Plain text and editors are asked at 400, not the family's regular: a family with no 400 face draws in two families — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** Plain `text`, the default attributes of `rich_text` and
every editor (`edit.rs`'s `attrs_for`) are asked at the family's
regular through `Weights::apply`, as spans and cells were. A family
of one Light face draws in that face everywhere.

**What was wrong.** F100 routed spans and cells through the family's
weights and left the other three at `Attrs::new()`, weight 400.
cosmic-text passes over a family with no face at the weight asked, so
a Light-only file, a Bold-only file, or Skia (`OS/2` weight 5) drew
its spans and cells in the family and its plain text and its text
fields in the fallback's family: one `TextStyle`, two families.

**Pinned by** `a_family_with_no_400_face_draws_in_it_everywhere` in
`tests/fonts.rs`, which draws the fixture's advances in a span, cells,
plain text and a `text_edit`; seen failing with `text.rs` reverted
(plain text) and with `edit.rs` alone reverted (the editor).

### `.` RG58 — A copy reads bold as 700 and up: a SemiBold bold copies plain, and a Bold-only family copies everything bold — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** `html_of_run` reads a run as bold when its weight is
heavier than the line's default, which is the family's regular since
RG57, or when it carries `SYNTHETIC_BOLD`.

**What was wrong.** It read `weight >= 700`. After F100 a family of a
Regular and a SemiBold asks bold at 600 with no flag, so its bold
copied as plain text, and a family whose only face is 700 asks regular
at 700, so every run copied as `<b>`. A family whose only face is its
bold still copies its bold plain: nothing on the shaped run tells the
two apart.

**Pinned by** `a_copy_reads_bold_against_the_familys_regular` in
`tests/selection.rs`.

### `.` RG59 — Four readings of F100 the regression pass left: an OS/2 weight over a named instance, the mapped weight handed to fallback faces, shaped text kept across a reweigh, and the nearest face's lean — **done (2026-09-26)**

**Outcome.** All four taken, the second as a limit written down.

1. `WghtAxis::read` pushes the named instances first and the
   `(OS/2 weight, default)` point only when no instance names that
   weight. Pinned by
   `an_instance_at_the_os2_weight_is_not_shadowed_by_the_default`;
   seen failing with the default's point always pushed.
2. **Not fixable at the ask, so named as a limit** (CHANGELOG,
   alpha.22). Clamping Skia's asked weight into 100–900 passes Skia
   itself over: cosmic-text's `default_font_match_key` takes a family's
   face only when `font_weight_diff == 0` or its `wght` axis spans the
   weight asked, and Skia's `OS/2` weight is 5 and its axis 0.48–3.2.
   The rasterizer alone cannot correct the fallback either, since
   `Font::new` builds the fallback face's shaping location at the same
   weight, so a glyph drawn at 400 would sit on hairline advances.
3. `Resources::reweigh` returns whether the weights of a font other
   than the one just registered changed, and the doors bump the
   session's `weights_rev`. Each `Core` checks it before its text store
   begins the frame (`sync_weights`), as a scale change is checked, and
   then drops its shaped text (`TextSystem::forget_shaped`) and cell
   tables (`CellStore::forget_shaped`) and gives each editor's lines the
   new attributes (`EditStore::reweigh`, text, caret and history kept).
   Leaving out the fresh font keeps a new family's registration free:
   kawoosh's fonts pane registers one per row as it scrolls. Pinned by
   `a_face_loaded_or_removed_under_shaped_text_reaches_it` in
   `tests/fonts.rs` (a Light face with no 字, then a Regular with it,
   then a Bold loaded and removed) and
   `a_reweigh_reports_only_a_family_text_may_be_shaped_in` in
   `resources.rs`. Seen failing with each of the three drops left out,
   with the sync skipped, and with the fresh font counted. A removal
   alone did not show it. The removed face's glyphs stay in the atlas
   under their old keys, so text shaped against it still draws, and
   the test needed a face that maps a glyph the other lacks.
4. `css_match` is CSS Fonts 4 § 5.2's order: for 400–500, up to 500
   then down then above 500; below 400, down then up; above 500, up
   then down. Regular with faces at 300 and 500 is asked at 500. Pinned
   by `the_nearest_face_is_the_one_css_matching_takes`.


Read in the review of F100, none seen in an app or with an installed
font on this Mac. Each is small; they are one entry because they
touch the same few functions in `weights.rs` and `text.rs`.

1. **The `OS/2` point shadows a named instance at the same weight**
   (`weights.rs`, where a face's `(OS/2 weight, default)` point is
   pushed before its named instances and the dedupe keeps the first).
   A face whose `OS/2` weight is 400 but whose default instance is
   Thin, with a "Regular" instance at 400, is read as off the CSS
   scale and `coordinate(400)` gives 100: regular draws Thin, where
   before F100 it drew at 400. Push the named instances first and
   the `(OS/2, default)` point only for a weight no instance names;
   Berkeley's result does not change. Test: a `variable_face` over
   [100, 100, 900], `OS/2` 400, instances Thin = 100 and Regular =
   400; `coordinate(400) == 400`.
2. **The mapped weight reaches fallback faces.** Skia, on every Mac,
   is off the scale with an `OS/2` weight of 5, so its regular is
   asked at weight 5. A glyph it lacks falls back at `ideal_weight` 5
   to `.SFNS`, whose axis spans 1–1000, and draws near hairline.
   Skia itself is shaped at its axis's clamp and drawn at 1.0, the
   limit the F100 entry names. Clamp the asked weight to 100–900 for
   a face whose `OS/2` weight is outside it, or name Skia in the
   limit.
3. **A reweigh does not reach shaped text.** `reweigh` runs after
   `load_fonts_dir`, `add_font_data` and `remove_font`, but
   `TextSystem` entries and cell tables are keyed by content, style
   and scale, and `load_fonts_dir` does not bump `fonts_rev`: text
   shaped with `SYNTHETIC_BOLD` stays synthetic after the family's
   real Bold is loaded, until it is evicted. The class predates F100
   (a font loaded under shaped text); a weights epoch in `style_key`,
   or clearing the entries when a reweigh changed anything, closes it.
4. **The nearest face leans light where CSS leans heavy.** For 400
   with faces at 300 and 500, `Weights::of` takes 300; CSS's font
   matching tries 500 first. The comment claims CSS's rule.

**From the kawoosh rounded-selection report (2026-09-27).** One entry,
F101, **built 2026-09-27**, the day it was filed.

### `~` F101 — A selection's backgrounds cannot be one rounded shape: an app draws the outline a frame late, and not at all over a wrapped paragraph — **done (2026-09-27)**

**Outcome.** A span's background takes a radius: `Span::bg_radius(r)`
in Rust, `bgRadius` on a `<span>` in JSX, `bg_radius` in a Lua span
table and on `KuiSpan` in C, in logical px, 0 (the default) the square
background it was. Rounded backgrounds of one colour and radius that
meet are one shape, whichever text drew them. They meet when one's
edge touches the other's exactly on the line above or below and
overlaps it sideways, or when the two meet end to end on one line.
Nothing names the shape
([ADR 0035](../adr/0035-a-rounded-background-is-joined-by-meeting.md)).
A rounded background is emitted square and noted (`text::JoinBg`: its
quad, radius, the clip its text was given, and its text's own box when
that box clips). Once the frame's quads are all emitted, `join::shape`
at the end of `emit_frame` does two things. It makes the pieces that
meet end to end on a line one extent, so no corner is drawn where a
line's text and the cell after it meet. Then it finds each piece's
neighbour above and below, the one overlapping it most, and rewrites
the piece's quad in place into a `fragment` quad of the stock
`fragment::JOIN`, which the core registers as it does the polygon's.
Its params, in physical px from the quad's left, are its own extent,
the neighbours' extents, the radius and which neighbours there are. A
corner is convex where the piece reaches past its neighbour, a fillet
where the neighbour reaches past it, square where the two end together,
and round where nothing meets it. At a join the radius is at most half
the step, and both pieces work it out from the same two ends, so the
halves meet. A piece is no wider than its text's own box shows and is
clipped as the text's parent is, so a fillet past a short no-wrap
line's end shows. Adjacent spans merge into one background only when
their radius matches too (`build_decorations`), and the radius is part
of a run's cache key.

The doors: Node's frame is v17 (a span's fifth slot, the radius; the
encoder refuses a `bgRadius` that is not a number of 0 or more); C's
`KuiSpan.bg_radius` is appended under the unreleased ABI 20, into what
was the struct's tail padding on 64-bit targets, so `abi_parity` holds
it at 40 bytes there; Lua reads `bg_radius` in `collect_spans`. The
schema's text element names `bgRadius` among a span's own props and
says what it does; props.md and jsx-runtime.d.ts regenerated, `SpanProps`
typed by hand. Tests: `tests/joined_backgrounds.rs` in kui-core (a
selection across three texts is three pieces told their neighbours and
meeting on one pixel line; only one colour and radius, and no gap,
joins; a wrapped text's lines join inside the text; two texts meeting
end to end on a line are one extent); `tests/joined_backgrounds.rs` in
kui-wgpu composites the pieces through `tests/wgsl::join_alpha`,
`JOIN`'s mirror; `the_stock_join_validates` in `fragment.rs`; the ffi's
`a_span_s_bg_radius_joins_its_background_across_the_boundary`; a Node
test for the pieces, the inherited radius, the square default and the
refusal. The corpus gains `joined-backgrounds` in four adapters: four
texts of mono spans, three in one translucent colour joined, the fourth
in another beside them. Lua was seen going red with the radius dropped.

**Found.** By kawoosh (2026-09-27), drawing its selection rounded. The
selection is a translucent `bg` on each selected span of each row,
seamless since dfc635b. Its first rounded cut was a `fragment` under
each row, told its own extent and its neighbours' from the last frame's
layout. a6092cc made a stack of those meet on pixel lines. That cut was
a frame behind on every edit, scroll and wrap. It could not follow a
markdown paragraph either: one text whose lines are wherever layout
breaks them, which the app would have had to measure again. The
corners depend on the lines around a line, in this text or the next,
so only a pass that sees every text's lines after layout can draw them
in the frame they belong to.

**From the kawoosh ⌘-Tab report (2026-09-27).** One entry, F102,
**built 2026-09-27**, the day it was filed.

### `!` F102 — A window brought back shows its last frame: a frame the surface skipped is never asked for again, and being uncovered asks for none — **done (2026-09-27)**

**Outcome.** A skipped frame is owed until one lands (`retry::Retry` in
kui-native, which took over the first frame's `first_frame` and its two
constants). The `Skip` arm of `Shell::redraw` marks it (`skipped`), and
the end of the loop asks for the next frame a retry apart — 16 ms, 60
times, the first frame's pacing — and wakes for it (`poll`). A present
clears it (`presented`); the tries, once spent, are not started over by
the skips after them, so a surface that stays unpresentable is not spun
on. `WindowEvent::Occluded`, which kui had not handled, now reaches it
(`occluded`): covered, a skip owes nothing; uncovered, a frame is owed
and asked for at once, with the tries fresh. The smoke count
(`KUI_SMOKE_FRAMES`) reads `presented_once`, as it read `first_frame`
being gone. Pinned by the four tests in `retry.rs`: the first frame
asked for until it lands, a later skip asked for again (the case this
is about), the bound, and covered against uncovered. The runner itself
has no headless test — a winit loop is built on the main thread only —
so the probe below is the evidence it works in a window.

**The finding.** kawoosh reported its window coming up late after ⌘-Tab
on macOS and Alt-Tab on Windows. Probed on macOS 27 with the `counter`
example, a trace in the runner, and AppleScript hiding the process
(`visible` false) and bringing it back (`frontmost` true). On the
commit before: two frames skipped as occluded after the window came
back, then nothing — no frame until the process was killed 2 s later.
With F102: the same two skips, `Occluded(false)` 35 ms after the focus,
and the frame presented within a millisecond of it. The first-frame
retry (a window ordered front calls itself occluded for a beat) had
been written for exactly this beat, but only until a window's first
present. Windows reports no occlusion to winit, so there the retry is
what covers a skipped frame; the Alt-Tab half was not probed.

**From the kawoosh trackpad-drift report (2026-09-28).** One entry,
F104, **built 2026-09-28**, the day it was filed.

### `~` F104 — A trackpad swipe moves both axes: a swipe down nudges whatever scrolls sideways under it — **done (2026-09-28)**

**Outcome.** A trackpad swipe keeps to one axis (`axis_lock::AxisLock`
in kui-native, one a window, fed by the `MouseWheel` arm). Until it has
travelled 4 px, both axes summed, each event keeps its own larger
component, so the first pixels are not lost; then the larger of the two
sums is the swipe's axis and the other is dropped. The lock lasts as
long as events come less than 200 ms apart, so a swipe's momentum —
which AppKit reports as a second began–ended run straight after the
first — keeps the axis its hand set. Sticky, not fixed: each axis's
recent travel is kept, decayed by a quarter an event, and when the
other axis's is three times the locked one's and at least 24 px the
lock turns — a hand that turns without lifting is followed about seven
events (~60 ms) in. Only `PixelDelta` is locked; a `LineDelta` notch
passes whole and ends any swipe, since a wheel moves one axis a notch
already and Shift turns it sideways on purpose. An event whose kept
delta is zero is not dispatched. winit's `TouchPhase` is not read: it
names a momentum run's start as it names a finger's, so the gap is what
tells a new swipe from the glide of the last. Pinned by the six tests in
`axis_lock.rs`: a vertical swipe with drift and its momentum, a
sideways one, the undecided first pixels, a turn, a pause and a notch.
The runner has no headless test (a winit loop is built on the main
thread only), and the thresholds are a first reading, to be tuned by
hand on a trackpad.

**Not built.** An opt-out for a surface that pans on both axes at once
— a map, a canvas — which macOS gives a scroll view as
`usesPredominantAxisScrolling`. Nothing in kui or its apps pans that
way yet; when one does, it is a flag on the scroll region the core
reads, and the lock moves from the runner to the dispatch that knows
the region.

**The finding.** kawoosh's user, on a trackpad: a swipe meant straight
down shifted the view a little sideways. Two places took the drift. A
`scrollY` list passes the x it cannot scroll to the `scrollX` strip
around it (DX13), so the column ribbon moved under every list; and the
editor's text column is an `onScroll` node that takes the whole delta
and moves its own sideways offset by `dx` wherever a line is wider than
the pane. The runner handed `PixelDelta` through as it came, both axes.
